Recon Build Spec

Version 0 · epic #466 · 2026-10-26 · rendered from specs/recon/ at 6b33d5d. An agent reads the rules, the root, its one task file and the code merged on main, and nothing else.

The models, the agents, thinking effort and the operator, measured. One crawler crawls every line the kernel captured, every repository those lines lead to and every host those repositories live on, in depth, into one structure, and the module reports on it in its own tab, on its own port. Two parts of the module read, each for its own task. The crawler gathers the history: it finds the details that are not readily apparent, ties what the agents did to what became of their work, and delivers the critical data no one knew to look for. The watcher watches every session as it works, the coding agents' adversary: it reads the operator's orders and the spec a session executes, judges every action of the agent against them, and reports a problem as it happens, so the operator learns of an agent that vanished, improvised or ignored them before the time is lost.

The spec pack

Every box is a file of the repository; open one to read it as the repository holds it. The arrows are how the files name each other. A build starts at its task file, reads the root, then the rules, then its task file whole.

The sequence
Five tasks: the structure first, whole, before anything fills it; then the crawler that fills it, the lines in one task and the repositories with their hosts in the next; then the tab, designed with the operator once the crawler is merged and built piece by piece (decision the-tab); then the watcher, the module's second part (decision watcher). Each task but the tab is one build an agent does from its file; the operator deploys and runs what it builds. A task starts when the task before it is merged on main. Its file under tasks/ carries, verbatim, what the structure and the authority files say about its piece, what it builds, its gates and what it owns; this list carries the piece, the files it reflects and what it builds or removes.
#TaskNameThe pieceBuildsAfter
1#536structureThe module becomes superx-mod-recon, and its structure is built whole before anything fills it: the crate, from superx-mod-performance, its code moved and renamed and its crawler doing what it does today; the executable superx-recon; the database superx/recon, from the structure, crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, which the crate applies byte for byte and never writes: the tables a measure uses or the crawler writes, every source kept whole beside them: the lines in the kernel, git in the repositories and the mirrors, every answer of every read API in host_object; the parameters attr_recon_*; the module folder with its repos folder and its credentials file; the status, listing every table and which hosts have credentials. superx-mod-performance and superx-mod-repos leave the workspace, and the core UI links to no module's own UI (#534).crates/superx-mod-recon/
2#537linesThe crawler crawls the captured lines in depth. Every line the kernel holds, of every agent and every kind, is read once into the tables it feeds, and its pass counts the lines that fed none; every field seen on a kind of line is a line_field row that says whether a column holds it; every pass checks the lines it read against the lines the kernel holds in the same range, and a difference makes the pass partial and is named. It fills the lines' tables whole: sessions and their places, prompts, triggers, replies, their states, thinking, tool calls and their results, edits, git events, tests and builds, turns, compactions and recaps.crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/t01
3#538repositoriesThe crawler crawls the repositories and their hosts. From the working directories the lines name, and the roots the operator names, every checkout and repository; one worker per repository, as many at once as attr_recon_repos_at_once allows. For each repository its mirror clone in the repos folder, fetched with the credentials, which keeps every fact git holds, and the facts the structure's tables hold: commits with their files, their time zones, patch ids and signatures; every reflog entry and the operations a checkout is in the middle of; where each commit sits and what landed through another commit, what each commit still holds at the main line's tip, remotes, and every git call. From the hosts, with the credentials and GET only, every answer whole in host_object, and beside it the pull requests, their commits and their CI runs. Every commit tied to the session that made it.crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/repos/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/hosts/t02
4#539tabThe tab: what the module shows on its own port, from what t02 and t03 filled. Designed with the operator once t03 is merged, and built piece by piece, each piece its own PR, without a task file (decision the-tab).crates/superx-mod-recon/t03
5#566watcherThe watcher: the module's second part, beside the crawler and sharing nothing that runs with it. Its own loop follows the newest captured lines of every session, holds one watch per session in memory, judges each line against the operator's orders and against the task file the session executes, finds each session's memory folder, and reports what it finds on a Watcher tab of the module's page, as it happens, with a notification in the browser. It writes nothing.crates/superx-mod-recon/src/watcher/ crates/superx-mod-recon/ui/src/watcher/ crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/watcher.rst04
The decisions
What was decided, when, and where it lives; what is still open. A confirmed decision is written into the file or element named, which is the authority; it is listed here so an agent knows a question is closed. An open decision is a question an agent never answers: a task that needs it stops. An open decision names in closes-before the task before whose file it is closed, and check.py refuses that file while the decision is open.
DecisionStatusWhenWhatWhere it lives
reconconfirmed2026-10-26The module is superx-mod-recon: the executable superx-recon, the database superx/recon, the parameters attr_recon_*, the module folder <home>/modules/recon/. It replaces superx-mod-performance, whose name the operator rejected.identity; sequence t01
one-module-one-crawlerconfirmed2026-10-25One module, one schema, one crawler: the lines and the repositories are crawled by one crawler into one database, through its sources, and a new source plugs in beside them.BLUEPRINT.md; rules one-module-its-own-process
own-processconfirmed2026-10-24Its own executable, process, database, port and tab, never linked into superx; it reads the kernel's data and the kernel never depends on it.rules one-module-its-own-process
crawler-is-the-readerconfirmed2026-10-26The crawler is the only thing that reads; nothing beside it, and no part of it, is called a reader. Since 2026-11-04 the watcher reads beside it, for its own task (decision watcher); nothing in the module is called a reader still.rules crawler-is-the-reader
watcherconfirmed2026-11-04Recon has two parts, the crawler that gathers the history and the watcher, sharing nothing that runs: they do very different tasks. The watcher watches every session like a hawk, especially the operator's instructions, which usually change the direction or correct the agent; it watches what the agent does, whether it follows the instructions or improvises, judges it against the operator's command and against the spec it executes, and reports problems as they happen: the coding agents' adversary. Its loop is the operator's parameter and it scales to many sessions at once. The operator approved its design on the page of 2026-11-04 and ordered it built from this spec.identity; rules crawler-is-the-reader, kernel-read-only, capture-first; sequence t05
crawl-not-skimconfirmed2026-10-25Slow, diligent, methodical: every line, field, commit and ref accounted for and checked against its source; the details that are not readily apparent found and tied together.rules crawl-not-skim
repos-from-the-logsconfirmed2026-10-25The repositories come from the session logs; the logs report a change first and the crawler follows them.rules repos-from-the-logs
hosts-and-mirrorsconfirmed2026-10-26The credentials serve both: the hosts' APIs, read-only, and the crawler's own mirror clones, fetched with them. The structure says so: git never fetches into the operator's checkouts, and the mirrors are fetched and nothing else is.rules hosts-read-only-with-credentials, never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t01, t03
repos-folderconfirmed2026-10-26The mirror clones live in a folder of their own, under the module's folder, which nothing else writes.identity data
mirrors-take-everythingconfirmed2026-10-26The crawler fetches into its own mirrors freely: one mirror per remote, a fetch takes every ref the remote advertises, and nothing a mirror has fetched is ever pruned or collected, so a branch rewritten or deleted on the host keeps what the crawler saw. The more it holds, the more it can find.rules never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t03
parallel-by-repositoryconfirmed2026-10-26The crawler scales by repository: one worker per repository, as many at once as a parameter allows.rules parallel-by-repository; sequence t03
structure-firstconfirmed2026-10-26The structure first, whole; then the crawler that fills it; then the tab, designed after.sequence
four-prsconfirmed2026-10-26Four PRs at most; fewer PRs, more work in each. The structure, the lines and the repositories took one each; the tab took three, built piece by piece on the operator's word of 2026-11-03 (decision the-tab); the watcher, decided on 2026-11-04, is one task more (decision watcher).sequence; decisions the-tab, watcher
specs-are-the-truthconfirmed2026-10-26The XML files are the source of truth, in the data lake's pack: a root, the rules, one file per task, and the tools that check and render them; a branch and a PR are built from a task file.rules specs-are-the-truth; identity specs
deep-structureconfirmed2026-10-25Every field the agents write and everything git holds has a place: a column, a table, or its source, kept whole, which the structure names; line_field lists every field the agents write and whether a column holds it.crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql
old-dataconfirmed2026-10-25The kernel's lines captured before a capture fix are read as they are: nothing is recaptured or repaired.rules derived-and-rebuilt
hosts-take-everythingconfirmed2026-10-26From the hosts, everything their read APIs return for each repository the crawler crawls: every answer kept whole in host_object, pull requests, their commits, reviews, comments, checks and pipelines, merges, issues, releases and the rest the answers hold, beside typed tables for what is measured, and every call recorded. Less data is more assumption; the operator has intuition already, and the crawler exists for the facts the operator does not know. The tables themselves are designed into the structure from the hosts' own API documentation, and the operator approves the structure (§7, §11).sequence t01, t03; rules crawl-not-skim
credentials-fileconfirmed2026-10-26<home>/modules/recon/credentials.json, in JSON as the instance's own files are, written by the operator: one entry per host, each a token with every read scope the host offers and no write scope; the crawler refuses the file when anyone but its owner can read it. A mirror's fetch gets its credential from superx-recon itself, answering git as its askpass program, so a token is never written into git's configuration, a URL, a command line or the environment.authority credentials; rules hosts-read-only-with-credentials
live-gatesconfirmed2026-10-26A task's live gates run before its merge, on the operator's instance, once the operator has deployed the branch's build of superx-recon; superx and its daemon are never touched, and every row the build writes is derived, so the next deploy rebuilds what it needs.method gates, deploy
retire-performanceconfirmed2026-10-26At t01's deploy superx-performance is stopped and superx-recon started in its place; once recon's first pass is complete, the operator drops superx/performance and superx/repos under root. Every row they hold is derived, and recon derives it again from the same lines and repositories.sequence t01
repos-at-onceconfirmed2026-10-26attr_recon_repos_at_once falls back to no limit: every repository a pass crawls gets its own worker at once, unless the operator sets a number. A limit's fallback is no limit.rules parallel-by-repository, nothing-dropped
structureconfirmed2026-10-29The structure: the tables a measure uses or the crawler writes, cut on 2026-10-29 from the structure designed on 2026-10-26 (the deep crawl's tables, the hosts' and the mirrors', git's facts), every source kept whole beside them, and a table joins when a measure needs it; checked on an empty SurrealDB 3.1.4. Approved by the operator on 2026-10-26, and cut on the operator's word on 2026-10-29: "yes lets slim up", "yes" (§7, §11). It lives where CI guards a module's schema, so every change to it carries the operator's approval marker.crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql
git-factsconfirmed2026-10-26Git's own documentation read whole (git 2.50.1), and what git holds that the structure lacked added to it as far as a measure uses it: where each commit was made in local time, the commits that make the same change, signatures, the operations a checkout is in the middle of and line counts blind to whitespace; files copied, how each merge's conflicts were resolved, upstreams, annotated tags and trailers stay in git (decision structure). Approved by the operator on 2026-10-26: "1 -9 yes change the schema for more data".crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql
git-cliconfirmed2026-10-26The crawler reads git through git itself, one process per call, never a library: every call is a git_call row, and git's answers are git's own. The operator, 2026-10-26: "Recommendation: keep the CLI. yes".rules never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t03
http-clientconfirmed2026-10-26The hosts' APIs are called through reqwest, with rustls, the one HTTP client for both hosts: no host's own client library, since every answer is kept whole with its headers, and no maintained Bitbucket Cloud client exists. Its redirects drop the credential when they leave the host. Recommended with the git CLI and answered with the same yes.sequence t03
devlake-ideasconfirmed2026-11-01Four more facts from Apache DevLake's design, proposed on the structure page on 2026-10-26: labels and milestones, issue events, the e-mails an account commits as, the commits each deployment shipped. No table joins for them: t03 crawls each and keeps it whole in host_object, the labels and milestones in each issue and pull request, the issue events as issue_event and timeline_event objects, the e-mail beside the account in each pull request's commits, each deployment's commit in each deployment; git holds every e-mail a commit was made with. A table joins when a measure needs one (decision structure). The operator, 2026-11-01: "1", kept whole, no table.sequence t03; crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql
the-tabconfirmed2026-11-03What the tab shows and how, designed with the operator from what t02 and t03 filled, on the tab's design page. The operator ruled it built piece by piece instead of from a task file, each piece its own PR, deployed and shown before the next: the crawler's stats and the parameters (#558), now and the repositories (#559), the results (#560).sequence t04

The operator, 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-25: its own executable, schema and URL; one module, one port, one schemaRecon is one module: its own executable, superx-recon; its own process, with its pid file and its logs in its module folder; its own database, superx/recon, applied by superx-recon --initialize; its own port and tab; its own parameters on its registry entity. It depends on superx-kernel alone, and no crate of the workspace depends on it. The kernel never waits on it: a recon that is stopped, slow or broken leaves capture untouched.a test of t01 that reads the workspace's dependency graph (no crate depends on superx-mod-recon, and it depends on no module); review
kernel-read-onlyBLUEPRINT.md: "The kernel has zero knowledge of any module's domain."; "SELECT + CREATE only, no verb ever issues UPDATE / DELETE / DDL." The operator: the kernel is off limits to a module's workThe crawler and the watcher read the kernel's database with SELECT and nothing else, a bounded page at a time, in the order of its rows' ids, as the service account the instance names; neither ever writes it or reads it through another client. No task changes superx-kernel, superx or superx-ops; what the module needs from the kernel it reads as the kernel writes it.review of every query the module issues; tools/skill_audit.py (§10)
append-onlySUPERX_SCHEMA.md: "No kernel verb ever emits UPDATE, DELETE, UPSERT, DEFINE, REMOVE, or ALTER." crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql: "Nothing is overwritten and nothing is removed (§10)."The module's own database takes CREATE only: a fact is a row for good, what changes about it appends a row, and the newest valid_from is the fact. The crawler never issues UPDATE, UPSERT, DELETE, REMOVE, DEFINE or ALTER; row ids are UUIDv7, set by the module's code. The structure is applied by superx-recon --initialize alone, by the operator, under root.tools/skill_audit.py (§10); review
the-structure-decidescrates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql: "Nothing here is judged." The operator, 2026-10-26: first build the structure, then the crawler that fills itThe structure, the module's one schema file, says every table, field, link, index and vocabulary the crawler writes, and a test reads the schema back out of the engine and diffs it against the file. Nothing the crawler writes is outside it. A task changes it only as its file says; any other change is designed with the operator and approved per change (§7, §11), and a task that needs one stops.the schema test of the module; CI's Operator-approved marker on a schema file; review
crawler-is-the-readerThe operator, 2026-10-26: the crawler is the reader; there are no readers beside it. The operator, 2026-11-04: two things, the crawler that gathers the historical info and the watcher, all parts of mod-recon; they do very different tasksTwo parts of the module read, each for its own task, and share nothing that runs: the crawler reads everything the module holds into its structure, for history: the lines, the repositories, the hosts; the watcher reads the newest captured lines, the task files the sessions build from and the agents' memory folders, read-only, to watch every session as it works, and writes nothing. Nothing else in the module reads a source. A source is a part of the crawler, named for what it crawls, never a component beside it; nothing in the module, its code, its tests, its specs or its PRs is called a reader.specs/recon/check.py (the word in a spec); review
crawl-not-skimThe operator, 2026-10-25: "crawler means slow diligent methodical"; "we crawl not skim". 2026-10-26: the crawler finds the details that are not readily apparent, and is counted on to deliver critical data no one was aware ofThe crawler crawls. Every field a source carries gets a column or a table, or a line_field row that says no column holds it. Every line read is counted in its pass, and so is every line that fed no table. Every repository is read whole: all of its history, every ref, every reflog, every blame. Every pass checks what it read against what its source holds, the kernel's lines in the range read, git's own counts, a host's totals, and records any difference; a pass that finds one is partial, the difference in its note. Speed is no goal; pacing is, and capture comes first.the tests of t02 and t03; their live gates (passes complete, the lines read equal to the lines held); review
nothing-droppedcrates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs: "The whole line, whatever its length: nothing is dropped (#471)." The operator, 2026-10-24: design to the requirement, never to the diskNo sampling, no cap, no truncation, no summary: a limit's fallback is no limit; a value is kept as the source wrote it, in the column the structure gives it; a line, a field or a value the crawler cannot place is counted and named, never dropped and never guessed at.the tests of every task; review
derived-and-rebuiltcrates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql: "EVERY ROW IS DERIVED, from the kernel's captured lines and from git,". The operator, 2026-10-25: the lines captured before today are read as they are, never fixedEvery row is derived from the kernel's lines, git and the hosts, and a provision rebuilds the whole database from them; the one exception is what git or a host has since expired or pruned and the crawler had kept, which the structure names. A line captured before a capture fix is read as it is: the crawler never recaptures, repairs or fills in a kernel line.review; the live gates after a provision
repos-from-the-logsThe operator, 2026-10-25: the crawler builds its list from the logs, which are the first to report a change. 2026-10-26: the crawler discovers the repositories from the session logs, then goes from the sessions into themThe repositories come from the logs: every working directory a session's lines name is followed to the checkout and the repository that hold it, and so is every root the operator names in attr_recon_include; a commit or a push a session printed sends the crawler to its repository in the same pass. A repository no session and no root leads to is never crawled, and one the operator excludes in attr_recon_exclude never is. A checkout whose folder is gone is recorded gone and keeps its history.the tests of t03; review
never-write-a-measured-repositoryThe operator, 2026-10-13 (#446): the product never writes to a repository it measures, or runs code in one. 2026-10-26: a dedicated folder for the repositories, under the module, into which the crawler fetches freely: the more it holds, the betterThe operator's repositories are read with git, read-only: never a write, a fetch, a checkout, a lock, or a program a repository names (no diff driver, no external diff, no signature check, no fsmonitor, optional locks off). Fetching happens only into the crawler's own mirror clones, in its repos folder, which nothing else writes: a fetch takes every ref the remote advertises, nothing a mirror has fetched is ever pruned or collected, and a mirror's hooks never run.the tests of the module's git calls, which read every call's arguments and environment; review
hosts-read-only-with-credentialsThe operator, 2026-10-25: the crawler gets a credentials file for the hosts. 2026-10-26: the credentials serve the hosts and the mirror clones bothThe hosts are read with GET only, with the operator's read-only credentials from the credentials file. The file is mode 600 and read by the crawler alone, and the crawler refuses a file others can read. A credential is never stored, logged, printed, shown on the tab, written into a URL, a command line, the environment, git's configuration or a mirror, and never sent to a host other than the one it is for; git asks superx-recon for it when it fetches.the tests of t01 and t03; specs/recon/check.py (no credential written in a spec); review
parallel-by-repositoryThe operator, 2026-10-26: the crawler scales by crawling more repositories, more of them at once, the number set by a parameterThe crawler crawls one repository per worker, as many at once as attr_recon_repos_at_once allows, each worker making its own git and host calls; within a repository its steps run in their order, and a repository's facts are written by its own worker alone. The lines are crawled as one stream, in the order the kernel captured them. The pass waits for every worker before it records itself.the tests of t03; review
