The operator, 2026-10-25: capturing correct, whole data is the kernel's purposeNothing the crawler or the watcher does slows or stops capture: each reads the kernel's database a bounded page at a time, holds nothing open between pages, and reads nothing while its interval is 0 (attr_recon_read_secs for the crawler, attr_recon_watch_secs for the watcher); a pass or a look that cannot read waits for the next.the live gates, read beside capture's own ticks; review
no-hardcoded-policy.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Facts go in columns; judgments do not."Every tunable is a parameter attr_recon_*, with its fallback in the module's params.rs, reported by name when it is set to a value of the wrong shape; a constant that is no policy carries an audited skill-allow marker. Judgments, such as which work the operator directed, what is rework and how work is split into sittings, are parameters applied when a chart is drawn, never columns and never constants.tools/skill_audit.py (§9); review
types-flow.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "A `record<table>` is a `Thing` everywhere."Types flow end to end: a link is a record id, a kernel line's id a Uuid, a time a DateTime<Utc>, from the line to the column; nothing is cast to text to pass the engine, and a refusal of the engine is fixed in the code, never by loosening the structure (§12, §14).tools/skill_audit.py (§14); the structure's own asserts; review
gates-are-the-truth.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Code does not exist until `cargo test --workspace` passes."A task is done when cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings and python3 tools/skill_audit.py pass and every gate of its file is green; nothing is called done, ready or complete before, and a gate that cannot fail is no gate.CI's required checks; the PR's gate table
trunk.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Every change ties to a GitHub issue"Each task is one issue, one branch cut from the head of main and one PR into main, merged by the operator; one open branch and one open PR at a time; a merged branch is deleted.review
specs-are-the-truthThe operator, 2026-10-26: "xmls are the source of truth"The XML files under specs/recon/ are the source: the issues and the Build Spec page are rendered from them (build_issues.py, build_page.py) and nothing flows back but an issue's number. A spec file is committed only when specs/recon/check.py passes on it, and the check's mutation test proves the check catches what it guards.specs/recon/check.py; specs/recon/mutate_check.py
one-agentThe operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is, the operator's ruling of 2026-10-03 for every build from a specOne session, one agent, sequential: the agent that holds a task does every step itself: reading, writing, testing, running the gates, writing the PR. It never spawns, delegates to, forks or messages another agent, subagent, worker, team or background session, and never asks another model to build or verify a part. A PR whose history shows another agent's hand is closed. This rule is a constraint of every task.review of the PR's history
one-build-per-taskThe operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is, the operator's rulings of 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-05 for every build from a specEach version of a task file is built once: one agent, one branch, one PR; the operator reviews and merges before the next task starts. The next task's agent reads what the root's method read lists and nothing of an unmerged branch. The model is the operator's choice per task and the label records it. Only when the task file's comparison attribute is yes is it built by several agents, one per model, each from the same file version on its own branch; none is merged until the operator chooses one.review
agent-decides-only-what-is-listedThe operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is; the operator, 2026-10-08: "what is left to the agent's determination? not much: we are building the defined product"Every task file lists under agent-decides the only things its agent decides: the names and the decomposition inside its own modules and tests, the means of a step within the standard library and the workspace's dependencies, the contents of its fixtures within the cases listed, its commit granularity. Everything else a build needs is written in the task file, the root, the rules, the authority files or the merged code; a build that decides anything else, or decides it differently from the file, is closed, and the step it departed from is named in the review. Every gate names the steps it proves, so a failed gate points at the step to look at.review of the PR against the task file's agent-decides list and its steps; the gates, each naming the steps it proves
kickoffThe operator, 2026-10-26: a branch and a PR are built from the task's XML file; the operator may name the session that builds a task, the spec's own includedA build starts with a model pointed at one task file in the repository, specs/recon/tasks/<issue>.xml. Nothing else is said at kickoff. The kickoff line is the first message of a session opened for that build, in a new session, on a checkout of the head of main updated from origin: not the session that wrote the spec, not a session that built or reviewed another build, not one that holds a conversation. There, before the branch is cut, python3 tools/skill_audit.py run on the head of main names what CI's Bright-line skill rules check names on that head, no more and no fewer: it reads no file on a path through a folder named worktrees, and every file elsewhere in the folder, whether git holds it or not; a folder where it names fewer or more is not a build folder, and the build stops there. A model whose session holds anything before the kickoff line beyond what the harness gives every session (its own text, the repository's CLAUDE.md, its settings, a command that set the model or the effort, with its output) stops at that line, says so, and builds nothing. The model writes its own label, <model>-<effort>, as the root's method model gives it. The operator may instead name a session to build a task, the spec's own included: that session builds when the operator says so, from the task file as written, reads the files in the order the root's method read gives from that word on, and its PR says it was the session the operator named. The file's start element names the branch, the PR title and the first word of every commit; a build started any other way, or on a branch or PR named any other way, does not count.review of the branch name and the PR title against the task file's start element; the PR body stating that the kickoff line was the session's first message, and on which checkout
every-rule-bindsThese rulesEvery rule here binds every task. Every gate of a task names in its proves attribute what it proves; a rule that no gate of a task names binds the task all the same.specs/recon/check.py
specs/recon/rules.xml, the XML as written
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<!--
  SuperX Recon: the build rules. Version 0.

  One file, no generator. Every rule has three parts:
    from    - where the rule comes from: a sentence of an authority file (quoted, checked verbatim by
              specs/recon/check.py), the merged code, or the operator's ruling with its date
    text    - the rule, as it binds every task and every agent
    breach  - what catches a breach, each a guard that exists or that a task of the sequence builds:
              specs/recon/check.py over the specs, the lints (clippy, tools/skill_audit.py) over the code,
              a test, a gate of a task, or the operator's review
  Nothing here writes a count of the captured lines, the sessions, the repositories or the hosts.
-->
<rules product="SuperX Recon" epic="466" version="0" date="2026-10-26">

  <rule id="one-module-its-own-process">
    <from>BLUEPRINT.md: "It is never linked into `superx`, so nothing it does can reach capture."; "own db `superx/&lt;name&gt;` + service account". The operator, 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-25: its own executable, schema and URL; one module, one port, one schema</from>
    <text>Recon is one module: its own executable, superx-recon; its own process, with its pid file and its logs in its module folder; its own database, superx/recon, applied by superx-recon --initialize; its own port and tab; its own parameters on its registry entity. It depends on superx-kernel alone, and no crate of the workspace depends on it. The kernel never waits on it: a recon that is stopped, slow or broken leaves capture untouched.</text>
    <breach>a test of t01 that reads the workspace's dependency graph (no crate depends on superx-mod-recon, and it depends on no module); review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="kernel-read-only">
    <from>BLUEPRINT.md: "The kernel has zero knowledge of any module's domain."; "SELECT + CREATE only, no verb ever issues UPDATE / DELETE / DDL." The operator: the kernel is off limits to a module's work</from>
    <text>The crawler and the watcher read the kernel's database with SELECT and nothing else, a bounded page at a time, in the order of its rows' ids, as the service account the instance names; neither ever writes it or reads it through another client. No task changes superx-kernel, superx or superx-ops; what the module needs from the kernel it reads as the kernel writes it.</text>
    <breach>review of every query the module issues; tools/skill_audit.py (§10)</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="append-only">
    <from>SUPERX_SCHEMA.md: "No kernel verb ever emits UPDATE, DELETE, UPSERT, DEFINE, REMOVE, or ALTER." crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql: "Nothing is overwritten and nothing is removed (§10)."</from>
    <text>The module's own database takes CREATE only: a fact is a row for good, what changes about it appends a row, and the newest valid_from is the fact. The crawler never issues UPDATE, UPSERT, DELETE, REMOVE, DEFINE or ALTER; row ids are UUIDv7, set by the module's code. The structure is applied by superx-recon --initialize alone, by the operator, under root.</text>
    <breach>tools/skill_audit.py (§10); review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="the-structure-decides">
    <from>crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql: "Nothing here is judged." The operator, 2026-10-26: first build the structure, then the crawler that fills it</from>
    <text>The structure, the module's one schema file, says every table, field, link, index and vocabulary the crawler writes, and a test reads the schema back out of the engine and diffs it against the file. Nothing the crawler writes is outside it. A task changes it only as its file says; any other change is designed with the operator and approved per change (§7, §11), and a task that needs one stops.</text>
    <breach>the schema test of the module; CI's Operator-approved marker on a schema file; review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="crawler-is-the-reader">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: the crawler is the reader; there are no readers beside it. The operator, 2026-11-04: two things, the crawler that gathers the historical info and the watcher, all parts of mod-recon; they do very different tasks</from>
    <text>Two parts of the module read, each for its own task, and share nothing that runs: the crawler reads everything the module holds into its structure, for history: the lines, the repositories, the hosts; the watcher reads the newest captured lines, the task files the sessions build from and the agents' memory folders, read-only, to watch every session as it works, and writes nothing. Nothing else in the module reads a source. A source is a part of the crawler, named for what it crawls, never a component beside it; nothing in the module, its code, its tests, its specs or its PRs is called a reader.</text>
    <breach>specs/recon/check.py (the word in a spec); review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="crawl-not-skim">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-25: "crawler means slow diligent methodical"; "we crawl not skim". 2026-10-26: the crawler finds the details that are not readily apparent, and is counted on to deliver critical data no one was aware of</from>
    <text>The crawler crawls. Every field a source carries gets a column or a table, or a line_field row that says no column holds it. Every line read is counted in its pass, and so is every line that fed no table. Every repository is read whole: all of its history, every ref, every reflog, every blame. Every pass checks what it read against what its source holds, the kernel's lines in the range read, git's own counts, a host's totals, and records any difference; a pass that finds one is partial, the difference in its note. Speed is no goal; pacing is, and capture comes first.</text>
    <breach>the tests of t02 and t03; their live gates (passes complete, the lines read equal to the lines held); review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="nothing-dropped">
    <from>crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs: "The whole line, whatever its length: nothing is dropped (#471)." The operator, 2026-10-24: design to the requirement, never to the disk</from>
    <text>No sampling, no cap, no truncation, no summary: a limit's fallback is no limit; a value is kept as the source wrote it, in the column the structure gives it; a line, a field or a value the crawler cannot place is counted and named, never dropped and never guessed at.</text>
    <breach>the tests of every task; review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="derived-and-rebuilt">
    <from>crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql: "EVERY ROW IS DERIVED, from the kernel's captured lines and from git,". The operator, 2026-10-25: the lines captured before today are read as they are, never fixed</from>
    <text>Every row is derived from the kernel's lines, git and the hosts, and a provision rebuilds the whole database from them; the one exception is what git or a host has since expired or pruned and the crawler had kept, which the structure names. A line captured before a capture fix is read as it is: the crawler never recaptures, repairs or fills in a kernel line.</text>
    <breach>review; the live gates after a provision</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="repos-from-the-logs">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-25: the crawler builds its list from the logs, which are the first to report a change. 2026-10-26: the crawler discovers the repositories from the session logs, then goes from the sessions into them</from>
    <text>The repositories come from the logs: every working directory a session's lines name is followed to the checkout and the repository that hold it, and so is every root the operator names in attr_recon_include; a commit or a push a session printed sends the crawler to its repository in the same pass. A repository no session and no root leads to is never crawled, and one the operator excludes in attr_recon_exclude never is. A checkout whose folder is gone is recorded gone and keeps its history.</text>
    <breach>the tests of t03; review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="never-write-a-measured-repository">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-13 (#446): the product never writes to a repository it measures, or runs code in one. 2026-10-26: a dedicated folder for the repositories, under the module, into which the crawler fetches freely: the more it holds, the better</from>
    <text>The operator's repositories are read with git, read-only: never a write, a fetch, a checkout, a lock, or a program a repository names (no diff driver, no external diff, no signature check, no fsmonitor, optional locks off). Fetching happens only into the crawler's own mirror clones, in its repos folder, which nothing else writes: a fetch takes every ref the remote advertises, nothing a mirror has fetched is ever pruned or collected, and a mirror's hooks never run.</text>
    <breach>the tests of the module's git calls, which read every call's arguments and environment; review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="hosts-read-only-with-credentials">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-25: the crawler gets a credentials file for the hosts. 2026-10-26: the credentials serve the hosts and the mirror clones both</from>
    <text>The hosts are read with GET only, with the operator's read-only credentials from the credentials file. The file is mode 600 and read by the crawler alone, and the crawler refuses a file others can read. A credential is never stored, logged, printed, shown on the tab, written into a URL, a command line, the environment, git's configuration or a mirror, and never sent to a host other than the one it is for; git asks superx-recon for it when it fetches.</text>
    <breach>the tests of t01 and t03; specs/recon/check.py (no credential written in a spec); review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="parallel-by-repository">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: the crawler scales by crawling more repositories, more of them at once, the number set by a parameter</from>
    <text>The crawler crawls one repository per worker, as many at once as attr_recon_repos_at_once allows, each worker making its own git and host calls; within a repository its steps run in their order, and a repository's facts are written by its own worker alone. The lines are crawled as one stream, in the order the kernel captured them. The pass waits for every worker before it records itself.</text>
    <breach>the tests of t03; review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="capture-first">
    <from>BLUEPRINT.md: "It is never linked into `superx`, so nothing it does can reach capture." The operator, 2026-10-25: capturing correct, whole data is the kernel's purpose</from>
    <text>Nothing the crawler or the watcher does slows or stops capture: each reads the kernel's database a bounded page at a time, holds nothing open between pages, and reads nothing while its interval is 0 (attr_recon_read_secs for the crawler, attr_recon_watch_secs for the watcher); a pass or a look that cannot read waits for the next.</text>
    <breach>the live gates, read beside capture's own ticks; review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="no-hardcoded-policy">
    <from>.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Facts go in columns; judgments do not."</from>
    <text>Every tunable is a parameter attr_recon_*, with its fallback in the module's params.rs, reported by name when it is set to a value of the wrong shape; a constant that is no policy carries an audited skill-allow marker. Judgments, such as which work the operator directed, what is rework and how work is split into sittings, are parameters applied when a chart is drawn, never columns and never constants.</text>
    <breach>tools/skill_audit.py (§9); review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="types-flow">
    <from>.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "A `record&lt;table&gt;` is a `Thing` everywhere."</from>
    <text>Types flow end to end: a link is a record id, a kernel line's id a Uuid, a time a DateTime&lt;Utc&gt;, from the line to the column; nothing is cast to text to pass the engine, and a refusal of the engine is fixed in the code, never by loosening the structure (§12, §14).</text>
    <breach>tools/skill_audit.py (§14); the structure's own asserts; review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="gates-are-the-truth">
    <from>.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Code does not exist until `cargo test --workspace` passes."</from>
    <text>A task is done when cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings and python3 tools/skill_audit.py pass and every gate of its file is green; nothing is called done, ready or complete before, and a gate that cannot fail is no gate.</text>
    <breach>CI's required checks; the PR's gate table</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="trunk">
    <from>.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Every change ties to a GitHub issue"</from>
    <text>Each task is one issue, one branch cut from the head of main and one PR into main, merged by the operator; one open branch and one open PR at a time; a merged branch is deleted.</text>
    <breach>review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="specs-are-the-truth">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: "xmls are the source of truth"</from>
    <text>The XML files under specs/recon/ are the source: the issues and the Build Spec page are rendered from them (build_issues.py, build_page.py) and nothing flows back but an issue's number. A spec file is committed only when specs/recon/check.py passes on it, and the check's mutation test proves the check catches what it guards.</text>
    <breach>specs/recon/check.py; specs/recon/mutate_check.py</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="one-agent">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is, the operator's ruling of 2026-10-03 for every build from a spec</from>
    <text>One session, one agent, sequential: the agent that holds a task does every step itself: reading, writing, testing, running the gates, writing the PR. It never spawns, delegates to, forks or messages another agent, subagent, worker, team or background session, and never asks another model to build or verify a part. A PR whose history shows another agent's hand is closed. This rule is a constraint of every task.</text>
    <breach>review of the PR's history</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="one-build-per-task">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is, the operator's rulings of 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-05 for every build from a spec</from>
    <text>Each version of a task file is built once: one agent, one branch, one PR; the operator reviews and merges before the next task starts. The next task's agent reads what the root's method read lists and nothing of an unmerged branch. The model is the operator's choice per task and the label records it. Only when the task file's comparison attribute is yes is it built by several agents, one per model, each from the same file version on its own branch; none is merged until the operator chooses one.</text>
    <breach>review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="agent-decides-only-what-is-listed">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is; the operator, 2026-10-08: "what is left to the agent's determination? not much: we are building the defined product"</from>
    <text>Every task file lists under agent-decides the only things its agent decides: the names and the decomposition inside its own modules and tests, the means of a step within the standard library and the workspace's dependencies, the contents of its fixtures within the cases listed, its commit granularity. Everything else a build needs is written in the task file, the root, the rules, the authority files or the merged code; a build that decides anything else, or decides it differently from the file, is closed, and the step it departed from is named in the review. Every gate names the steps it proves, so a failed gate points at the step to look at.</text>
    <breach>review of the PR against the task file's agent-decides list and its steps; the gates, each naming the steps it proves</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="kickoff">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: a branch and a PR are built from the task's XML file; the operator may name the session that builds a task, the spec's own included</from>
    <text>A build starts with a model pointed at one task file in the repository, specs/recon/tasks/&lt;issue&gt;.xml. Nothing else is said at kickoff. The kickoff line is the first message of a session opened for that build, in a new session, on a checkout of the head of main updated from origin: not the session that wrote the spec, not a session that built or reviewed another build, not one that holds a conversation. There, before the branch is cut, python3 tools/skill_audit.py run on the head of main names what CI's Bright-line skill rules check names on that head, no more and no fewer: it reads no file on a path through a folder named worktrees, and every file elsewhere in the folder, whether git holds it or not; a folder where it names fewer or more is not a build folder, and the build stops there. A model whose session holds anything before the kickoff line beyond what the harness gives every session (its own text, the repository's CLAUDE.md, its settings, a command that set the model or the effort, with its output) stops at that line, says so, and builds nothing. The model writes its own label, &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;, as the root's method model gives it. The operator may instead name a session to build a task, the spec's own included: that session builds when the operator says so, from the task file as written, reads the files in the order the root's method read gives from that word on, and its PR says it was the session the operator named. The file's start element names the branch, the PR title and the first word of every commit; a build started any other way, or on a branch or PR named any other way, does not count.</text>
    <breach>review of the branch name and the PR title against the task file's start element; the PR body stating that the kickoff line was the session's first message, and on which checkout</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="every-rule-binds">
    <from>These rules</from>
    <text>Every rule here binds every task. Every gate of a task names in its proves attribute what it proves; a rule that no gate of a task names binds the task all the same.</text>
    <breach>specs/recon/check.py</breach>
  </rule>

</rules>

The root · specs/recon/root.xml
identity
product
The models, the agents, thinking effort and the operator, measured. One crawler crawls every line the kernel captured, every repository those lines lead to and every host those repositories live on, in depth, into one structure, and the module reports on it in its own tab, on its own port. Two parts of the module read, each for its own task. The crawler gathers the history: it finds the details that are not readily apparent, ties what the agents did to what became of their work, and delivers the critical data no one knew to look for. The watcher watches every session as it works, the coding agents' adversary: it reads the operator's orders and the spec a session executes, judges every action of the agent against them, and reports a problem as it happens, so the operator learns of an agent that vanished, improvised or ignored them before the time is lost.
repository
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/
The module. schema/, the structure, one file, recon.surql: every table the crawler writes, designed with the operator and approved by the operator change by change, in the place CI guards a module's schema; no task writes it. Made by t01 from the merged superx-mod-performance: Cargo.toml; src/, its code, the executable superx-recon, the crawler, one part per source it crawls, and the watcher; tests/, the tests that cross its parts; examples/, watch_replay, which feeds any transcripts through the watcher's own code and serves its routes and page over what it finds, the watcher's proof over all history (#573); ui/, the tab's page on the module's own URL, built with Vite and embedded in superx-recon (#539).
platform
path
crates/
The code merged on main is what every task builds on: superx-kernel, which captures every agent's lines into the kernel's database and holds the substrate verbs, the adapters and the module system; superx-ops; superx, the CLI and the daemon; superx-mod-ui, the core UI; superx-mod-performance, which t01 makes this module; and after it what each task merged. An agent reads of it what its task file's reads element names. No task changes superx-kernel, superx or superx-ops; a change outside the module's own crate is named in its task file, or it is not made.
specs
path
specs/recon/
rules.xml, the rules; root.xml, this file; tasks/, one XML file per task, named by the task's id and name as the sequence gives them, renamed to its GitHub issue number once the issue exists; build_issues.py, the issues rendered from these files; build_page.py, the Build Spec page rendered from them into build_spec.html; check.py, the spec check; mutate_check.py, its mutation test, which plants one defect of each class the check guards and proves the check names it.
data
What the crawler reads and never writes: the kernel's captured lines, in message and telemetry_stream, with SELECT; the repositories the sessions worked in, with git, read-only; the hosts those repositories live on, through their APIs, with GET. What it writes: its own database, superx/recon, with CREATE only; and its mirror clones, in the repos folder of its module folder, <home>/modules/recon/repos/, which nothing else writes. The operator's checkouts are never written, fetched into or run. What the watcher reads and never writes: the newest captured lines, in message and telemetry_stream, with SELECT; the task file a session builds from, in the session's checkout, as a file; the memory folders the agents keep under the projects roots the Claude Code adapter names, as files. It writes nothing anywhere: what it judges it holds in memory, and its page shows it.
branches
main, and one branch per build of a task, cut from the head of main and merged into it by the operator; the next task starts from the merged head and reads nothing of an unmerged branch.
issues
site
github.com/k8nstantin/superx
epic
466
One issue per task, under the epic; its number names the task file, the branch and the PR.
authority
The files that decide. A task reads them by the sections its file names; what they say is the contract. Every quote below is the file's own sentence, verbatim, runs of white space read as one; check.py refuses a quote the file does not hold.
file
path
BLUEPRINT.md
decides
the architecture: what the kernel does and nothing else, that everything else is a module, the module facility contract, the decisions record, and the module's row in the phase plan
The kernel has zero knowledge of any module's domain.
SELECT + CREATE only, no verb ever issues UPDATE / DELETE / DDL.
A module is a self-contained mini-app: own db `superx/<name>` + service account (via `modules provision`), own dir, own log target, own CLI namespace, own parameters; kernel is the hub — modules depend on the kernel only, never on each other
It is never linked into `superx`, so nothing it does can reach capture.
It correlates what the agents did with what the code bases show, diligence before speed.
file
path
.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md
decides
the mandates every change obeys, §1 to §14, which CI and tools/skill_audit.py read by their numbers
NEVER optimize for speed.
Code does not exist until `cargo test --workspace` passes.
Facts go in columns; judgments do not.
A `record<table>` is a `Thing` everywhere.
file
path
SUPERX_SCHEMA.md
decides
the kernel's tables, of which the crawler reads message, telemetry_stream, and the entities and states that name the agents and the sessions
No kernel verb ever emits UPDATE, DELETE, UPSERT, DEFINE, REMOVE, or ALTER.
Every row id is a UUIDv7 generated by the kernel at insert time — never SurrealDB auto-ids.
file
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql
decides
the structure: every table the crawler writes, its fields, its links, its indexes and its vocabularies, approved by the operator change by change; the module applies it as its own schema, byte for byte, and no task writes it
Nothing here is judged.
EVERY ROW IS DERIVED, from the kernel's captured lines and from git,
Nothing is overwritten and nothing is removed (§10).
file
path
crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs
decides
how Claude Code's lines are captured: a user or assistant line as a message row with the line whole; every other line whole as a transcript_event; a line that is not JSON as a transcript_raw; every file beside the transcripts as a transcript_event of kind file
The whole line, whatever its length: nothing is dropped (#471).
file
path
crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/gemini_cli.rs
decides
how Gemini CLI's lines are captured: a user or gemini record as a message row; a header, a header change, an info, error or warning line, and every earlier write of a record, whole, as a transcript_event; an unknown shape as a transcript_raw
file
path
crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_desktop.rs
decides
how Claude Desktop's log records are captured: each record whole as a desktop_event
params
decides
every tunable of the module: attr_recon_* on its registry entity, set with superx param set recon <name> <json>, each running on its fallback until it is set; a limit's fallback is no limit
The module's params.rs names each one and its fallback; a task that adds one names it, its fallback and what it tunes in its file.
credentials
decides
the hosts' credentials: <home>/modules/recon/credentials.json, written by the operator, mode 600, read by the crawler alone
Its shape is decision credentials-file.
code
path
crates/
decides
how the module runs: what the merged code does is what is merged, never what a spec says about it
Read as the task file's reads element names.
not_sources
Earlier spec versions, earlier build branches, the issues' own text, conversations, memory files, the pages, CLAUDE.md, the README files, docs/, any document outside the repository.
method
How a task is done. Every step binds every task; a task file never loosens one.
read
A build starts at the task file (rules.xml kickoff); its start element sends the model here. Then, in this order: the merged code its task file's reads element names, this root, rules.xml and your task file, each whole; then the files your task file names, by the sections it lists. Nothing else: nothing authority not_sources names. The order is required: a session that reads a file out of this order reads again from the first file in order before it writes anything.
silence
Everything the task needs is in those files; an agent assumes nothing and figures nothing out. When something is missing or two of them disagree, stop: build nothing around it, and hand the operator the branch and the PR with exactly which element is silent or which two lines conflict and what was needed (method pr). An agent never changes the structure, the kernel or an authority file; a task that needs one changed is a stop, and the operator holds a design session. What an agent decides on its own is only what its task file lists under agent-decides.
agent
A session opened for the build, with the kickoff line as its first message and nothing before it but what rules.xml kickoff allows the harness: not the spec's own session, not one that built or reviewed another build, unless the operator names that session to build the task (rules.xml kickoff). One session, one agent, sequential: the agent that holds the task does every step itself, reading, writing, testing, running the gates, writing the PR. It never spawns, delegates to, forks or messages another agent, subagent, worker, team or background session, and never asks another model to build or verify a part. A PR whose history shows another agent's hand is closed.
build
Each version of a task file is built once: one agent, one branch from the head of main, one PR into main, reviewed and merged by the operator before the next task starts. The next task's agent starts from the merged head. The model is the operator's choice per task and the label records it. Exception, only when the task file's comparison attribute is yes: one agent per model, each from the same file version on its own branch with its own PR, none merged until the operator compares the results and chooses one; the others are closed unmerged.
branch
feat/<issue>-<name>-v<file version>-<model>-<effort>, cut from the head of main: the task's issue number, the task's name as its file gives it with spaces as hyphens, the task file's version attribute, the label the model wrote at kickoff. The task file's start element writes it out. One branch per build; never amended after its PR is merged; a build of a later file version has a branch of its own beside it.
model
The model label is <model>-<effort>: the model's own name and the effort it runs at, as its session reports them, lowercase, dots and spaces as hyphens; the model writes it itself at kickoff; when its session does not report its effort, it asks the operator for that before it cuts the branch, and is told nothing else. The label names the branch and the PR title's suffix. The agent never changes it during a build.
commits
Every commit message starts with #<issue>. No attribution trailer of any kind. Small commits, each a step that leaves the local gates green. Paths are staged by name, never all at once.
tests
Rust tests, run with cargo test, written as the module's merged tests are: a module's own rules in its tests module, what crosses its parts under crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/, on SurrealDB's in-memory engine with the kernel's schema and the module's own applied, as the merged tests apply them. A task file's tests element lists every rule its steps state, one item each, and the agent writes one test per item, named by it, and none outside the list: a rule the list lacks is a stop, not a test. A fixture property one item needs is stated in that item; a property every test shares is stated once in the tests element's how. The fixtures are the test's own: lines built in the shapes the adapters write, repositories made with git in a scratch folder, hosts answered by a server of the test's own on the loopback; no test reads the operator's instance, transcripts, repositories or a real host, and no fixture copies a real line, name or token.
lint
cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings exits 0, and python3 tools/skill_audit.py names no breach in a file the task owns, before every commit. The audit names each breach under the mandate it breaks, by its number; main's own breaches, in files no task owns, stand as main's.
gates
Run the task's gates in their order; each is a command and an expectation that is a relation over what the command prints, never a number written down. A gate of kind local runs in the checkout, from the repository root; its output goes into the gate table (method pr). A gate of kind live runs on the operator's instance after the operator has deployed the build it names (method deploy), at the agent's request and on the operator's word: the agent runs the command the gate gives, superx-recon at the instance's home, and reads only what superx-recon prints and what its own URL answers; it never reads the kernel's database or the module's with any other client, and never runs a command the gate does not give. A gate run any other way does not count. A gate that fails is a stop.
ownership
Create, modify and touch only the paths the task file lists under owns; a path it lists under forbid is never opened for writing; a path it does not list is not touched. A PR that touches another path is closed.
pr
One PR per branch, into main, titled "#<issue> <name> v<file version> · <model>-<effort>" as the task file's start element writes it, opened by the agent with gh once every local gate is green, or when the build stops (method silence, gates). Its body, in this order: the spec version built, the root's version and the task file's version attribute; the model label; the session: that the kickoff line was its first message, the order the files were read (method read) and the main head the branch was cut from; the gate table: id, the command as run, exit, the matched line or the gate's output, local or live, and for a live gate the build it ran on; what stopped, if anything: the element that is silent or the two lines that conflict, and what was needed; the lint and test commands as run with their last line; for every step of the task file, the function or test that carries it, so a reviewer who finds a fault knows which step to look at; and the Skill compliance section CI requires, each of the three gates with its result. The operator reviews and merges; the agent never merges.
deliverable
A task delivers code, tests and the PR with its gate table. Nothing else: no change to the structure beyond its task file, none to the kernel, no data, no deploy.
rerun
A task is re-run when its task file changes: a new file version, and a new branch from the head of main carrying that version in its name, so it stands beside the earlier build's branch; the earlier branch is not amended, and one that is not merged is closed unmerged. A change to the root or the rules alone re-runs a built task only when the build as made no longer follows them as changed; the review names such a task, and its task file takes a new version. Nothing is patched on a merged branch. A task already merged is re-run only after the operator has reverted, on main, its merge and the merge of every task after it, so that the re-run starts from what its file's before element states; the tasks after it are then re-run in the order of the sequence.
deploy
The operator runs the module from a build of one commit. At the agent's request (method gates) the operator deploys it by the steps below in their order; an agent never builds the release, installs, provisions, starts or stops anything on the instance, and never touches the superx daemon or its binary. <home> is the instance's home and <bin> the folder its executables are installed in. The agent's request names these commands with every value filled.
step · build
n
1
where
a worktree checked out at the commit, never a checkout on another branch
cmd
cargo build --release -p superx-mod-recon
result
target/release/superx-recon, built from that commit
step · install
n
2
cmd
mkdir -p <home>/modules/recon/backup && cp -p <bin>/superx-recon <home>/modules/recon/backup/superx-recon-<the running build's commit>
cmd
cp target/release/superx-recon <bin>/superx-recon.new && mv -f <bin>/superx-recon.new <bin>/superx-recon
step · provision
n
3
Only when the task file says so: when the structure changed, or when the build derives what the rows already written lack, since no row is ever changed. The database is rebuilt from the file, run by the operator, the root password typed at a terminal or given as input from <home>/db/superx-credentials, which nothing prints; every row is crawled again (rules derived-and-rebuilt).
cmd
superx-recon --home <home> --initialize
step · restart
n
4
cmd
superx-recon --home <home> stop
cmd
superx-recon --home <home> start
specs/recon/root.xml, the XML as written
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<!--
  SuperX Recon: the root of the build spec. Version 0.

  An agent reads the merged code its task file names, this root, rules.xml, its one task file under tasks/ and the files
  that task file names, in the order the method's read step gives, and nothing else. The root says where the truth is
  and how the work is done; of the authority files and the merged code it says only what a task relies on, and leaves
  the rest to them.
  Parts: identity, authority, method, sequence, decisions.
  Nothing here writes a count of the captured lines, the sessions, the repositories or the hosts, or a value of the
  operator's instance: a home, a session, a token.
-->
<root product="SuperX Recon" epic="466" version="0" date="2026-10-26">

  <identity>
    <product>The models, the agents, thinking effort and the operator, measured. One crawler crawls every line the kernel captured, every repository those lines lead to and every host those repositories live on, in depth, into one structure, and the module reports on it in its own tab, on its own port. Two parts of the module read, each for its own task. The crawler gathers the history: it finds the details that are not readily apparent, ties what the agents did to what became of their work, and delivers the critical data no one knew to look for. The watcher watches every session as it works, the coding agents' adversary: it reads the operator's orders and the spec a session executes, judges every action of the agent against them, and reports a problem as it happens, so the operator learns of an agent that vanished, improvised or ignored them before the time is lost.</product>
    <repository path="crates/superx-mod-recon/">The module. schema/, the structure, one file, recon.surql: every table the crawler writes, designed with the operator and approved by the operator change by change, in the place CI guards a module's schema; no task writes it. Made by t01 from the merged superx-mod-performance: Cargo.toml; src/, its code, the executable superx-recon, the crawler, one part per source it crawls, and the watcher; tests/, the tests that cross its parts; examples/, watch_replay, which feeds any transcripts through the watcher's own code and serves its routes and page over what it finds, the watcher's proof over all history (#573); ui/, the tab's page on the module's own URL, built with Vite and embedded in superx-recon (#539).</repository>
    <platform path="crates/">The code merged on main is what every task builds on: superx-kernel, which captures every agent's lines into the kernel's database and holds the substrate verbs, the adapters and the module system; superx-ops; superx, the CLI and the daemon; superx-mod-ui, the core UI; superx-mod-performance, which t01 makes this module; and after it what each task merged. An agent reads of it what its task file's reads element names. No task changes superx-kernel, superx or superx-ops; a change outside the module's own crate is named in its task file, or it is not made.</platform>
    <specs path="specs/recon/">rules.xml, the rules; root.xml, this file; tasks/, one XML file per task, named by the task's id and name as the sequence gives them, renamed to its GitHub issue number once the issue exists; build_issues.py, the issues rendered from these files; build_page.py, the Build Spec page rendered from them into build_spec.html; check.py, the spec check; mutate_check.py, its mutation test, which plants one defect of each class the check guards and proves the check names it.</specs>
    <data>What the crawler reads and never writes: the kernel's captured lines, in message and telemetry_stream, with SELECT; the repositories the sessions worked in, with git, read-only; the hosts those repositories live on, through their APIs, with GET. What it writes: its own database, superx/recon, with CREATE only; and its mirror clones, in the repos folder of its module folder, &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/repos/, which nothing else writes. The operator's checkouts are never written, fetched into or run. What the watcher reads and never writes: the newest captured lines, in message and telemetry_stream, with SELECT; the task file a session builds from, in the session's checkout, as a file; the memory folders the agents keep under the projects roots the Claude Code adapter names, as files. It writes nothing anywhere: what it judges it holds in memory, and its page shows it.</data>
    <branches>main, and one branch per build of a task, cut from the head of main and merged into it by the operator; the next task starts from the merged head and reads nothing of an unmerged branch.</branches>
    <issues site="github.com/k8nstantin/superx" epic="466">One issue per task, under the epic; its number names the task file, the branch and the PR.</issues>
  </identity>

  <authority>
    <note>The files that decide. A task reads them by the sections its file names; what they say is the contract. Every quote below is the file's own sentence, verbatim, runs of white space read as one; check.py refuses a quote the file does not hold.</note>

    <file path="BLUEPRINT.md" decides="the architecture: what the kernel does and nothing else, that everything else is a module, the module facility contract, the decisions record, and the module's row in the phase plan">
      <quote>The kernel has zero knowledge of any module's domain.</quote>
      <quote>SELECT + CREATE only, no verb ever issues UPDATE / DELETE / DDL.</quote>
      <quote>A module is a self-contained mini-app: own db `superx/&lt;name&gt;` + service account (via `modules provision`), own dir, own log target, own CLI namespace, own parameters; kernel is the hub — modules depend on the kernel only, never on each other</quote>
      <quote>It is never linked into `superx`, so nothing it does can reach capture.</quote>
      <quote>It correlates what the agents did with what the code bases show, diligence before speed.</quote>
    </file>

    <file path=".claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md" decides="the mandates every change obeys, §1 to §14, which CI and tools/skill_audit.py read by their numbers">
      <quote>NEVER optimize for speed.</quote>
      <quote>Code does not exist until `cargo test --workspace` passes.</quote>
      <quote>Facts go in columns; judgments do not.</quote>
      <quote>A `record&lt;table&gt;` is a `Thing` everywhere.</quote>
    </file>

    <file path="SUPERX_SCHEMA.md" decides="the kernel's tables, of which the crawler reads message, telemetry_stream, and the entities and states that name the agents and the sessions">
      <quote>No kernel verb ever emits UPDATE, DELETE, UPSERT, DEFINE, REMOVE, or ALTER.</quote>
      <quote>Every row id is a UUIDv7 generated by the kernel at insert time — never SurrealDB auto-ids.</quote>
    </file>

    <file path="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql" decides="the structure: every table the crawler writes, its fields, its links, its indexes and its vocabularies, approved by the operator change by change; the module applies it as its own schema, byte for byte, and no task writes it">
      <quote>Nothing here is judged.</quote>
      <quote>EVERY ROW IS DERIVED, from the kernel's captured lines and from git,</quote>
      <quote>Nothing is overwritten and nothing is removed (§10).</quote>
    </file>

    <file path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs" decides="how Claude Code's lines are captured: a user or assistant line as a message row with the line whole; every other line whole as a transcript_event; a line that is not JSON as a transcript_raw; every file beside the transcripts as a transcript_event of kind file">
      <quote>The whole line, whatever its length: nothing is dropped (#471).</quote>
    </file>
    <file path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/gemini_cli.rs" decides="how Gemini CLI's lines are captured: a user or gemini record as a message row; a header, a header change, an info, error or warning line, and every earlier write of a record, whole, as a transcript_event; an unknown shape as a transcript_raw"/>
    <file path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_desktop.rs" decides="how Claude Desktop's log records are captured: each record whole as a desktop_event"/>

    <params decides="every tunable of the module: attr_recon_* on its registry entity, set with superx param set recon &lt;name&gt; &lt;json&gt;, each running on its fallback until it is set; a limit's fallback is no limit">The module's params.rs names each one and its fallback; a task that adds one names it, its fallback and what it tunes in its file.</params>
    <credentials decides="the hosts' credentials: &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/credentials.json, written by the operator, mode 600, read by the crawler alone">Its shape is decision credentials-file.</credentials>

    <code path="crates/" decides="how the module runs: what the merged code does is what is merged, never what a spec says about it">Read as the task file's reads element names.</code>

    <not_sources>Earlier spec versions, earlier build branches, the issues' own text, conversations, memory files, the pages, CLAUDE.md, the README files, docs/, any document outside the repository.</not_sources>
  </authority>

  <method>
    <note>How a task is done. Every step binds every task; a task file never loosens one.</note>

    <read>A build starts at the task file (rules.xml kickoff); its start element sends the model here. Then, in this order: the merged code its task file's reads element names, this root, rules.xml and your task file, each whole; then the files your task file names, by the sections it lists. Nothing else: nothing authority not_sources names. The order is required: a session that reads a file out of this order reads again from the first file in order before it writes anything.</read>

    <silence>Everything the task needs is in those files; an agent assumes nothing and figures nothing out. When something is missing or two of them disagree, stop: build nothing around it, and hand the operator the branch and the PR with exactly which element is silent or which two lines conflict and what was needed (method pr). An agent never changes the structure, the kernel or an authority file; a task that needs one changed is a stop, and the operator holds a design session. What an agent decides on its own is only what its task file lists under agent-decides.</silence>

    <agent>A session opened for the build, with the kickoff line as its first message and nothing before it but what rules.xml kickoff allows the harness: not the spec's own session, not one that built or reviewed another build, unless the operator names that session to build the task (rules.xml kickoff). One session, one agent, sequential: the agent that holds the task does every step itself, reading, writing, testing, running the gates, writing the PR. It never spawns, delegates to, forks or messages another agent, subagent, worker, team or background session, and never asks another model to build or verify a part. A PR whose history shows another agent's hand is closed.</agent>

    <build>Each version of a task file is built once: one agent, one branch from the head of main, one PR into main, reviewed and merged by the operator before the next task starts. The next task's agent starts from the merged head. The model is the operator's choice per task and the label records it. Exception, only when the task file's comparison attribute is yes: one agent per model, each from the same file version on its own branch with its own PR, none merged until the operator compares the results and chooses one; the others are closed unmerged.</build>

    <branch>feat/&lt;issue&gt;-&lt;name&gt;-v&lt;file version&gt;-&lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;, cut from the head of main: the task's issue number, the task's name as its file gives it with spaces as hyphens, the task file's version attribute, the label the model wrote at kickoff. The task file's start element writes it out. One branch per build; never amended after its PR is merged; a build of a later file version has a branch of its own beside it.</branch>

    <model>The model label is &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;: the model's own name and the effort it runs at, as its session reports them, lowercase, dots and spaces as hyphens; the model writes it itself at kickoff; when its session does not report its effort, it asks the operator for that before it cuts the branch, and is told nothing else. The label names the branch and the PR title's suffix. The agent never changes it during a build.</model>

    <commits>Every commit message starts with #&lt;issue&gt;. No attribution trailer of any kind. Small commits, each a step that leaves the local gates green. Paths are staged by name, never all at once.</commits>

    <tests>Rust tests, run with cargo test, written as the module's merged tests are: a module's own rules in its tests module, what crosses its parts under crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/, on SurrealDB's in-memory engine with the kernel's schema and the module's own applied, as the merged tests apply them. A task file's tests element lists every rule its steps state, one item each, and the agent writes one test per item, named by it, and none outside the list: a rule the list lacks is a stop, not a test. A fixture property one item needs is stated in that item; a property every test shares is stated once in the tests element's how. The fixtures are the test's own: lines built in the shapes the adapters write, repositories made with git in a scratch folder, hosts answered by a server of the test's own on the loopback; no test reads the operator's instance, transcripts, repositories or a real host, and no fixture copies a real line, name or token.</tests>

    <lint>cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings exits 0, and python3 tools/skill_audit.py names no breach in a file the task owns, before every commit. The audit names each breach under the mandate it breaks, by its number; main's own breaches, in files no task owns, stand as main's.</lint>

    <gates>Run the task's gates in their order; each is a command and an expectation that is a relation over what the command prints, never a number written down.
      A gate of kind local runs in the checkout, from the repository root; its output goes into the gate table (method pr).
      A gate of kind live runs on the operator's instance after the operator has deployed the build it names (method deploy), at the agent's request and on the operator's word: the agent runs the command the gate gives, superx-recon at the instance's home, and reads only what superx-recon prints and what its own URL answers; it never reads the kernel's database or the module's with any other client, and never runs a command the gate does not give. A gate run any other way does not count. A gate that fails is a stop.</gates>

    <ownership>Create, modify and touch only the paths the task file lists under owns; a path it lists under forbid is never opened for writing; a path it does not list is not touched. A PR that touches another path is closed.</ownership>

    <pr>One PR per branch, into main, titled "#&lt;issue&gt; &lt;name&gt; v&lt;file version&gt; · &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;" as the task file's start element writes it, opened by the agent with gh once every local gate is green, or when the build stops (method silence, gates). Its body, in this order: the spec version built, the root's version and the task file's version attribute; the model label; the session: that the kickoff line was its first message, the order the files were read (method read) and the main head the branch was cut from; the gate table: id, the command as run, exit, the matched line or the gate's output, local or live, and for a live gate the build it ran on; what stopped, if anything: the element that is silent or the two lines that conflict, and what was needed; the lint and test commands as run with their last line; for every step of the task file, the function or test that carries it, so a reviewer who finds a fault knows which step to look at; and the Skill compliance section CI requires, each of the three gates with its result. The operator reviews and merges; the agent never merges.</pr>

    <deliverable>A task delivers code, tests and the PR with its gate table. Nothing else: no change to the structure beyond its task file, none to the kernel, no data, no deploy.</deliverable>

    <rerun>A task is re-run when its task file changes: a new file version, and a new branch from the head of main carrying that version in its name, so it stands beside the earlier build's branch; the earlier branch is not amended, and one that is not merged is closed unmerged. A change to the root or the rules alone re-runs a built task only when the build as made no longer follows them as changed; the review names such a task, and its task file takes a new version. Nothing is patched on a merged branch. A task already merged is re-run only after the operator has reverted, on main, its merge and the merge of every task after it, so that the re-run starts from what its file's before element states; the tasks after it are then re-run in the order of the sequence.</rerun>

    <deploy>
      <note>The operator runs the module from a build of one commit. At the agent's request (method gates) the operator deploys it by the steps below in their order; an agent never builds the release, installs, provisions, starts or stops anything on the instance, and never touches the superx daemon or its binary. &lt;home&gt; is the instance's home and &lt;bin&gt; the folder its executables are installed in. The agent's request names these commands with every value filled.</note>
      <step n="1" name="build">
        <where>a worktree checked out at the commit, never a checkout on another branch</where>
        <cmd>cargo build --release -p superx-mod-recon</cmd>
        <result>target/release/superx-recon, built from that commit</result>
      </step>
      <step n="2" name="install">
        <cmd>mkdir -p &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/backup &amp;&amp; cp -p &lt;bin&gt;/superx-recon &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/backup/superx-recon-&lt;the running build's commit&gt;</cmd>
        <cmd>cp target/release/superx-recon &lt;bin&gt;/superx-recon.new &amp;&amp; mv -f &lt;bin&gt;/superx-recon.new &lt;bin&gt;/superx-recon</cmd>
      </step>
      <step n="3" name="provision">
        <note>Only when the task file says so: when the structure changed, or when the build derives what the rows already written lack, since no row is ever changed. The database is rebuilt from the file, run by the operator, the root password typed at a terminal or given as input from &lt;home&gt;/db/superx-credentials, which nothing prints; every row is crawled again (rules derived-and-rebuilt).</note>
        <cmd>superx-recon --home &lt;home&gt; --initialize</cmd>
      </step>
      <step n="4" name="restart">
        <cmd>superx-recon --home &lt;home&gt; stop</cmd>
        <cmd>superx-recon --home &lt;home&gt; start</cmd>
      </step>
    </deploy>
  </method>

  <sequence>
    <note>Five tasks: the structure first, whole, before anything fills it; then the crawler that fills it, the lines in one task and the repositories with their hosts in the next; then the tab, designed with the operator once the crawler is merged and built piece by piece (decision the-tab); then the watcher, the module's second part (decision watcher). Each task but the tab is one build an agent does from its file; the operator deploys and runs what it builds. A task starts when the task before it is merged on main. Its file under tasks/ carries, verbatim, what the structure and the authority files say about its piece, what it builds, its gates and what it owns; this list carries the piece, the files it reflects and what it builds or removes.</note>

    <task id="t01" issue="536" name="structure" file="specs/recon/tasks/536.xml" reflects="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql BLUEPRINT.md" command="superx-recon --initialize; superx-recon start; superx-recon stop; superx-recon status; superx-recon run" builds="crates/superx-mod-recon/" removes="crates/superx-mod-performance/ crates/superx-mod-repos/">
      <piece>The module becomes superx-mod-recon, and its structure is built whole before anything fills it: the crate, from superx-mod-performance, its code moved and renamed and its crawler doing what it does today; the executable superx-recon; the database superx/recon, from the structure, crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, which the crate applies byte for byte and never writes: the tables a measure uses or the crawler writes, every source kept whole beside them: the lines in the kernel, git in the repositories and the mirrors, every answer of every read API in host_object; the parameters attr_recon_*; the module folder with its repos folder and its credentials file; the status, listing every table and which hosts have credentials. superx-mod-performance and superx-mod-repos leave the workspace, and the core UI links to no module's own UI (#534).</piece>
      <quote from="BLUEPRINT.md">It is never linked into `superx`, so nothing it does can reach capture.</quote>
      <quote from="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">Nothing is overwritten and nothing is removed (§10).</quote>
    </task>

    <task id="t02" issue="537" name="lines" file="specs/recon/tasks/537.xml" depends="t01" reflects="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/" command="superx-recon run" builds="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/">
      <piece>The crawler crawls the captured lines in depth. Every line the kernel holds, of every agent and every kind, is read once into the tables it feeds, and its pass counts the lines that fed none; every field seen on a kind of line is a line_field row that says whether a column holds it; every pass checks the lines it read against the lines the kernel holds in the same range, and a difference makes the pass partial and is named. It fills the lines' tables whole: sessions and their places, prompts, triggers, replies, their states, thinking, tool calls and their results, edits, git events, tests and builds, turns, compactions and recaps.</piece>
      <quote from="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs">The whole line, whatever its length: nothing is dropped (#471).</quote>
    </task>

    <task id="t03" issue="538" name="repositories" file="specs/recon/tasks/538.xml" depends="t02" reflects="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql" command="superx-recon run" builds="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/repos/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/hosts/">
      <piece>The crawler crawls the repositories and their hosts. From the working directories the lines name, and the roots the operator names, every checkout and repository; one worker per repository, as many at once as attr_recon_repos_at_once allows. For each repository its mirror clone in the repos folder, fetched with the credentials, which keeps every fact git holds, and the facts the structure's tables hold: commits with their files, their time zones, patch ids and signatures; every reflog entry and the operations a checkout is in the middle of; where each commit sits and what landed through another commit, what each commit still holds at the main line's tip, remotes, and every git call. From the hosts, with the credentials and GET only, every answer whole in host_object, and beside it the pull requests, their commits and their CI runs. Every commit tied to the session that made it.</piece>
      <quote from="BLUEPRINT.md">It correlates what the agents did with what the code bases show, diligence before speed.</quote>
    </task>

    <task id="t04" issue="539" name="tab" file="specs/recon/tasks/539.xml" depends="t03" reflects="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql" command="superx-recon run" builds="crates/superx-mod-recon/">
      <piece>The tab: what the module shows on its own port, from what t02 and t03 filled. Designed with the operator once t03 is merged, and built piece by piece, each piece its own PR, without a task file (decision the-tab).</piece>
    </task>

    <task id="t05" issue="566" name="watcher" file="specs/recon/tasks/566.xml" depends="t04" reflects="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs" command="superx-recon run" builds="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/watcher/ crates/superx-mod-recon/ui/src/watcher/ crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/watcher.rs">
      <piece>The watcher: the module's second part, beside the crawler and sharing nothing that runs with it. Its own loop follows the newest captured lines of every session, holds one watch per session in memory, judges each line against the operator's orders and against the task file the session executes, finds each session's memory folder, and reports what it finds on a Watcher tab of the module's page, as it happens, with a notification in the browser. It writes nothing.</piece>
      <quote from="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">Nothing here is judged.</quote>
    </task>
  </sequence>

  <decisions>
    <note>What was decided, when, and where it lives; what is still open. A confirmed decision is written into the file or element named, which is the authority; it is listed here so an agent knows a question is closed. An open decision is a question an agent never answers: a task that needs it stops. An open decision names in closes-before the task before whose file it is closed, and check.py refuses that file while the decision is open.</note>

    <decision id="recon" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="identity; sequence t01">The module is superx-mod-recon: the executable superx-recon, the database superx/recon, the parameters attr_recon_*, the module folder &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/. It replaces superx-mod-performance, whose name the operator rejected.</decision>
    <decision id="one-module-one-crawler" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-25" where="BLUEPRINT.md; rules one-module-its-own-process">One module, one schema, one crawler: the lines and the repositories are crawled by one crawler into one database, through its sources, and a new source plugs in beside them.</decision>
    <decision id="own-process" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-24" where="rules one-module-its-own-process">Its own executable, process, database, port and tab, never linked into superx; it reads the kernel's data and the kernel never depends on it.</decision>
    <decision id="crawler-is-the-reader" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules crawler-is-the-reader">The crawler is the only thing that reads; nothing beside it, and no part of it, is called a reader. Since 2026-11-04 the watcher reads beside it, for its own task (decision watcher); nothing in the module is called a reader still.</decision>
    <decision id="watcher" status="confirmed" date="2026-11-04" where="identity; rules crawler-is-the-reader, kernel-read-only, capture-first; sequence t05">Recon has two parts, the crawler that gathers the history and the watcher, sharing nothing that runs: they do very different tasks. The watcher watches every session like a hawk, especially the operator's instructions, which usually change the direction or correct the agent; it watches what the agent does, whether it follows the instructions or improvises, judges it against the operator's command and against the spec it executes, and reports problems as they happen: the coding agents' adversary. Its loop is the operator's parameter and it scales to many sessions at once. The operator approved its design on the page of 2026-11-04 and ordered it built from this spec.</decision>
    <decision id="crawl-not-skim" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-25" where="rules crawl-not-skim">Slow, diligent, methodical: every line, field, commit and ref accounted for and checked against its source; the details that are not readily apparent found and tied together.</decision>
    <decision id="repos-from-the-logs" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-25" where="rules repos-from-the-logs">The repositories come from the session logs; the logs report a change first and the crawler follows them.</decision>
    <decision id="hosts-and-mirrors" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules hosts-read-only-with-credentials, never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t01, t03">The credentials serve both: the hosts' APIs, read-only, and the crawler's own mirror clones, fetched with them. The structure says so: git never fetches into the operator's checkouts, and the mirrors are fetched and nothing else is.</decision>
    <decision id="repos-folder" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="identity data">The mirror clones live in a folder of their own, under the module's folder, which nothing else writes.</decision>
    <decision id="mirrors-take-everything" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t03">The crawler fetches into its own mirrors freely: one mirror per remote, a fetch takes every ref the remote advertises, and nothing a mirror has fetched is ever pruned or collected, so a branch rewritten or deleted on the host keeps what the crawler saw. The more it holds, the more it can find.</decision>
    <decision id="parallel-by-repository" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules parallel-by-repository; sequence t03">The crawler scales by repository: one worker per repository, as many at once as a parameter allows.</decision>
    <decision id="structure-first" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="sequence">The structure first, whole; then the crawler that fills it; then the tab, designed after.</decision>
    <decision id="four-prs" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="sequence; decisions the-tab, watcher">Four PRs at most; fewer PRs, more work in each. The structure, the lines and the repositories took one each; the tab took three, built piece by piece on the operator's word of 2026-11-03 (decision the-tab); the watcher, decided on 2026-11-04, is one task more (decision watcher).</decision>
    <decision id="specs-are-the-truth" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules specs-are-the-truth; identity specs">The XML files are the source of truth, in the data lake's pack: a root, the rules, one file per task, and the tools that check and render them; a branch and a PR are built from a task file.</decision>
    <decision id="deep-structure" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-25" where="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">Every field the agents write and everything git holds has a place: a column, a table, or its source, kept whole, which the structure names; line_field lists every field the agents write and whether a column holds it.</decision>
    <decision id="old-data" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-25" where="rules derived-and-rebuilt">The kernel's lines captured before a capture fix are read as they are: nothing is recaptured or repaired.</decision>

    <decision id="hosts-take-everything" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="sequence t01, t03; rules crawl-not-skim">From the hosts, everything their read APIs return for each repository the crawler crawls: every answer kept whole in host_object, pull requests, their commits, reviews, comments, checks and pipelines, merges, issues, releases and the rest the answers hold, beside typed tables for what is measured, and every call recorded. Less data is more assumption; the operator has intuition already, and the crawler exists for the facts the operator does not know. The tables themselves are designed into the structure from the hosts' own API documentation, and the operator approves the structure (§7, §11).</decision>
    <decision id="credentials-file" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="authority credentials; rules hosts-read-only-with-credentials">&lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/credentials.json, in JSON as the instance's own files are, written by the operator: one entry per host, each a token with every read scope the host offers and no write scope; the crawler refuses the file when anyone but its owner can read it. A mirror's fetch gets its credential from superx-recon itself, answering git as its askpass program, so a token is never written into git's configuration, a URL, a command line or the environment.</decision>
    <decision id="live-gates" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="method gates, deploy">A task's live gates run before its merge, on the operator's instance, once the operator has deployed the branch's build of superx-recon; superx and its daemon are never touched, and every row the build writes is derived, so the next deploy rebuilds what it needs.</decision>
    <decision id="retire-performance" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="sequence t01">At t01's deploy superx-performance is stopped and superx-recon started in its place; once recon's first pass is complete, the operator drops superx/performance and superx/repos under root. Every row they hold is derived, and recon derives it again from the same lines and repositories.</decision>
    <decision id="repos-at-once" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules parallel-by-repository, nothing-dropped">attr_recon_repos_at_once falls back to no limit: every repository a pass crawls gets its own worker at once, unless the operator sets a number. A limit's fallback is no limit.</decision>
    <decision id="structure" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-29" where="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">The structure: the tables a measure uses or the crawler writes, cut on 2026-10-29 from the structure designed on 2026-10-26 (the deep crawl's tables, the hosts' and the mirrors', git's facts), every source kept whole beside them, and a table joins when a measure needs it; checked on an empty SurrealDB 3.1.4. Approved by the operator on 2026-10-26, and cut on the operator's word on 2026-10-29: "yes lets slim up", "yes" (§7, §11). It lives where CI guards a module's schema, so every change to it carries the operator's approval marker.</decision>
    <decision id="git-facts" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">Git's own documentation read whole (git 2.50.1), and what git holds that the structure lacked added to it as far as a measure uses it: where each commit was made in local time, the commits that make the same change, signatures, the operations a checkout is in the middle of and line counts blind to whitespace; files copied, how each merge's conflicts were resolved, upstreams, annotated tags and trailers stay in git (decision structure). Approved by the operator on 2026-10-26: "1 -9 yes change the schema for more data".</decision>
    <decision id="git-cli" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t03">The crawler reads git through git itself, one process per call, never a library: every call is a git_call row, and git's answers are git's own. The operator, 2026-10-26: "Recommendation: keep the CLI. yes".</decision>
    <decision id="http-client" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="sequence t03">The hosts' APIs are called through reqwest, with rustls, the one HTTP client for both hosts: no host's own client library, since every answer is kept whole with its headers, and no maintained Bitbucket Cloud client exists. Its redirects drop the credential when they leave the host. Recommended with the git CLI and answered with the same yes.</decision>
    <decision id="devlake-ideas" status="confirmed" date="2026-11-01" where="sequence t03; crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">Four more facts from Apache DevLake's design, proposed on the structure page on 2026-10-26: labels and milestones, issue events, the e-mails an account commits as, the commits each deployment shipped. No table joins for them: t03 crawls each and keeps it whole in host_object, the labels and milestones in each issue and pull request, the issue events as issue_event and timeline_event objects, the e-mail beside the account in each pull request's commits, each deployment's commit in each deployment; git holds every e-mail a commit was made with. A table joins when a measure needs one (decision structure). The operator, 2026-11-01: "1", kept whole, no table.</decision>
    <decision id="the-tab" status="confirmed" date="2026-11-03" where="sequence t04">What the tab shows and how, designed with the operator from what t02 and t03 filled, on the tab's design page. The operator ruled it built piece by piece instead of from a task file, each piece its own PR, deployed and shown before the next: the crawler's stats and the parameters (#558), now and the repositories (#559), the results (#560).</decision>
  </decisions>

Your label is your own, <model>-<effort>: your model's name and the effort you run at, as your session reports them, lowercase, dots and spaces as hyphens; when your session does not report your effort, ask the operator for it before you cut the branch.
