piece
The structure task. superx-mod-performance becomes superx-mod-recon around the approved structure, which is already in place, crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, and which this task never writes. The crate's code moves into that folder and takes the recon names: the executable superx-recon, the module recon, the database superx/recon, the parameters attr_recon_*, the module folder <home>/modules/recon/ with its repos folder and its credentials file. The crawler does what it does today, on the new structure, which holds everything it writes today. Status lists every table of the structure, and says what the credentials file holds by host, never a token. superx-mod-performance and superx-mod-repos leave the workspace, and the core UI links to no module's own UI (#534). Nothing is crawled that is not crawled today: the lines in depth are t02, the repositories, their mirrors and their hosts t03.

This spec, and the structure, crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, the one file in that folder. On the operator's instance superx-performance runs, and superx-recon does not exist.

Nothing else of the workspace reads the module.

step · the structure
n
2
lib.rs: SCHEMA_DDL is include_str!("../schema/recon.surql") and MODULE_NAME is "recon", so the module's database is superx/recon and its account superx_mod_recon (crates/superx-kernel/src/substrate.rs module_db), its registry entry recon, its folder <home>/modules/recon/. The structure holds every table and column the crawler writes today and every word it writes in them, so the crawler's code writes what it writes today, unchanged.
step · the names
n
3
Every name the module shows takes recon: the executable superx-recon in its command line, its help, its messages and its errors; its pid file superx-recon.pid and its output log superx-recon.out in the module folder; daemon.rs's executable name superx-recon, a running process told by its name as ps prints it, so names_this_executable accepts superx-recon alone, the Linux cut of a long name keeping the rule it has today for any name it would cut; the Host guard's refusal, status's first line and own_hint's provisioning command name superx-recon; every tracing target "performance" becomes "recon". The doc comments say recon. None says the core UI links to the module: the module publishes its URL in the registry (attr_module_ui_url, unchanged), and the core UI does not read it after step 11.
step · the parameters
n
4
Every attr_performance_<name> becomes attr_recon_<name>: the same names after the prefix, in the same order, with the same fallbacks, the same shapes and the same reports. No parameter is added or removed. The struct params.rs reads the set values with takes a name other than `reader`.
step · the module folder
n
5
At run, after the module is registered as starting and before it says it is active: the module folder holds repos/, made with mode 700 when it is absent and left as it is, mode included, when it is present; and credentials.json, written when it is absent, with mode 600, holding the template, and never written when it is present, whatever it holds. The template is this JSON object, written by serde_json::to_string_pretty with a newline after it: "_doc" with the text "Read-only tokens, one per host. github.com: a fine-grained personal access token with read access to metadata, contents, pull requests, issues, actions, commit statuses, deployments and administration. bitbucket.org: an API token with the read scopes. Keep this file mode 600. superx-recon reads it, never writes it once it exists, and never shows a token."; "github.com" with the object holding "token": ""; "bitbucket.org" with the object holding "token": "". Instance gains the two paths, <home>/modules/recon/repos and <home>/modules/recon/credentials.json, beside the pid file and the logs.
step · the credentials
n
6
A module of the crate reads credentials.json, when status renders and when a part of the crawler asks for a host's token (t03). Its answer is one of: none, the file is absent; refused, the file's mode lets anyone but its owner read or write it (its mode and 0o077 is not 0), and nothing of it is read; unreadable, the file cannot be read, with the system's reason, or is not a JSON object whose every key that does not start with "_" names an object with a string "token", with serde_json's reason or the key that broke it; or the hosts, each github.com or bitbucket.org, with its token or no token when the string is empty, and every other key that does not start with "_" named as an unknown host. A key that starts with "_" is a note and is skipped. A token is held in a type whose Debug and Display print <token> and nothing of the token, and no log line, status text, error or test output ever holds one.
step · the status
n
7
status lists every table of the structure but pass and pass_source, each once, in groups, each table with the name its rows are called: sessions, models, tools, repos and census, each with the tables of today's group that the structure keeps, in today's order and with today's names; and, after repos and before census, hosts: host_repository "repositories on hosts", host_user "host accounts", host_crawl "host crawls", host_call "host calls", host_object "host objects", mirror "mirrors", pull_request "pull requests", pull_request_state "pull request states", pull_request_commit "pull request commits", ci_run "CI runs", ci_run_state "CI run states". Today's context group keeps no table, and is gone. After the groups and before the last pass, two lines: `credentials <path>: ` and then, when the answer of step 6 is the hosts, github.com then bitbucket.org, each `<host> token set` or `<host> no token`, then each unknown host as `<host> unknown`, joined by ` · `; when it is none, `none`; refused, `refused, mode <its mode and 0o777, in octal>; chmod 600 <path>`; unreadable, `unreadable: <the reason>`. Then `repos <path>`, the repos folder. When the database is not available, the same two lines follow its line.

agent-decides
The names and the decomposition of the private functions and types inside the crate and its tests, the credentials module's name included; the means of each step within the standard library and the workspace's dependencies; the contents of the test fixtures within the items the tests element lists; the granularity of the commits within the root's commits step. Nothing else: no name of a command, file, path, table, parameter, line or status word other than this file's; no change to the crawler's behaviour; no new dependency; no number; no default; no test the list lacks. What is not here and not in the root, the rules, the authority files or the merged code is a stop (root method silence).
gates
Local gates run in the checkout, from the repository root. The live gates run after the operator has deployed the branch's head (root method deploy) with these steps of this task's own: the release built with cargo build --release -p superx-mod-recon; superx-recon installed in <bin>, where none was before, so nothing is backed up; superx-performance --home <home> stop; superx-recon --home <home> --initialize, the root password typed by the operator in a terminal; superx-recon --home <home> start. superx-performance stays installed and stopped until the operator drops its database (decision retire-performance). The agent asks for these with every value filled, waits for the operator's word, and runs the live gates' commands only. <port> is the port status prints in its url line.

  <piece>The structure task. superx-mod-performance becomes superx-mod-recon around the approved structure, which is already in place, crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, and which this task never writes.     <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/" name="the recon module">
      <step n="1" name="the move">Every file of crates/superx-mod-performance moves to crates/superx-mod-recon with git mv, keeping its history, except schema/performance.surql, which is removed with git rm: the structure that replaces it is crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, already there.       <step n="2" name="the structure">lib.rs: SCHEMA_DDL is include_str!("../schema/recon.surql") and MODULE_NAME is "recon", so the module's database is superx/recon and its account superx_mod_recon (crates/superx-kernel/src/substrate.rs module_db), its registry entry recon, its folder &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/. The structure holds every table and column the crawler writes today and every word it writes in them, so the crawler's code writes what it writes today, unchanged.</step>
      <step n="3" name="the names">Every name the module shows takes recon: the executable superx-recon in its command line, its help, its messages and its errors; its pid file superx-recon.pid and its output log superx-recon.out in the module folder; daemon.rs's executable name superx-recon, a running process told by its name as ps prints it, so names_this_executable accepts superx-recon alone, the Linux cut of a long name keeping the rule it has today for any name it would cut; the Host guard's refusal, status's first line and own_hint's provisioning command name superx-recon; every tracing target "performance" becomes "recon".       <step n="4" name="the parameters">Every attr_performance_&lt;name&gt; becomes attr_recon_&lt;name&gt;: the same names after the prefix, in the same order, with the same fallbacks, the same shapes and the same reports. No parameter is added or removed. The struct params.rs reads the set values with takes a name other than `reader`.</step>
      <step n="5" name="the module folder">At run, after the module is registered as starting and before it says it is active: the module folder holds repos/, made with mode 700 when it is absent and left as it is, mode included, when it is present; and credentials.json, written when it is absent, with mode 600, holding the template, and never written when it is present, whatever it holds. The template is this JSON object, written by serde_json::to_string_pretty with a newline after it: "_doc" with the text "Read-only tokens, one per host. github.com: a fine-grained personal access token with read access to metadata, contents, pull requests, issues, actions, commit statuses, deployments and administration. bitbucket.org: an API token with the read scopes. Keep this file mode 600. superx-recon reads it, never writes it once it exists, and never shows a token."; "github.com" with the object holding "token": ""; "bitbucket.org" with the object holding "token": "". Instance gains the two paths, &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/repos and &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/credentials.json, beside the pid file and the logs.</step>
      <step n="6" name="the credentials">A module of the crate reads credentials.json, when status renders and when a part of the crawler asks for a host's token (t03). Its answer is one of: none, the file is absent; refused, the file's mode lets anyone but its owner read or write it (its mode and 0o077 is not 0), and nothing of it is read; unreadable, the file cannot be read, with the system's reason, or is not a JSON object whose every key that does not start with "_" names an object with a string "token", with serde_json's reason or the key that broke it; or the hosts, each github.com or bitbucket.org, with its token or no token when the string is empty, and every other key that does not start with "_" named as an unknown host. A key that starts with "_" is a note and is skipped. A token is held in a type whose Debug and Display print &lt;token&gt; and nothing of the token, and no log line, status text, error or test output ever holds one.</step>
      <step n="7" name="the status">status lists every table of the structure but pass and pass_source, each once, in groups, each table with the name its rows are called: sessions, models, tools, repos and census, each with the tables of today's group that the structure keeps, in today's order and with today's names; and, after repos and before census, hosts: host_repository "repositories on hosts", host_user "host accounts", host_crawl "host crawls", host_call "host calls", host_object "host objects", mirror "mirrors", pull_request "pull requests", pull_request_state "pull request states", pull_request_commit "pull request commits", ci_run "CI runs", ci_run_state "CI run states". Today's context group keeps no table, and is gone. After the groups and before the last pass, two lines: `credentials &lt;path&gt;: ` and then, when the answer of step 6 is the hosts, github.com then bitbucket.org, each `&lt;host&gt; token set` or `&lt;host&gt; no token`, then each unknown host as `&lt;host&gt; unknown`, joined by ` · `; when it is none, `none`; refused, `refused, mode &lt;its mode and 0o777, in octal&gt;; chmod 600 &lt;path&gt;`; unreadable, `unreadable: &lt;the reason&gt;`. Then `repos       &lt;path&gt;`, the repos folder. When the database is not available, the same two lines follow its line.</step>
      <step n="8" name="the words">No file of the crate calls anything a `reader`: the doc comments that do, in crawler/value.rs, crawler/lines/mod.rs, crawler/lines/facts.rs, crawler/lines/kernel.rs, crawler/lines/state.rs, crawler/lines/formats/mod.rs and crawler/lines/formats/claude_code.rs, say the crawler or what it does, their meaning unchanged.</step>
      <step n="9" name="the tests">The crate's tests move with it and take the new names. tests/schema.rs's contract becomes the structure's: every table the structure defines, with its fields and indexes, written out in the test as today, and the account superx_mod_recon. The rows and refusals the tests hold of the tables the structure no longer has, and of reply's request, leave with them; every other refusal tests/schema.rs holds today stands but one: the source hosts, which the structure makes valid; that case takes a source the structure does not list. The new rules are proven as the tests element lists them.</step>

piece
The lines task. The crawler crawls every line the kernel holds, of every agent and every kind, side files and lines that are not JSON included, in depth. Each field a kind of line carries is a line_field row saying whether a column holds it. The tables the first crawler fills gain the columns the structure added to them, and triggers join them. A reply read again steps over the lines between its own. Every pass checks the lines it read against the lines the kernel holds in the same ids, and counts the lines that fed no table. Nothing of the repositories, their mirrors or their hosts is crawled here: that is t03.
before
Merged on main when this task starts: t01 (#536), the module superx-mod-recon on the approved structure, on the operator's instance superx-recon running on superx/recon. Its crawler does what superx-performance's did. It reads the kernel's message rows and, of its telemetry, Claude Code's system lines turn_duration, compact_boundary and away_summary and its queued_command attachments. It writes session; session_place; prompt, all but prompt_id, source, permission_mode, turn_index and prompt_index; reply, all but prompt_id, client_version, skill, plugin, mcp_server, mcp_tool, refusal, refusal_text, cache_miss and cache_missed; reply_state, all but cache_write_1h_tokens and cache_write_5m_tokens; thinking; tool_call, all but skill, model and timeout_ms; tool_result, all but decision, decided_by, decision_source, decision_reason, denial, operator_feedback, sandboxed, persisted_bytes, return_note, background_task and text; edit, from the file tools alone; git_event, test_run, build_run, turn, compaction and away. And it lists the repositories. Every other table of the lines is empty.

build
module · the lines part of the crawler
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/
step · every line
n
1
Each pass reads, in the order of their ids as today and in pages as bounded as today's, every message row, its raw_text beside its raw, and every telemetry_stream row whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, transcript_raw or desktop_event, its payload whole, a side file's content included; every other telemetry row is no line and is skipped, as today. A line is read from its raw_text, parsed as JSON, wherever the kernel kept one, and from its parsed copy, a message row's raw or a payload's line, only where the kernel kept no raw_text: the kernel kept the parsed copy alone before #529, both from #529, the raw_text alone while #542 was its build, and both again from #546, and a line kept in any of these shapes goes through the same steps below. A side file and a Claude Desktop record are read from their payload's line, as today. A Gemini transcript_event of kind superseded holds an earlier write of a user or gemini record, whole, and is read as that record is read, in its place among the lines. Each agent's format knows the kinds of line the steps below read, and these, which feed no table: Claude Code's file-history-snapshot, file-history-delta, last-prompt, atis-latch, agent-name, fork-context-ref, started, launched, result, failed, artifact-autoreact-ledger, artifact-comment-monitor, api-request, api-request-blob, api-request-shape, ai-title, permission-mode, mode, cost-state, pr-link, frame-link and queue-operation lines, its system lines but turn_duration, compact_boundary and away_summary, its attachments but queued_command, and its side files; Gemini's header, $set, info, error and warning lines; every Claude Desktop record. A line of a kind no format knows, a line that is not JSON, a line the kernel names no agent for, and a line in which a format finds a shape it does not read, as today, is counted unread.
step · the fields
n
2
Every field of every line that is JSON is counted by its path. Each (agent, kind, path) is one line_field row the first time a line shows it, first_at the line's at, first_line its kernel id, stored whether a column holds it; and one row more each time stored changes for it. The crawler holds the newest row of each, read from its database at its first pass, and writes one only when it differs. A line's kind is its type; for a Claude Code system line its type, a slash and its subtype, and for an attachment its type, a slash and its attachment's type; where the line holds no type, the kind the kernel gave it (a Gemini header or $set, a line kept by its kind alone before #471). A path is the keys from the line's top to the field, joined by a dot. A list's items take the list's path with [] after it. A block of a message's content list is named by its type in brackets, message.content[text]; a tool call by its name, message.content[tool_use:Bash]; a tool result by the name of the call it answers, message.content[tool_result:Bash], with ? where the crawler holds no such call; a line's toolUseResult the same way, toolUseResult[Bash]. A key is written <k> when it is data: a key that is not a name, a name being made of ASCII letters, digits, _, $ and - and beginning with a letter, _ or $; a key that is a UUID; and every key of a map the agent's format names as keyed by data, for Claude Code modelUsage, snapshot.trackedFileBackups, artifacts, wireToolInputs and wireIngestContext, the answers and the annotations of an AskUserQuestion result, and the files of an Artifact call's input. Each agent's format declares, per kind, the paths whose values its reading puts in a column, a path whose value a column holds whole standing for itself and every path under it; those paths are stored, and every other is not.
step · the operator's prompts
n
3
A prompt is read as today, and takes the columns the structure added: prompt_id its promptId, source its promptSource, permission_mode its permissionMode, turn_index and prompt_index its turnPosition.turnIndex and turnPosition.promptIndex, each NONE where the line has none.
step · the triggers
n
4
A line that is no prompt as today's prompts are read is a trigger row when the first of these holds. A user line that is no tool result and whose origin.kind is set and is not human: kind its origin.kind. A queued_command attachment whose origin.kind is set and is not human: kind its origin.kind; one with no origin whose commandMode is set and is not prompt: kind its commandMode. A user line marked isCompactSummary: kind compact_summary. A user line that is no tool result, marked isSidechain, with no parentUuid and no origin, the instruction an agent gave its subagent: kind subagent. source: its origin.from, else its origin.producer, else its origin.name, else NONE. prompt_id: its promptId. text: whole, a user line's string content or its text blocks joined by a newline, a queued command's prompt or its text parts joined by a newline. chars: the characters of text. Any other user line that is no tool result and no prompt feeds no table.
step · the reply
n
5
A reply takes the columns the structure added, from the line it is written from: prompt_id, the newest promptId of the session's lines read before it, a session first read since the crawler began taking its newest from its prompt and trigger rows; client_version its version; skill, plugin, mcp_server and mcp_tool its attributionSkill, attributionPlugin, attributionMcpServer and attributionMcpTool; refusal and refusal_text its message.stop_details.category and message.stop_details.explanation where message.stop_details.type is refusal; cache_miss and cache_missed its message.diagnostics.cache_miss_reason.type and cache_missed_input_tokens. A reply_state takes cache_write_1h_tokens and cache_write_5m_tokens from its usage's cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens and ephemeral_5m_input_tokens. A Claude Code line whose model is <synthetic> stays no reply, as today.
step · the reply read again
n
6
A reply read again from its middle (state.rs hold) reads the session's lines from the reply's first line to the line being read, that one included, keeps those of the reply and steps over every other, a subagent's lines among them; it no longer stops at the first line that is not the reply's.
step · calls and results
n
7
A tool call takes skill (a Skill call's input.skill, a Gemini activate_skill call's args.name), model (an Agent call's input.model) and timeout_ms (its input.timeout). A result takes, from its line, when the line carries that result alone, as today's sandbox hosts are read: decision, decided_by, decision_source and decision_reason from permissionDecision.decision, reasonType, source and reasonCode; denial from toolDenialKind; operator_feedback from userFeedback; sandboxed from sandboxed. From its toolUseResult, persisted_bytes, return_note and background_task: its persistedOutputSize, returnCodeInterpretation and backgroundTaskId. text: what the tool returned, as text as today's results read it, when its outcome is not ok. A shell command's result that ended ok, whose toolUseResult carries a bashEditDiff not marked shared, is an edit row per entry of its files: path its filePath; kind created where it is marked created, deleted where it is marked deleted, else modified; added and removed the lines of its hunks that begin with + and with -; counted_from shell; operator_changed false. A diff marked shared saw files change while other commands ran too, so the call alone did not change them, and it is no edit row.
step · the check
n
8
For every page it reads, the pass counts with count() the rows the kernel holds over the same ids, the page's first to its last: message rows on a page of them, and on a page of the telemetry stream the rows whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, transcript_raw or desktop_event. Their sum is the source's lines_held; items stays the lines it read. When they differ the source's outcome is partial, its note naming both. pass_source.counts gains lines_held; lines_unused, the lines the pass read that fed no table; fields_seen, the fields (agent, kind, path) its lines showed; fields_new, those it wrote a first line_field row for; and fields_not_stored, those of fields_seen that no column holds. status prints the lines source's line as `<items> read of <lines_held> held, <lines_unused> fed no table, <unread> in a shape it does not know · <fields_seen> fields, <fields_new> new, <fields_not_stored> not stored · <rows> rows`, its outcome after it as today; every other source's line stays as it is.

Every line is a fixture of the test's own in the shapes this file names, never a copy of a real line. A format's own rules are tested in its tests module; what crosses the crawler is tested in tests/crawler.rs, a pass run on the kernel and its rows read back. One test per item below, named by the item it proves; no item without its test; no test outside this list and the tests t01 left: a rule the list lacks is a stop, not a test.

agent-decides
The names and the decomposition of the private functions and types inside the crawler and its tests; the means of each step within the standard library and the workspace's dependencies; the contents of the test fixtures within the items the tests element lists; the granularity of the commits within the root's commits step. Nothing else: no table, column, kind, path rule, count name or status word other than this file's and the structure's; no new dependency; no number; no default; no test the list lacks. What is not here and not in the root, the rules, the authority files or the merged code is a stop (root method silence).
gates
Local gates run in the checkout, from the repository root. The live gates run after the operator has deployed the branch's head with every step of the root's method deploy, the provision included: the structure is unchanged, but the rows t01's build wrote lack what this task derives, and no row is ever changed, so the database is rebuilt and every line crawled again (rules derived-and-rebuilt). The first pass then reads every line the kernel holds, which takes as long as it takes. The agent asks for the deploy with every value filled, waits for the operator's word, and runs the live gates' commands only.

  <start>Pointed at this file, you are told nothing else. This line must be the first message of a session opened for this build, in a new session, on a checkout of the head of main updated from origin, with no other branch checked out and no worktree of an earlier build checked out in the build folder; if your session holds anything before it beyond what rules.xml kickoff allows the harness (its own text, the repository's CLAUDE.md, its settings, a command that set your model or effort, with its output), such as a conversation, the writing of this spec or another build, stop here, say so, and build nothing, unless the operator named this session to build this task (rules.xml kickoff), and then build on the operator's word. Your label is your own, &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;: your model's name and the effort you run at, as your session reports them, lowercase, dots and spaces as hyphens; when your session does not report your effort, ask the operator for it before you cut the branch. Then run python3 tools/skill_audit.py on the head of main and compare what it names with what CI's Bright-line skill rules check names on that head: one fewer is a file the audit did not read, since it reads none on a path through a folder named worktrees, and one more is a file git does not hold; either is a stop: say so and build nothing (gate G3). What this file, the root, the rules and the merged code do not say is a stop, not a guess.</start>

  <piece>The lines task. The crawler crawls every line the kernel holds, of every agent and every kind, side files and lines that are not JSON included, in depth. Each field a kind of line carries is a line_field row saying whether a column holds it. The tables the first crawler fills gain the columns the structure added to them, and triggers join them. A reply read again steps over the lines between its own. Every pass checks the lines it read against the lines the kernel holds in the same ids, and counts the lines that fed no table. Nothing of the repositories, their mirrors or their hosts is crawled here: that is t03.</piece>

  <before>Merged on main when this task starts: t01 (#536), the module superx-mod-recon on the approved structure, on the operator's instance superx-recon running on superx/recon. Its crawler does what superx-performance's did. It reads the kernel's message rows and, of its telemetry, Claude Code's system lines turn_duration, compact_boundary and away_summary and its queued_command attachments. It writes session; session_place; prompt, all but prompt_id, source, permission_mode, turn_index and prompt_index; reply, all but prompt_id, client_version, skill, plugin, mcp_server, mcp_tool, refusal, refusal_text, cache_miss and cache_missed; reply_state, all but cache_write_1h_tokens and cache_write_5m_tokens; thinking; tool_call, all but skill, model and timeout_ms; tool_result, all but decision, decided_by, decision_source, decision_reason, denial, operator_feedback, sandboxed, persisted_bytes, return_note, background_task and text; edit, from the file tools alone; git_event, test_run, build_run, turn, compaction and away. And it lists the repositories. Every other table of the lines is empty.</before>

  <after>Who uses what this task builds: t03 (#538), which follows the sessions' places and git events to the repositories and their hosts, and ties commits to sessions; t04 (#539), the tab, which reads every table this task fills.</after>

    <code path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/gemini_cli.rs">What a Gemini CLI line is in the kernel: a user or gemini record, a message row; a header, a $set, an info, error or warning line, and an earlier write of a record (kind superseded), a transcript_event; a line of a shape it does not know, a transcript_raw whose payload holds its line.</code>
    <code path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_desktop.rs">What a Claude Desktop record is in the kernel: a desktop_event whose payload holds the record's text as line, and file.</code>
    <code path="crates/superx-kernel/src/message.rs">message rows: raw, and raw_text.</code>
  </reads>

  <command>
    <form>superx-recon run, as t01 left it: the crawler's passes, one every attr_recon_read_secs.</form>
    <runs>Its own process, as today. After the provision of this task's deploy, the first pass crawls every line the kernel holds.</runs>
  </command>

  <build>
    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/" name="the lines part of the crawler">
      <step n="1" name="every line">Each pass reads, in the order of their ids as today and in pages as bounded as today's, every message row, its raw_text beside its raw, and every telemetry_stream row whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, transcript_raw or desktop_event, its payload whole, a side file's content included; every other telemetry row is no line and is skipped, as today. A line is read from its raw_text, parsed as JSON, wherever the kernel kept one, and from its parsed copy, a message row's raw or a payload's line, only where the kernel kept no raw_text: the kernel kept the parsed copy alone before #529, both from #529, the raw_text alone while #542 was its build, and both again from #546, and a line kept in any of these shapes goes through the same steps below. A side file and a Claude Desktop record are read from their payload's line, as today. A Gemini transcript_event of kind superseded holds an earlier write of a user or gemini record, whole, and is read as that record is read, in its place among the lines. Each agent's format knows the kinds of line the steps below read, and these, which feed no table: Claude Code's file-history-snapshot, file-history-delta, last-prompt, atis-latch, agent-name, fork-context-ref, started, launched, result, failed, artifact-autoreact-ledger, artifact-comment-monitor, api-request, api-request-blob, api-request-shape, ai-title, permission-mode, mode, cost-state, pr-link, frame-link and queue-operation lines, its system lines but turn_duration, compact_boundary and away_summary, its attachments but queued_command, and its side files; Gemini's header, $set, info, error and warning lines; every Claude Desktop record. A line of a kind no format knows, a line that is not JSON, a line the kernel names no agent for, and a line in which a format finds a shape it does not read, as today, is counted unread.</step>
      <step n="2" name="the fields">Every field of every line that is JSON is counted by its path. Each (agent, kind, path) is one line_field row the first time a line shows it, first_at the line's at, first_line its kernel id, stored whether a column holds it; and one row more each time stored changes for it. The crawler holds the newest row of each, read from its database at its first pass, and writes one only when it differs. A line's kind is its type; for a Claude Code system line its type, a slash and its subtype, and for an attachment its type, a slash and its attachment's type; where the line holds no type, the kind the kernel gave it (a Gemini header or $set, a line kept by its kind alone before #471). A path is the keys from the line's top to the field, joined by a dot. A list's items take the list's path with [] after it. A block of a message's content list is named by its type in brackets, message.content[text]; a tool call by its name, message.content[tool_use:Bash]; a tool result by the name of the call it answers, message.content[tool_result:Bash], with ? where the crawler holds no such call; a line's toolUseResult the same way, toolUseResult[Bash]. A key is written &lt;k&gt; when it is data: a key that is not a name, a name being made of ASCII letters, digits, _, $ and - and beginning with a letter, _ or $; a key that is a UUID; and every key of a map the agent's format names as keyed by data, for Claude Code modelUsage, snapshot.trackedFileBackups, artifacts, wireToolInputs and wireIngestContext, the answers and the annotations of an AskUserQuestion result, and the files of an Artifact call's input. Each agent's format declares, per kind, the paths whose values its reading puts in a column, a path whose value a column holds whole standing for itself and every path under it; those paths are stored, and every other is not.</step>
      <step n="3" name="the operator's prompts">A prompt is read as today, and takes the columns the structure added: prompt_id its promptId, source its promptSource, permission_mode its permissionMode, turn_index and prompt_index its turnPosition.turnIndex and turnPosition.promptIndex, each NONE where the line has none.</step>
      <step n="4" name="the triggers">A line that is no prompt as today's prompts are read is a trigger row when the first of these holds. A user line that is no tool result and whose origin.kind is set and is not human: kind its origin.kind. A queued_command attachment whose origin.kind is set and is not human: kind its origin.kind; one with no origin whose commandMode is set and is not prompt: kind its commandMode. A user line marked isCompactSummary: kind compact_summary. A user line that is no tool result, marked isSidechain, with no parentUuid and no origin, the instruction an agent gave its subagent: kind subagent. source: its origin.from, else its origin.producer, else its origin.name, else NONE. prompt_id: its promptId. text: whole, a user line's string content or its text blocks joined by a newline, a queued command's prompt or its text parts joined by a newline. chars: the characters of text. Any other user line that is no tool result and no prompt feeds no table.</step>
      <step n="5" name="the reply">A reply takes the columns the structure added, from the line it is written from: prompt_id, the newest promptId of the session's lines read before it, a session first read since the crawler began taking its newest from its prompt and trigger rows; client_version its version; skill, plugin, mcp_server and mcp_tool its attributionSkill, attributionPlugin, attributionMcpServer and attributionMcpTool; refusal and refusal_text its message.stop_details.category and message.stop_details.explanation where message.stop_details.type is refusal; cache_miss and cache_missed its message.diagnostics.cache_miss_reason.type and cache_missed_input_tokens. A reply_state takes cache_write_1h_tokens and cache_write_5m_tokens from its usage's cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens and ephemeral_5m_input_tokens. A Claude Code line whose model is &lt;synthetic&gt; stays no reply, as today.</step>
      <step n="6" name="the reply read again">A reply read again from its middle (state.rs hold) reads the session's lines from the reply's first line to the line being read, that one included, keeps those of the reply and steps over every other, a subagent's lines among them; it no longer stops at the first line that is not the reply's.</step>
      <step n="7" name="calls and results">A tool call takes skill (a Skill call's input.skill, a Gemini activate_skill call's args.name), model (an Agent call's input.model) and timeout_ms (its input.timeout). A result takes, from its line, when the line carries that result alone, as today's sandbox hosts are read: decision, decided_by, decision_source and decision_reason from permissionDecision.decision, reasonType, source and reasonCode; denial from toolDenialKind; operator_feedback from userFeedback; sandboxed from sandboxed. From its toolUseResult, persisted_bytes, return_note and background_task: its persistedOutputSize, returnCodeInterpretation and backgroundTaskId. text: what the tool returned, as text as today's results read it, when its outcome is not ok. A shell command's result that ended ok, whose toolUseResult carries a bashEditDiff not marked shared, is an edit row per entry of its files: path its filePath; kind created where it is marked created, deleted where it is marked deleted, else modified; added and removed the lines of its hunks that begin with + and with -; counted_from shell; operator_changed false. A diff marked shared saw files change while other commands ran too, so the call alone did not change them, and it is no edit row.</step>
      <step n="8" name="the check">For every page it reads, the pass counts with count() the rows the kernel holds over the same ids, the page's first to its last: message rows on a page of them, and on a page of the telemetry stream the rows whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, transcript_raw or desktop_event. Their sum is the source's lines_held; items stays the lines it read. When they differ the source's outcome is partial, its note naming both. pass_source.counts gains lines_held; lines_unused, the lines the pass read that fed no table; fields_seen, the fields (agent, kind, path) its lines showed; fields_new, those it wrote a first line_field row for; and fields_not_stored, those of fields_seen that no column holds. status prints the lines source's line as `&lt;items&gt; read of &lt;lines_held&gt; held, &lt;lines_unused&gt; fed no table, &lt;unread&gt; in a shape it does not know · &lt;fields_seen&gt; fields, &lt;fields_new&gt; new, &lt;fields_not_stored&gt; not stored · &lt;rows&gt; rows`, its outcome after it as today; every other source's line stays as it is.</step>
    </module>

    <tests path="crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/">
      <how>cargo test, on SurrealDB's in-memory engine, the kernel's schema and the structure applied as the merged tests apply them. Lines are written into the kernel as each adapter writes them: message rows with raw and raw_text, telemetry rows with their payloads; and, where an item says so, in the earlier shapes step 1 names. Every line is a fixture of the test's own in the shapes this file names, never a copy of a real line. A format's own rules are tested in its tests module; what crosses the crawler is tested in tests/crawler.rs, a pass run on the kernel and its rows read back. One test per item below, named by the item it proves; no item without its test; no test outside this list and the tests t01 left: a rule the list lacks is a stop, not a test.</how>
      <prove step="1">a pass reads every message row and every transcript_event, transcript_raw and desktop_event row in id order, a side file's content included, and no other telemetry row</prove>
      <prove step="1">a line kept as its raw_text alone, as its parsed copy alone and as both is read through the same steps into the same rows, for a message row and a transcript_event of each agent; where it is kept as both and the two differ, its rows hold the raw_text's values</prove>
      <prove step="1">a Gemini superseded write is read as the record it holds, in its place among the lines</prove>
      <prove step="1">a line of each kind step 1 names as feeding no table is read and counted read; a line of a kind no format knows, a line that is not JSON and a line the kernel names no agent for are counted unread</prove>
      <prove step="2">a path names content blocks by their type, a tool call and its result and toolUseResult by the tool's name, a list's items by [], and a result whose call the crawler does not hold by ?</prove>
      <prove step="2">a key that is not a name, a UUID, and each key of each map step 2 names is written &lt;k&gt;, and a name elsewhere is not</prove>
      <prove step="2">a field is a line_field row the first time its agent, kind and path are seen and never again while stored stands, and again when stored changes</prove>
      <prove step="2">every path a format declares stored lands in a column: a line of each kind the format reads, each declared path holding a value of its own, each value found in a column of the rows that line fed</prove>
      <prove step="3">a prompt takes its prompt_id, source, permission_mode, turn_index and prompt_index, each NONE where absent</prove>
      <prove step="4">a task notification, a peer, a coordinator and an auto-continuation on a user line, a queued command marked by its origin and one marked by its commandMode alone, a compact summary and a subagent's first line are each a trigger with its kind, source and text; a meta line with no origin, a line of a shell command's output and a queued command whose commandMode is prompt are none</prove>
      <prove step="5">a reply takes its prompt_id, client_version, skill, plugin, mcp_server, mcp_tool, refusal, refusal_text, cache_miss and cache_missed, and a reply_state its cache writes by duration; a reply of a session first read since the crawler began takes its prompt_id from the session's newest prompt or trigger row</prove>
      <prove step="6">a reply read again from its middle, with a subagent's lines between its own, counts each of its lines once and places its blocks as when read in one pass</prove>
      <prove step="7">a call takes skill, model and timeout_ms; a result takes the permission decision's four columns, its denial, operator_feedback, sandboxed, persisted_bytes, return_note and background_task, and, when it did not end ok, its text</prove>
      <prove step="7">a shell command's bashEditDiff is one edit per file, created, deleted or modified, its lines counted from its hunks, counted_from shell; a diff marked shared, and one of a command that did not end ok, are none</prove>
      <prove step="8">a pass whose pages' counts equal the lines it read is complete; one that read fewer is partial, its note naming both</prove>
      <prove step="8">pass_source.counts holds lines_held, lines_unused, fields_seen, fields_new and fields_not_stored, and status prints them on the lines source's line as step 8 words them</prove>
    </tests>
  </build>

  <forbid>crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, the structure, which no task writes; crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/repos/**, the repositories' part, which is t03's; crates/superx-kernel/**; crates/superx/**; crates/superx-ops/**; every other crate; schema/**; SUPERX_SCHEMA.md; BLUEPRINT.md; specs/**; tools/**; .github/**; .claude/**. Nothing on the operator's instance is touched by the agent: the deploy is the operator's (root method deploy).</forbid>

  <done>On the branch: the lines part of the crawler crawling every line in depth, every table of the lines filled from the shapes this file names, the reply read again across the lines between its own, every pass checked against the kernel; G1 to G7 green, their evidence in the PR as the root method pr lists it, the PR body naming the root's version and this file's version. piece
The repositories task. The crawler crawls in depth every repository the lines lead to, and the hosts those repositories live on. Each repository: its remotes; its commits, each read once whole with its files, its time zones, its patch id and its signature; every reflog entry git keeps; the commits no ref reaches; where each commit sits, what of it landed through another commit, and what of it the main line's tip still holds; what each checkout is in the middle of; every git call. Each repository on a host: its mirror clone in the repos folder, fetched with the credentials, every ref the host advertises kept and never pruned; every answer of the host's read APIs kept whole in host_object, and beside it its accounts, its pull requests with their states and commits, and its CI runs with their states. Every commit a session printed or put through a pull request tied to that session. Every pass checks the commits it read against what git holds, and what it read of a host against the totals the host states. The structure is unchanged: every table this task fills is in it.
before
Merged on main when this task starts: t01 (#536), the module superx-mod-recon on the approved structure, and t02 (#537), the lines crawled in depth. The crawler's lines source fills every table of the lines, git_event among them, each pass checked against the kernel. Its repos source lists the repositories: from every working directory the lines name (session_place) and every root attr_recon_include names, the directory, checkout and repository rows, and each checkout's state (branch, first_branch, present), every attr_recon_crawl_secs for all of them and at every pass for the new ones and those the pass's lines were written in; its git calls are counted in its record and are no rows. Nothing writes the tables of the commits, the reflogs, the remotes or the hosts, and no source called hosts exists. The module folder holds the repos folder, empty, and the credentials file, which credentials.rs reads; status lists every table, the hosts group among them, and says by host whether the credentials file gives a token. On the operator's instance superx-recon runs on superx/recon, provisioned at t02's deploy.
after
Who uses what this task builds: t04 (#539), the tab, which reads every table t02 and this task fill.
