build
module · the repositories' part of the crawler
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/repos/
step · which repositories, and their workers
n
1
The list is read as t01 left it, with step 12. Then the pass crawls repositories in depth: at a full pass (every attr_recon_crawl_secs, as today) every repository on the list; at any other pass, each repository new to the list in this pass, and each repository one of whose checkouts holds the dir of a git_event the lines source wrote in this pass (its valid_from at or after the pass began) of the action commit, push, merge, rebase, revert, cherry_pick, reset, branch_deleted, pr_created or pr_merged. A repository the operator excludes (attr_recon_exclude) is never crawled. A repository with no present checkout is crawled through its common git directory while that exists; one whose common git directory is gone is not crawled, and what the crawler holds of it stays. Each repository crawled has a worker of its own, as many at once as attr_recon_repos_at_once allows (step 24), started in the order of the repositories' common directories. A worker runs steps 3 to 11 in their order, step 4 starting the host crawl of each host repository it takes; then it finishes those host crawls (steps 15 to 21); then step 13; and it holds every row they make; when they are done it writes them, the crawl row first and then each row after the rows it links, and nothing of another repository. The pass waits for every worker before it records itself. A stop ends every worker where it is: the call it waits on is killed, and a worker stopped writes nothing.
step · git, read-only
n
2
Every git call of a crawl runs as t01's calls run (repos/git.rs): one git process per call, never a shell; the signature check and the fsmonitor overridden; no diff driver and no external diff where a subcommand takes the flags; no optional lock; killed at attr_recon_git_timeout_ms. In the operator's repositories nothing is written, fetched or checked out, no format that verifies a signature is used (git's %G placeholders), and no program a repository names runs. Every call of a crawl is a git_call row: crawl; step, the word the step below gives it (setup, fetch, refs, unreachable, log, patches, reflog, placement, landed, tip, blame; tags, merges and checkouts are not used); dir; args, exactly as run, the overrides included; started_at; ms; exit, NONE when it was cut off or could not start; timed_out; bytes_out and lines_out, what it printed; stderr, what git wrote to its error output, whole, NONE when it wrote nothing. The list's calls (t01's) stay counted in the source's record and are no rows, since no crawl holds them.
step · the remotes
n
3
A crawl first reads its repository's configuration (step setup): every remote's url and pushurl, user.email, and whether git says the repository is shallow. A remote is a remote_state row (repository, crawl, name, url, push_url, NONE when it has no pushurl, host, path) when one of url, push_url, host and path differs from the newest row of the same repository and name; a remote of a newest row whose url is not NONE and that the configuration no longer holds is one row more with url, push_url, host and path NONE. host and path come from the url, in its forms https://[user@]host[:port]/path, ssh://[user@]host[:port]/path and the scp-like [user@]host:path: the host lowercased, the path lowercased without a trailing .git or slash; a local path, a file:// url and a url of no such form have neither. crawl.identity is the user.email the configuration gives there, NONE when it gives none. A shallow repository's crawl says shallow in its note, and its commits are those git holds.
step · the hosts' repositories and the mirrors
n
4
Then, for each remote, in the order of their names, whose host is github.com or bitbucket.org and which has a path: its host_repository (host, path), made the first time a crawl reaches it, found_at then, host_id the host's own id for it (GitHub's id, Bitbucket's uuid) from the repository object that host crawl reads, NONE when it reads none; no second row is ever made for it. A host repository is crawled at most once a pass, by the first worker to reach it; a worker that reaches one already taken in the pass goes on without it and uses what is kept of it. The worker that takes it starts its host crawl (step 21): when the credentials file (credentials.rs), read afresh at each pass, gives the host a token, with the repository object, its first call (step 17, step 18), and, when the host answers it, with the mirror: a bare repository of the crawler's own at <repos folder>/<host>/<path>.git, made with git init --bare and no template when its folder is absent, its mirror row (host_repository, url https://<host>/<path>.git, folder, created_at) written when the database holds none for it, then fetched from its url (step fetch). Every git call on a mirror has on its command line and in its environment: every ref the host advertises taken as it is (the refspec +refs/*:refs/*, the fetch's only one); nothing pruned (fetch.prune and fetch.pruneTags false, no --prune); no maintenance and nothing collected (gc.auto 0, maintenance.auto false, --no-auto-maintenance); every ref's moves logged (core.logAllRefUpdates always) and never expired (gc.reflogExpire and gc.reflogExpireUnreachable never); no hook (core.hooksPath /dev/null); no redirect followed (http.followRedirects false); no submodule (--no-recurse-submodules); the operator's configuration unread (GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL /dev/null, GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM 1) and no credential helper (credential.helper empty); no prompt (GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT 0); the username credential.username, x-access-token for github.com (GitHub uses no username to authenticate, and needs one that is not empty) and x-bitbucket-api-token-auth for bitbucket.org; the password from superx-recon itself, which GIT_ASKPASS names (step 23), with SUPERX_RECON_ASKPASS the host and SUPERX_HOME the instance's home in its environment; no token in any argument, variable, file or url. A fetch alone is not cut off at attr_recon_git_timeout_ms, since a first fetch takes what the repository's size makes it: git ends one whose transfer moves less than a byte a second for a minute (http.lowSpeedLimit 1, http.lowSpeedTime sixty). No mirror is made or fetched when the credentials file gives the host no token or the host does not answer its repository.
step · the refs and the main line
n
5
Every ref the repository holds (branches, remote-tracking refs, tags, notes, the stash, any other) and every ref of the mirrors of its remotes is read with the commit it points at, a tag's peeled to its commit (step refs); crawl.refs_read counts them. Against the refs the crawler read at its previous crawl of the same repository since superx-recon started, a ref is new; moved, when it points at another commit; rewritten, when it moved and its old commit is not an ancestor of its new one (a rebase, an amend, a force-push); or gone: refs_new, refs_moved, refs_rewritten (each also counted moved) and refs_gone; at the repository's first crawl since superx-recon started they are 0, and the crawl's note says the refs were compared with none. The main line is the first of these that resolves: override, the ref attr_recon_mainline_refs names for the repository's name, as git resolves it there; host_default, the default branch the newest repository object kept of the host repository its remote origin names says (GitHub's default_branch, Bitbucket's mainbranch.name), as refs/heads/<branch> in that mirror when the mirror holds it, else as refs/remotes/origin/<branch>; origin_head, the ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD points at; local_branch, refs/heads/main, else refs/heads/master; head, HEAD. crawl.mainline is that ref's full name and mainline_from its word, HEAD and head when none resolves; tip is the commit it points at, NONE when none resolves; a crawl with no tip places no commit on the main line, blames nothing, and says so in its note.
step · the unreachable commits
n
6
The commits git still holds in the repository that neither a ref nor a reflog reaches, as git fsck --unreachable names them (step unreachable), the first attr_recon_unreachable_commits of them in the order fsck names them, join the commits step 7 reads; crawl.unreachable_read counts those fsck named. A mirror is not asked: nothing in it is ever collected.
step · the commits
n
7
The repository's commits are every commit its refs and reflogs reach and every commit the refs and reflogs of its mirrors reach (step log), with those of step 6, each once, committed at or after the crawl's since: the time attr_recon_since_days before the crawl began, since NONE and all of history when it is 0. commits_held is how many they are. Each one the repository holds no commit row for is read once, whole, from the repository's git directory when that holds it, else from the mirror that does (step log): hash; tree; parent_hashes, first parent first, and parents, their count; author and committer, the emails, and author_name and committer_name; authored_at and committed_at, git's times; author_offset and committer_offset, the offsets git recorded beside them in minutes east of UTC; subject, the message's first line as written; body, what follows that line, the empty lines that open it removed, whole, trailers included, NONE when nothing follows; signature, the commit object's gpgsig header whole, its lines joined by newlines without the space that opens each line after the first, NONE when it has none, never verified; patch_id, git patch-id --stable of the commit's change against its first parent (step patches), NONE for a merge and for a commit that changes nothing. Its files are commit_file rows: every path its change against its first parent (a root commit's against the empty tree) touches, with no rename detection, so a rename is a delete and an add; change added, modified, deleted or type_changed as git's A, M, D and T say; added and removed as git's numstat counts them; added_ignoring_space and removed_ignoring_space, the same with --ignore-all-space; binary when git counts no lines in it, its counts 0. A commit's added and removed sum its files. crawl.commits_read counts the commits of commits_held the repository holds rows for once the crawl is done; commits_new, commit_files_new, lines_added_new and lines_removed_new what this crawl wrote; files_read the file changes it read.
step · the reflogs
n
8
Every reflog entry git keeps is one ref_move row: the repository's, each ref's under its common git directory, each checkout's HEAD, the main one's and each worktree's own (checkout, that checkout), the stash's; and each mirror's (mirror, that mirror), read by the worker that took the mirror's host repository in the pass and by no other. An entry is read from its reflog file as git writes it, one line each: the old and the new commit, the identity's name and <email>, the time in seconds and its offset, then a tab and the message. ref is the ref's full name, HEAD for a checkout's; at, the time; old and new, the commits, NONE for git's zero id; identity, the email; action, the message whole; crawl, this crawl; checkout and mirror as said, NONE otherwise. A repository whose refs git keeps in a reftable (as git rev-parse --show-ref-format says) is read with git reflog show instead (step reflog), its old NONE. An entry the table holds is not written again: the crawler reads what the table holds of the repository before it writes, since the engine holds no unique index where a field is NONE. crawl.reflog_read counts the entries read, head_moves_new the checkouts' HEAD entries this crawl wrote. An entry git has expired since stays as written.
step · the placements
n
9
Every commit the repository holds, committed in the crawl's since, has one place at each crawl (step placement): mainline when the main line's tip reaches it; else in_flight when a branch under refs/heads/ that a present checkout of the repository has checked out reaches it; else branch when any other ref of the repository or of its mirrors reaches it; else recovered; whether a ref reaches a commit is read in the git directory that holds the ref. first_parent is true when it is on the main line's first-parent history. A placement row (commit, crawl, place, first_parent) is written when the commit has none or its newest differs; crawl.placements_changed counts them.
step · what landed
n
10
For each commit placed branch, in_flight or recovered, and each path its change against its first parent added or modified, the version it wrote is the blob it gives the path. When a commit of the main line's first-parent history gave the same path the same blob in its own change against its first parent, one landed_via row (commit, path, via, crawl) is written, via the oldest such commit (step landed): the file's work landed by squash or by replay though the commit did not. A row the table holds is not written again; crawl.landed_new counts the new ones.
step · what survives
n
11
When the main line's tip differs from the tip the crawler last blamed whole for the repository since superx-recon started, every file of the tip's tree (step tip) is blamed at the tip (step blame), in the git directory that holds the tip, attr_recon_blames_at_once at once, but a file whose newest change on the main line git counted no lines in (a binary commit_file), a file attr_recon_survival_skip names, and the files past the first attr_recon_blame_files in the tree's order, which crawl.files_left counts. A commit's alive is the lines blame credits it, never more than its added; lines credited to a commit the repository holds no row for are counted in blame_lines_read and make no row. A survival row (commit, crawl, alive) is written for each commit whose alive differs from its newest survival row, and with alive 0 for a commit blame no longer credits whose newest row is above 0. crawl.files_blamed, blame_lines_read, survival_changed and survival_delta, the sum over the new rows of alive less the newest earlier alive, record it. When a blame fails or is cut off, the crawl writes no survival row, its survival_changed and survival_delta are 0, and the tip is not blamed whole, so the next crawl blames it again; the crawl's note names each file whose blame failed, in git's words. A commit's survival is as of its newest survival row's crawl, that crawl's tip and time; a commit with no survival row is not known, never 0.
step · the checkouts' operations
n
12
Where the list reads a present checkout's branch, it also reads what git is in the middle of there, from the state files of the checkout's own git directory, never through a command that touches the checkout: operations, in this order, each that is there of merge (MERGE_HEAD), am (rebase-apply/applying), rebase (rebase-apply without applying, or rebase-merge without interactive), rebase_interactive (rebase-merge/interactive), cherry_pick (CHERRY_PICK_HEAD), revert (REVERT_HEAD) and bisect (BISECT_LOG), empty when none; operation_commits, the commits MERGE_HEAD, CHERRY_PICK_HEAD and REVERT_HEAD name, in that order, NONE when none; rebase_branch, the branch the rebase's head-name names without refs/heads/; rebase_onto, its onto; rebase_done and rebase_left, the commands in rebase-merge/done and in rebase-merge/git-rebase-todo, a line that is empty or starts with # being none; bisect_start, what BISECT_START holds; each NONE where its file is not there. A checkout not present has operations NONE. A checkout_state row is written when one of these, branch, first_branch or present differs from the newest.
step · commits tied to sessions
n
13
Last, a crawl ties commits to sessions. printed: each git_event the database holds, of the action commit, merge, revert or cherry_pick, with a hash, whose dir a checkout of the repository holds, ties the one commit of the repository whose hash begins with it (none when no commit or more than one does) to the session of its tool call's reply, with tool_call that call and at the event's at. pull_request: each git_event of the action pr_created or pr_merged whose url is the url of a pull request of a host repository the repository's remotes name, or, when its url is NONE and a checkout of the repository holds its dir, whose pr_number is the number of one, ties each commit of the repository whose hash begins with a hash that pull request has listed (pull_request_commit) or with its newest merge_sha, to the event's session, with pull_request that pull request, tool_call and at as above. Each (commit, session, via) is one commit_session row, written once.
step · the crawl and the repos source's record
n
14
Each repository crawled is one crawl row: repository, started_at, finished_at, mainline, mainline_from, identity, tip, since; outcome complete; partial when a git call failed or was cut off, or the repository's commits_read differs from its commits_held; unreadable, every count 0, mainline HEAD and mainline_from head, when its git directory is there and git cannot read it; every count of steps 5 to 11; checkouts_read, the repository's checkouts; git_calls, git_failed, git_timeouts and git_ms of its git calls; bytes_read, the bytes they printed; rows_written; note, what could not be read in git's words, what steps 3, 5 and 11 say, and each difference. The repos source's pass_source lists its crawls (crawls); its calls, failed and timed_out count the list's calls and the crawls' git calls; its counts keep t01's and gain commits_held and commits_read, summed over its crawls. It is partial when a crawl is partial or unreadable, its note naming each repository and, for a difference, both numbers.
module · the hosts' part of the crawler
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/hosts/
step · the calls
n
15
Every call to a host is a GET, through reqwest with rustls (decision http-client), at most one at a time per host across every worker, cut off at attr_recon_host_timeout_ms (step 24). GitHub's go to https://api.github.com with Accept application/vnd.github+json, X-GitHub-Api-Version 2022-11-28 (the version whose pull requests hold merge_commit_sha, which 2026-03-10 removed; it is served until 2028-03-10), User-Agent superx-recon and Authorization Bearer and the github.com token; Bitbucket's to https://api.bitbucket.org/2.0 with Accept application/json, User-Agent superx-recon and Authorization Bearer and the bitbucket.org token. These addresses and headers, the page sizes and the minute below are the hosts' own, constants with an audited skill-allow marker, never parameters; a test points the crawler at a server of its own on the loopback, and a mirror at a repository of its own, through the crawler's arguments, never through a parameter or the environment. A token goes to its own host's address alone, never into a path or a query. A list is paged as the host gives it: GitHub's by the URL its link header names rel="next", Bitbucket's by its answer's next, each followed as given until there is none; a list's first page as the crawler asks it sets per_page a hundred on GitHub and pagelen fifty on Bitbucket, the most each list of theirs takes. A redirect is followed when it keeps the scheme, the host and the port, the token with it; any other is not followed, and the call fails. When the newest answer to the same path, query included, gave an etag in a host crawl that ended complete, the etag is sent back as If-None-Match, and an answer HTTP 304 is not_modified: what that path held is unchanged, and nothing of it is read again; after a host crawl that did not end complete, its paths are asked again whole. A page of a list answered HTTP 304 is followed by the page the newest earlier answer to it named next, asked with that page's own etag. The rate limit is read from each answer: GitHub's x-ratelimit-limit, x-ratelimit-remaining and x-ratelimit-reset (UTC epoch seconds), Bitbucket's X-RateLimit-Limit, and retry-after in seconds. A call answered HTTP 429, or HTTP 403 with x-ratelimit-remaining 0, a retry-after, or a message that says a rate limit was exceeded, is refused for the rate: the crawler waits retry-after seconds when the answer gives them, else until x-ratelimit-reset when x-ratelimit-remaining is 0, else one minute, and asks again; a call made while the host's newest answer said x-ratelimit-remaining 0 waits until its reset first; each wait is counted, and a stop ends it. An answer HTTP 400, HTTP 403 or HTTP 404 to a call after the host repository's own (steps 17 and 18) that is not refused for the rate is the host's answer: what the host gives this token for that path, such as settings only an admin may read, or the commits of a pull request whose branch is gone. It holds no object and is no failure; its call is kept as every call is, and its host crawl's note names its path and status (step 21). Every path is asked at every host crawl whatever the token can read, so a token given more scopes reads more at the next host crawl, with nothing rebuilt or restarted. Every call is a host_call row: host_crawl; method GET; path, the path and query asked, never the host and never a token; started_at; ms; status, NONE when no answer came; not_modified; bytes, the answer's body; etag; rate_limit, rate_remaining, rate_reset; retry_after_ms; objects, the objects its answer held; next, whether a page followed; error, NONE for an answer HTTP 304 and for an answer HTTP 200 that is read, else what went wrong, whole: the transport's error; for an answer HTTP 200 that is unread (step 16), `an answer not of the shape asked: ` and the answer's body; else the status and the answer's body.
step · the objects
n
16
An answer's objects are the items of a GitHub array, the items of the one list a GitHub answer wraps beside its total_count (workflow_runs, jobs, environments, check_suites, check_runs), the values of a Bitbucket page, or the answer itself when it is one object. Each is a host_object of the kind its call gives (steps 17 and 18), with the key given there; its hash the SHA-256, in lowercase hexadecimal, of the object as serde_json writes a Value, compact, every object's keys in their sorted order; a row (host_repository, kind, key, object whole, hash, call, at, when the answer came) when the host repository holds none of that kind, key and hash: host_crawl.objects_new counts those of a key it held none of, objects_changed the others. Every account an object holds at any depth is a host_object of the kind user, its key the account's id: on GitHub an object with login, id and type; on Bitbucket an object with uuid and the type user, team or app_user. The first time a host names an account it is a host_user row: host; host_id, GitHub's id or Bitbucket's uuid; login, GitHub's login or Bitbucket's nickname; name, GitHub's name or Bitbucket's display_name; kind, its type as written; found_at; each NONE where the object has none. An answer that is not JSON, or not of the shape its call gives, is unread: counted in the source's unread, its call failed, its error as step 15 gives it.
step · GitHub
n
17
A host repository on github.com, path <owner>/<repo>, is asked, in this order, each under /repos/<path>: itself, kind repository, key id, an answer HTTP 401, HTTP 403 or HTTP 404 making the host crawl unreadable with nothing more asked; /pulls?state=all&sort=created&direction=asc, whose objects are not kept, since each pull request is kept whole as its own answer, /pulls/<number>, kind pull_request, key id, asked when no pull_request object of that id is kept or the list's updated_at differs from the newest kept one's, and with it /pulls/<number>/commits, kind pull_request_commit, key sha, /pulls/<number>/files, kind pull_request_file, key <number>/<filename>, /pulls/<number>/reviews, kind review, key id, and its timeline; /issues?state=all&sort=created&direction=asc, kind issue, key id, a pull request being an issue there too, and for each issue object new or changed, and each pull request asked, /issues/<number>/timeline, kind timeline_event, key its id, else a committed event's sha, else its own hash; /issues/comments?sort=created&direction=asc, kind issue_comment, key id; /pulls/comments?sort=created&direction=asc, kind review_comment, key id; /issues/events, kind issue_event, key id; /actions/runs, kind workflow_run, key id, and for each run new or changed /actions/runs/<id>/jobs?filter=all, kind workflow_job, key id; /commits/<sha>/statuses, kind commit_status, key id, for the head commit of every open pull request and the main line's tip at every host crawl, for every other commit a pull request has listed and every commit of the main line's first-parent history at the first host crawl that knows it, and again at each host crawl while a status it holds is pending; /commits/<sha>/check-suites, kind check_suite, key id, and then /commits/<sha>/check-runs?filter=all, kind check_run, key id, for the same commits at the same host crawls as the statuses, and again at each host crawl while a suite or run it holds is not completed; /deployments, kind deployment, key id, and for every deployment /deployments/<id>/statuses, kind deployment_status, key id; /environments, kind environment, key id; /releases, kind release, key id; /rulesets, whose objects are not kept, and /rulesets/<id>, kind ruleset, key id, asked when none of that id is kept or the list's updated_at differs; /branches/<the repository's default_branch>/protection, kind branch_protection, key the branch's name, which GitHub answers HTTP 404, the host's answer (step 15), when the branch is not protected and when the token is not an admin of the repository. A token that cannot call GitHub's Checks API (a fine-grained token cannot; GitHub names it among the gaps of fine-grained tokens) is given the host's answer for the check suites and runs, and they are asked again at the next host crawl.
step · Bitbucket
n
18
A host repository on bitbucket.org, path <workspace>/<repo>, is asked, in this order, each under /repositories/<path>: itself, kind repository, key uuid, an answer HTTP 401, HTTP 403 or HTTP 404 making the host crawl unreadable with nothing more asked; /pullrequests?state=OPEN&state=MERGED&state=DECLINED&state=SUPERSEDED&sort=created_on, whose objects are not kept, and /pullrequests/<id>, kind pull_request, key id, asked when no pull_request object of that id is kept or the list's updated_on differs from the newest kept one's, and with it /pullrequests/<id>/commits, kind pull_request_commit, key hash, /pullrequests/<id>/diffstat, which the host redirects within itself, kind pull_request_file, key <id>/<its new path, else its old>, /pullrequests/<id>/comments, kind pull_request_comment, key id, /pullrequests/<id>/activity, kind pull_request_activity, key its own hash, and /pullrequests/<id>/tasks, kind pull_request_task, key id; /commit/<hash>/statuses, kind commit_status, key <hash>/<its key>, for the same commits at the same crawls as on GitHub, a status pending while its state is INPROGRESS; /pipelines?sort=-created_on, kind pipeline, key uuid, and for each pipeline new or changed /pipelines/<uuid>/steps, kind pipeline_step, key uuid, a uuid's braces written %7B and %7D in a path; /deployments, kind deployment, key uuid; /environments, kind environment, key uuid; /branch-restrictions, kind branch_restriction, key id; /default-reviewers, kind default_reviewer, key uuid. Issues are not asked: Bitbucket removed its issue tracker and its API on 2026-08-20.
step · the pull requests
n
19
A pull_request object makes its pull_request row the first time (host_repository, number): number, GitHub's number or Bitbucket's id; host_id, GitHub's id or Bitbucket's id; author, the host_user of GitHub's user or of Bitbucket's author; created_at, GitHub's created_at or Bitbucket's created_on; source_branch and target_branch, GitHub's head.ref and base.ref or Bitbucket's source.branch.name and destination.branch.name; source_path, the repository its source branch lives in (GitHub's head.repo.full_name, Bitbucket's source.repository.full_name, lowercased) when that is not the host repository, else NONE; url, GitHub's html_url or Bitbucket's links.html.href; object. Each pull_request object read also makes a pull_request_state row when one of these differs from the newest: state as written; merged, GitHub's merged, Bitbucket's state MERGED; draft; title; head_sha and base_sha, GitHub's head.sha and base.sha, Bitbucket's source.commit.hash and destination.commit.hash; merge_sha, GitHub's merge_commit_sha when merged is true (before a merge it names a trial merge), Bitbucket's merge_commit.hash; merged_at, GitHub's, NONE on Bitbucket; merged_by, GitHub's merged_by, Bitbucket's closed_by when the state is MERGED; closed_at, GitHub's, NONE on Bitbucket; closed_by, on GitHub the actor of the newest closed event of its timeline as kept, on Bitbucket its closed_by; reason, Bitbucket's; additions, deletions, changed_files, commits, comments and review_comments, GitHub's own counts, and on Bitbucket comment_count as comments, the others NONE; updated_at, GitHub's updated_at or Bitbucket's updated_on; object, the object it was read from. Each reading of a pull request's commits whose every page was answered HTTP 200 or HTTP 304 makes pull_request_commit rows, and one with a page that failed or that the host gave its answer to (step 15) makes none: a commit listed for the first time, present true and position its place in the list from 0; a commit listed before and absent now, present false, position NONE; a commit absent before and listed again, present true; object, its pull_request_commit object. GitHub lists two hundred and fifty commits of a pull request at most: when its own count of commits is more than its list gave, no commit is marked absent in that reading.
step · the CI runs
n
20
A check_suite, check_run, workflow_run, workflow_job or commit_status object of GitHub, and a pipeline, pipeline_step or commit_status object of Bitbucket, makes its ci_run row the first time (host_repository, kind, host_id), kind the object's kind: a check suite, host_id its id, name its app's name, commit_sha its head_sha, ref its head_branch, pull_request the first of its pull_requests whose number is a pull request of the host repository, url its url, created_at its created_at; a check run, host_id its id, name its name, commit_sha its head_sha, parent its suite's ci_run by check_suite.id, pull_request as a suite's, url its html_url; a workflow run, host_id its id, name its name, commit_sha its head_sha, ref its head_branch, pull_request the first of its pull_requests whose number is a pull request of the host repository, url its html_url, created_at its created_at; a workflow job, host_id its id, name its name, commit_sha its head_sha, ref its head_branch, parent its run's ci_run by run_id, url its html_url, created_at its created_at; a GitHub commit status, host_id its id, name its context, commit_sha the commit asked, url its target_url, created_at its created_at; a pipeline, host_id its uuid, name its target.selector.pattern, commit_sha its target.commit.hash, ref its target.ref_name, pull_request the pull request its target.pullrequest.id names where its target names one, created_at its created_on; a pipeline step, host_id its uuid, name its name where it has one, parent its pipeline's ci_run, commit_sha and ref its pipeline's; a Bitbucket commit status, host_id <hash>/<its key>, name its name, commit_sha the commit asked, ref its refname, url its url, created_at its created_on; object; every other column NONE. Each such object read also makes a ci_run_state row when one of these differs from the newest: status and conclusion, a check suite's and check run's, a GitHub run's and job's status and conclusion, a commit status's state as status and conclusion NONE, a pipeline's and a step's state.name and state.result.name; started_at, a check run's started_at, a run's run_started_at, a job's started_at, a step's started_on; completed_at, a check run's completed_at, a job's completed_at, a pipeline's and a step's completed_on; attempt, a run's and a job's run_attempt; number, a run's run_number, a pipeline's build_number; actor, the host_user of a run's triggering_actor or a pipeline's creator; runner, a job's runner_name; each NONE where the object has none; and object, the object it was read from, which alone never makes a row.
step · the host crawl and the hosts source's record
n
21
Each host repository a worker crawls is one host_crawl row, written after the crawl row it ran beside and before the rows that point to it: host_repository; crawl; started_at, finished_at; calls, not_modified, failed, rate_waits, waited_ms, objects_new, objects_changed, rows_written; outcome no_credentials, nothing asked, when the credentials file gives the host no token, its note naming why (none, refused or unreadable as credentials.rs answers, or no token); unreadable as steps 17 and 18 say; partial when a call failed or was cut off, or a list's stated total differs from what was read of it, a host's answer (step 15) being no failure; complete otherwise; note, each path that failed with its status, each path the host gave its answer to with its status, and each difference. A list states its total in GitHub's total_count beside workflow_runs, jobs, environments, check_suites and check_runs, and in Bitbucket's size where a page gives it; when every page of it was answered HTTP 200 in this crawl, the objects read on it are compared with that total. The hosts source is a pass_source row of its own, source hosts: items, the host repositories crawled; unread; crawls empty; calls, failed and timed_out, its host calls; rows; counts objects_new, objects_changed, not_modified, rate_waits, waited_ms, totals_stated and totals_read, the totals compared and the objects read on those lists; outcome partial when a host crawl is partial, its note naming each and its difference; a host crawl unreadable or without credentials leaves the source complete and is named in its note.
module · the crate
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/src/
step · the pass
n
22
A pass runs the lines source first, as today; then the repos source with its workers, each crawling beside its repository the host repositories it takes; then it records itself, a pass_source row each for lines, repos and hosts, its outcome partial when one of them is, as today. The pass's log line also names hosts_items and host_calls.
step · the askpass
n
23
superx-recon started with SUPERX_RECON_ASKPASS in its environment and one argument is git's askpass: it reads no command, starts nothing and logs nothing, and answers the prompt git gives as that argument. When the prompt asks for the password of https://<host> or https://<user>@<host>, that host is the one SUPERX_RECON_ASKPASS names and is github.com or bitbucket.org, and the credentials file of the home SUPERX_HOME names gives that host a token (credentials.rs), it prints the token and a newline to its standard output and exits 0; for any other prompt, or with no token, it prints nothing there, says why on its error output without a token, and exits 1.
step · the parameters
n
24
Two parameters join the module's, after today's, in this order: attr_recon_repos_at_once, the repositories crawled at once, a whole number, 1 or more, its fallback no limit, shown all; attr_recon_host_timeout_ms, the milliseconds a host call may take before it is cut off, a whole number, 1 or more, its fallback the fallback of attr_recon_git_timeout_ms. Each is reported by name when it is set to a value of the wrong shape, as today's are.
step · the status
n
25
In status's last pass, the repos source's line is `<items> read, <unread> in a shape it does not know · <commits_read> commits read of <commits_held> held · <calls> calls, <failed> failed, <timed_out> cut off · <rows> rows` and the hosts source's `<items> read, <unread> in a shape it does not know · <totals_read> objects read of <totals_stated> stated · <calls> calls, <failed> failed, <timed_out> cut off, <rate_waits> waits · <rows> rows`, each with its outcome after it as today; the lines source's line stays as t02 left it.
step · the dependencies
n
26
crates/superx-mod-recon/Cargo.toml gains reqwest 0.13, its default features off and its feature rustls on, and sha2 0.10, the version the workspace's lock holds; Cargo.lock follows. No other dependency.
tests
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/repos/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/hosts/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/
how
cargo test, on SurrealDB's in-memory engine, the kernel's schema and the structure applied as the merged tests apply them. The fixtures are the test's own: repositories made with git in a scratch folder, their authors, committers, times and offsets set by the test, their remotes urls of github.com and bitbucket.org it makes up; a mirror fetched from a bare repository of the test's own in the scratch folder in place of the host's url; hosts answered by a server of the test's own on the loopback, in the shapes steps 15 to 21 name, which records every request it is sent; sessions, tool calls and git events written into the module's database as the lines source writes them; a token the test makes up, searched for in every output, row, log line, argument and environment; a wait the test's server asks for is a second at most, and the minute a refusal without retry-after or reset waits is proven as the wait the crawler computes, never waited. A part's own rules are tested in its tests module; what crosses the crawler is tested under tests/, a pass run and its rows read back. One test per item below, named by the item it proves; no item without its test; no test outside this list and the tests t01 and t02 left. Those stand; one whose expectation a step of this file changes (the parameters of step 24, the sources' lines of step 25, a pass's three sources, a checkout state's operations) is changed to hold this file's, and keeps its name. A rule the list lacks is a stop, not a test.

agent-decides
The names and the decomposition of the private functions and types inside the crawler, its hosts part and their tests; the means of each step within the standard library, the workspace's dependencies and the two step 26 adds, the git commands and their flags included where a step names what they give; the contents of the test fixtures within the items the tests element lists; the granularity of the commits within the root's commits step. Nothing else: no table, column, kind, key, word, count name, status word or parameter other than this file's and the structure's; no path asked of a host other than steps 17 and 18 give; no dependency other than step 26's; no number but the hosts' own this file gives; no default; no test the list lacks. What is not here and not in the root, the rules, the authority files or the merged code is a stop (root method silence).
gates
Local gates run in the checkout, from the repository root. The live gates run after the operator has deployed the branch's head with the root's method deploy steps build, install and restart: the structure is unchanged and no row already written lacks what this task derives, so nothing is provisioned. Before the deploy the operator writes, in <home>/modules/recon/credentials.json, a fine-grained read-only token for github.com, and for bitbucket.org an API token with the read scopes where a repository's remote names it (decision credentials-file, and the file's own note). The first pass after the restart crawls every repository on the list and every host it leads to in depth, for as long as git and the hosts' rate limits make it. The agent asks for the deploy with every value filled, waits for the operator's word, and runs the live gates' commands only.

owns
crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/repos/**; crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/hosts/**; crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/mod.rs, crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/store.rs and crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/value.rs, where a step needs them; crates/superx-mod-recon/src/main.rs, the askpass of step 23 and what passes the instance to the crawler; crates/superx-mod-recon/src/params.rs, step 24; crates/superx-mod-recon/src/status.rs, step 25 and the read of its counts; crates/superx-mod-recon/Cargo.toml and Cargo.lock, step 26; crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/**, the tests of this file's list.
forbid
crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, the structure, which no task writes; crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/**, the lines' part, which is t02's; crates/superx-kernel/**; crates/superx/**; crates/superx-ops/**; every other crate; schema/**; SUPERX_SCHEMA.md; BLUEPRINT.md; specs/**; tools/**; .github/**; .claude/**. Nothing on the operator's instance is touched by the agent: the deploy and the credentials file are the operator's (root method deploy).
done
On the branch: the repositories crawled in depth, each with its remotes, commits, files, reflog entries, placements, landings, survival and ties to sessions, and its checkouts' operations; every repository on a host with its mirror and every answer of its host kept whole, its accounts, pull requests and CI runs typed beside them; every pass checked against git and against the hosts' stated totals; G1 to G8 green, their evidence in the PR as the root method pr lists it, the PR body naming the root's version and this file's version. After this task the operator's instance holds what became of the agents' work in every repository they worked in, and on every host those repositories live on.

  <start>Pointed at this file, you are told nothing else. Your label is your own, &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;: your model's name and the effort you run at, as your session reports them, lowercase, dots and spaces as hyphens; when your session does not report your effort, ask the operator for it before you cut the branch.   <piece>The repositories task. The crawler crawls in depth every repository the lines lead to, and the hosts those repositories live on. Each repository: its remotes; its commits, each read once whole with its files, its time zones, its patch id and its signature; every reflog entry git keeps; the commits no ref reaches; where each commit sits, what of it landed through another commit, and what of it the main line's tip still holds; what each checkout is in the middle of; every git call. Each repository on a host: its mirror clone in the repos folder, fetched with the credentials, every ref the host advertises kept and never pruned; every answer of the host's read APIs kept whole in host_object, and beside it its accounts, its pull requests with their states and commits, and its CI runs with their states. Every commit a session printed or put through a pull request tied to that session. Every pass checks the commits it read against what git holds, and what it read of a host against the totals the host states. The structure is unchanged: every table this task fills is in it.</piece>

  <before>Merged on main when this task starts: t01 (#536), the module superx-mod-recon on the approved structure, and t02 (#537), the lines crawled in depth. The crawler's lines source fills every table of the lines, git_event among them, each pass checked against the kernel. Its repos source lists the repositories: from every working directory the lines name (session_place) and every root attr_recon_include names, the directory, checkout and repository rows, and each checkout's state (branch, first_branch, present), every attr_recon_crawl_secs for all of them and at every pass for the new ones and those the pass's lines were written in; its git calls are counted in its record and are no rows. Nothing writes the tables of the commits, the reflogs, the remotes or the hosts, and no source called hosts exists. The module folder holds the repos folder, empty, and the credentials file, which credentials.rs reads; status lists every table, the hosts group among them, and says by host whether the credentials file gives a token. On the operator's instance superx-recon runs on superx/recon, provisioned at t02's deploy.</before>

  <after>Who uses what this task builds: t04 (#539), the tab, which reads every table t02 and this task fill.</after>
