step · the routes
n
16
server.rs serves, beside today's routes and behind the same Host check, two routes, each answering from the watcher's snapshot and never reading the kernel's database: GET /api/watch, the snapshot as JSON (step 14); and GET /api/watch/memory?session=<a session's id>&file=<a file's name>, that file of that session's memory folder, read at the request as a file, read-only, and answered whole as text/plain; charset=utf-8. It refuses with HTTP 400 a file that is empty, holds a slash or a backslash, or is . or .., and a session that is not a UUID; with HTTP 404 a session no watch is held for, one with no memory folder, and a file the folder's newest listing does not hold; each refusal is a JSON object whose error says why, as today's refusals are. A route that refuses writes nothing and reads nothing else.
step · the process
n
17
superx-recon run starts the watcher once the page listens, as a task of its own beside the crawler's, with the kernel's connection, the instance and the same stop the crawler is given, so one stop ends both; the page's routes are given the watcher's snapshot. At the stop the process waits for the watcher as it waits for the crawler, and logs a watcher that did not end cleanly as it logs the crawler. Nothing else of run changes.
module · the page
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/ui/
step · the Watcher tab
n
18
ui/src/App.tsx: the page's tabs are Crawler, Results and Watcher, in that order; #watcher opens the Watcher; on it the header's trail reads SuperX › Recon › Watcher, Recon leading to #crawler as on the other tabs, and the browser's title is Watcher · Recon · SuperX. The subtitle under Recon reads: The crawler, and what it gathers: the models, the agents and the repositories they worked in, measured over time; and the watcher, every session as it works. The Watcher's panel and its parts are in ui/src/watcher/.
step · the sessions
n
19
The Watcher asks /api/watch every two seconds, as the Crawler's Now panel asks /api/now, and shows at its top when the watcher last looked, its interval, paused when it is, the window's start, the lines read and the rows not read by why and agent; then one card per session, those with an alert among their reports first, then those with a warn, then the others, each group by its newest line, newest first. A card shows: its name, agent, model and folder; its state as a badge; what it is doing now; its newest words; your newest message with its class and marks; the orders in force, or that none is known; the run since your newest message; its jobs running; its recap; the task file it executes with its version, the version it held when the session was first held to it, its owns and its forbid, or why none; its memory files (step 20); its reports, newest first. Times are in the operator's own zone, as ui/src/time.ts writes them. A long text is shown clamped and opens whole on a click.
step · the reports and the memory
n
20
Beside the cards, every report, newest first: its time, its level as a badge, its kind in words (stop not honoured for stop_not_honoured, and so on), the session's name, what it says, and its words, clamped, opening whole. A card's memory lists each file with its description and when it last changed; a file opens in a modal that holds it whole, as /api/watch/memory answers it.
step · notify me
n
21
A switch, Notify me, kept in the browser's localStorage, each read and write in a try/catch, off when it cannot be read. When it is turned on the page asks the browser's permission (Notification.requestPermission). While it is on and the permission granted, each report of level warn or alert that an answer of /api/watch holds for the first time since the page opened or the switch was turned on raises one browser notification: its title the kind in words and the session's name, its body what the report says. A report the page already held raises none. When the browser refuses or has no notifications, the switch says so.
step · the parameters and the build
n
22
ui/src/panels/Params.tsx lists the group watches as Watches, after Measures, and types the input pairs: one pair per line, the pattern, an equals sign and its words separated by commas, the first equals sign of a line splitting it; none for no pair; its hint says so. ui/src/api.ts gains the snapshot's types and the two fetches. No dependency joins: package.json and package-lock.json are as merged. The page is built with npm ci and npm run build in crates/superx-mod-recon/ui, and ui/dist and ui/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo are committed as the build writes them (gate G5).
tests
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/watcher/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/
how
cargo test, on SurrealDB's in-memory engine, the kernel's schema and the structure applied as the merged tests apply them. The fixtures are the test's own: sessions registered as capture registers them, as tests/common's Capture does; their lines written into the kernel's tables as the adapter writes them, a user or assistant line a message row with raw_text beside raw, every other line a transcript_event with its payload's session, kind, file, line and raw_text, at the times the test gives; the projects roots set on the adapter's registry entity as the operator sets them, never read from the environment's HOME; task files, checkouts, transcripts and memory folders made by the test in a scratch folder. No test reads the operator's instance, transcripts, memory folders or task files (root method tests). A look is given the time it judges at, and no test waits for a threshold. A part's own rules are tested in its tests module; what crosses the watcher, its routes and the page's bundle is tested under tests/watcher.rs, its looks driven and its snapshot read back. One test per item below, named by the item it proves; no item without its test; no test outside this list and the tests main holds when the branch is cut. Those stand; one whose expectation a step of this file changes (the parameters of step 15, the routes' arguments of step 16) is changed to hold this file's, and keeps its name. Gate G6 proves step 17 on the operator's instance, the operator's review of the deployed page proves the page's steps 19 to 21, and gate G5 proves step 22's build. A rule the list lacks is a stop, not a test.

agent-decides
The names and the decomposition of the private functions and types inside src/watcher/ and ui/src/watcher/ and their tests; the field names of the snapshot's JSON and of the page's types, within what steps 14 and 16 list; the wording of each report's sentence, within the facts its step names; the means of each step within the standard library and the workspace's dependencies, and on the page within its dependencies and the browser's own APIs; the layout of the cards and the reports within what steps 19 and 20 list; the contents of the test fixtures within the items the tests element lists; the granularity of the commits within the root's commits step. Nothing else: no table, row or file written; no route, report kind, level, class, state, input or parameter other than this file's; no dependency; no number but the fallbacks of step 15, the page's two seconds, and the page sizes, the overlap and the pause's minute, each a constant with an audited skill-allow marker; no default; no test the list lacks. What is not here and not in the root, the rules, the authority files or the merged code is a stop (root method silence).
gates
Local gates run in the checkout, from the repository root. The live gates run after the operator has deployed the branch's head with the root's method deploy steps build, install and restart: the structure is unchanged and the watcher writes nothing, so nothing is provisioned. <url> is the URL superx-recon --home <home> status prints on its url line. The agent asks for the deploy with every value filled and, for G7, for one message of the operator's in the build session that holds a word of attr_recon_status_words; it waits for the operator's word, and runs the live gates' commands only.

done
On the branch: the watcher, the module's second part, sharing nothing that runs with the crawler; it follows every session's newest lines, holds one watch per session in memory, hears the operator's words and keeps the orders in force, holds a build session to its task file, finds each session's memory, and reports as it happens every way an agent vanishes, departs from the operator's orders or its task file, or heads for trouble; the Watcher tab shows every session, what it is doing this second and every report, and notifies in the browser; every threshold and word list is a parameter; G1 to G8 green, their evidence in the PR as the root method pr lists it, the PR body naming the root's version and this file's version. After this task the operator learns of an agent that vanished, improvised or ignored them while it happens, not an hour later.
specs/recon/tasks/566.xml, the XML as written
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<!--
  SuperX Recon, build spec version 0: task t05, the watcher. File version 1 (2026-11-04).
  An agent reads this file in the order the root's method read gives.
  Its GitHub issue is #566, rendered from this file by specs/recon/build_issues.py.
  The history of this file is its git log.
-->
<task id="t05" name="watcher" issue="566" version="1" date="2026-11-04" depends="t04"
      reflects="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs"
      command="superx-recon run"
      builds="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/watcher/ crates/superx-mod-recon/ui/src/watcher/ crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/watcher.rs">

  <start>Pointed at this file, you are told nothing else. This line must be the first message of a session opened for this build, in a new session, on a checkout of the head of main updated from origin, with no other branch checked out and no worktree of an earlier build checked out in the build folder; if your session holds anything before it beyond what rules.xml kickoff allows the harness (its own text, the repository's CLAUDE.md, its settings, a command that set your model or effort, with its output), such as a conversation, the writing of this spec or another build, stop here, say so, and build nothing, unless the operator named this session to build this task (rules.xml kickoff), and then build on the operator's word. Your label is your own, &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;: your model's name and the effort you run at, as your session reports them, lowercase, dots and spaces as hyphens; when your session does not report your effort, ask the operator for it before you cut the branch. Read whole, in the order the root's method read gives. Then run python3 tools/skill_audit.py on the head of main and compare what it names with what CI's Bright-line skill rules check names on that head: one fewer is a file the audit did not read, since it reads none on a path through a folder named worktrees, and one more is a file git does not hold; either is a stop: say so and build nothing (gate G3). Cut the branch feat/566-watcher-v1-&lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt; from the head of main. Every commit message starts with #566 and carries no attribution trailer. Build what this file says and run its local gates; ask the operator for the deploy its live gates need (root method deploy, this file's gates note) and run them on the operator's word; push the branch and open the PR into main with gh: the title "#566 watcher v1 · &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;", the body the root's method pr step lists, naming the root's version and this file's version attribute. What this file, the root, the rules and the merged code do not say is a stop, not a guess.</start>

  <piece>The watcher task. The module's second part, beside the crawler and sharing nothing that runs with it (decision watcher): its own loop, every attr_recon_watch_secs, follows the newest captured lines of every session and holds one watch per session in memory. It hears the operator's words, orders, approvals, questions, status asks, stops, corrections and escalations, and keeps the orders in force; it judges every act of the agent against them and, for a session building from a task file, against that file: what it owns, what it forbids, its version and its words. It finds each session's memory folder. It reports, as it happens, an agent that vanished (quiet, behind a job, stalled, a question unanswered, a status asked, waiting on the operator), one that does not follow (it acted on a question, an unordered action, unordered work, a stop not honoured, a repeated order) and trouble ahead (a long estimate, a long run, struggling, memory written, a compaction, frustration). The Watcher tab of the module's page shows every session, what it is doing this second, its words and the operator's, the orders in force, its memory and every report, and notifies in the browser. It writes nothing: no row, no file, no parameter; the structure is unchanged.</piece>

  <before>Merged on main when this task starts: t01 (#536), t02 (#537), t03 (#538) and t04 (#539): the module superx-mod-recon, its crawler crawling the lines, the repositories and the hosts into the structure, and its page on the module's own URL with the tabs Crawler and Results; and #562 (PR #563): the page's header with its trail of breadcrumbs, SuperX, Recon and the tab, its first crumb the core dashboard's URL that /api/ping gives. server.rs serves the page and its routes under /api/ and answers only a request that names it (its Host check); the Crawler tab lists and sets the parameters by group (when, where, how much, listens, measures) through /api/params, as params.rs's about gives them; superx-recon run starts the crawler beside the page and waits for it at the stop. No part of the module watches a session: nothing under src/watcher/ or ui/src/watcher/ exists, and no parameter attr_recon_watch_*, attr_recon_stop_words, attr_recon_status_words, attr_recon_question_words or attr_recon_yes_words. The kernel captures every Claude Code line whole (crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs): a user or assistant line as a message row, its raw_text beside its parsed copy since #546; every other line as a transcript_event, its payload holding session, kind, file, line and raw_text; a file beside the transcripts as a transcript_event of kind file. The projects roots the adapter captures from are the parameter attr_claude_code_projects_root on adapter_claude_code. On the operator's instance superx-recon runs on superx/recon.</before>

  <after>Who uses what this task builds: the operator, on the Watcher tab, while the sessions run. Keeping the reports in recon's database, so the Results can chart them over time, is a change to the structure, designed with the operator and approved per change (rules the-structure-decides); notifications outside the browser, and Gemini's lines, come after.</after>

  <command>

  <build>
    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/watcher/" name="the watcher">
      <step n="1" name="its own part">The watcher is a part of the module of its own, src/watcher/, beside the crawler, sharing nothing that runs with it (rules crawler-is-the-reader): no file under src/watcher/ names crate::crawler or crate::results, and no file under src/crawler/ or src/results/ names crate::watcher. It reads the kernel's database with queries of its own, SELECT alone (step 3; rules kernel-read-only), reads the task files and the memory folders as files, read-only (steps 11 and 13), keeps what it judges in memory, and writes nothing: no row in any database, no file, no parameter, no registry entry. What it has from the rest of the module is what every part has: the kernel's connection the process opened, the instance, and the parameters as params.rs resolves them (step 15). It judges Claude Code's lines, the agent the kernel names claude_code (AGENT_NAME of the adapter); another agent's lines are counted (step 3) and judged nowhere.</step>
      <step n="2" name="its loop">superx-recon run starts the watcher beside the crawler (step 17). It looks every attr_recon_watch_secs seconds. Each look resolves the parameters and the projects roots (step 13); reads the lines captured since the last look (step 3); gives each line to its session's watch, in the order of their ids (steps 4 to 13); judges every watch at the look's time for what time alone makes (steps 8 and 10); takes from each watch what the window leaves behind (step 5); reads again each task file whose modification time changed (step 11); lists each memory folder (step 13); and publishes what it holds as its snapshot (step 14), which the routes answer from without waiting on a look. While attr_recon_watch_secs is 0 it reads nothing and holds what it held, and looks at the parameter again every minute, a constant with an audited skill-allow marker; set again, it reads every line captured since its last look. A look that cannot read, because the kernel's database or a parameter does not answer, changes nothing the watcher holds and is logged at warn with the error, once until a look reads again; the next look tries again from where the last look that read stood (rules capture-first). A stop ends the watcher where it is, in its wait or in a look, and nothing it holds outlives the process. It logs at the module's log target, recon: that it started, with its window; a look that could not read; that it stopped. A look judges at the clock's time, and can be given the time it judges at, so a test judges at a time of its own and waits for nothing.</step>
      <step n="3" name="the lines it follows">At its start the watcher reads back the window: every line captured from attr_recon_watch_window_secs before its start on, so it never starts blind; then each look reads every line captured since. A line is a row the kernel captured: in message, a conversation line, with its id, session, agent, raw_text and raw; in telemetry_stream, a row whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, with its id, agent and payload. Each table is read a page of rows at a time, in the order of their ids, the UUIDv7 the kernel set when it captured the line, with SELECT … FROM type::record('&lt;table&gt;', $from&gt;..) LIMIT $n, never through a time or a datetime index; a page of telemetry_stream reads its rows' ids and whether each is a transcript_event, then those rows whole by their ids, since most of that stream is not a line. The page sizes are constants with audited skill-allow markers. Each look starts a minute before the newest id it has read in each table, a constant with an audited skill-allow marker, and skips every id it has read already, so a line the kernel committed after a newer one is read, and every line is read once. The lines of both tables are given to the watches in the order of their ids. A line is its raw_text parsed as JSON; a row that holds no raw_text is read from the parsed copy the kernel kept, a message's raw or a payload's line. A row's agent is the name the kernel gives its agent entity; its session is the session entity a message row names, or, for a telemetry row, the session the kernel names &lt;agent&gt;/&lt;key&gt;, the key being the payload's session; both come from kernel.list_named_entities (node_agent with attr_agent_descriptor, node_session with attr_session_descriptor), read again when a row names an agent or a session not yet known, at most once a look. A transcript_event of kind file is a file beside the transcripts, not a line, and is skipped. A session's transcript is the file the payload of its newest transcript_event names. A look counts the lines it read and, apart, by why, the rows it could not read: no line, when a row holds neither raw_text nor a parsed copy or its raw_text is not JSON; no session, when the names give none; another agent, by the agent's name. These are counted and shown (step 14), never guessed at (rules nothing-dropped). A line's time is the timestamp its agent wrote in it, else the time its id holds.</step>
      <step n="4" name="what a line says">Of a Claude Code line the watcher reads, as the adapter captured it (crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs): from a user line (type user), each tool_result block of its content, the result of the call its tool_use_id names: failed when its is_error is true, and then its error, the block's content as text, whole (a string, or its text parts joined by newlines); and the lines it wrote: in the line's toolUseResult, the lines of its structuredPatch hunks that start with + and not with +++, else, for a toolUseResult of type create, the lines of its content. The line's text, its content when that is a string, else its text blocks joined by newlines, when it is not empty: when the line's origin.kind is task-notification, the end of a background job, the call its &lt;tool-use-id&gt; names, ending as its &lt;status&gt; says, each the text between its tags, trimmed; else, unless the line is isSidechain, isMeta or isCompactSummary, the operator's message: an interrupt when it starts with [Request interrupted by user; a shell command when it starts with &lt;bash-input&gt;; a slash command when it starts with &lt;command-name&gt;; else typed, when its origin.kind is human, or it has no origin and does not start with &lt;; any other text is no one's message. From an attachment line (type attachment) whose attachment.type is queued_command, the operator's message typed while the agent worked, its text the attachment's prompt (a string, or its parts' text joined by newlines): queued when attachment.origin.kind is human, a shell or slash command by its start as above; a shell command when it has no origin and starts with &lt;bash-input&gt;; any other is no one's. A subagent's lines are captured into the session that ran it, marked isSidechain. From an assistant line (type assistant), its model, message.model, unless it starts with &lt; or the line is isSidechain; each text block that is not empty, unless the line is isSidechain, the agent's words to the operator; each tool_use block, a call: its id; its tool, the block's name; what it was called on, the first its input holds of command, file_path, notebook_path, pattern, url, query, description and prompt, else the input whole as JSON; its input whole; background, when the input's run_in_background is true or the tool is Monitor; and the paths it writes: an Edit, Write, MultiEdit or NotebookEdit call's file_path or notebook_path; and a Bash call's targets: the word after each &gt; or &gt;&gt;, one that starts with &amp; aside; each word after a tee to the end of its command, the next |, ;, &amp;&amp;, || or newline, that does not start with -; and for a sed with an option that starts with -i, each word of its command after the first that does not start with -, the script, that does not start with -. A command's words are split at white space, their quotes set aside; a target is read as an absolute path, with the environment's HOME for a ~ that starts it, or joined to the line's folder. From a system line (type system) that is not isSidechain: a turn end, subtype turn_duration; a compaction, compact_boundary; the agent's own recap, away_summary, its content whole. A line's folder is the cwd it names. Every other line says nothing to the watcher. Every text is kept whole, as written.</step>
      <step n="5" name="one watch per session">Each session the lines name has one watch, in memory. It holds, of its session's lines in the window, what step 4 reads from them, whole and in their order, and what steps 6 to 13 make of them: its agent and its name, &lt;agent&gt;/&lt;key&gt;; its model, the newest; its folder, the newest a line names; the time of its newest line; its transcript (step 3); the operator's messages with their classes and marks; the agent's words; each call with its result; each background job running, a background call whose end no task notification has given; its turn, ended from a turn end until the next operator's message, task notification or assistant line, working otherwise; its orders in force; the task file it is held to; its memory folder; its reports. The window is the span the watcher watches: a line captured more than attr_recon_watch_window_secs before the look leaves its watch, with what was made of it, its reports included, but for the orders in force and the task file the session is held to, which stay while the watch holds any line (steps 7 and 11); a watch that holds no line leaves the watcher. Every line stays in the kernel, and the crawler crawls them all. When more watches are held than attr_recon_watch_sessions allows, those whose newest line is oldest leave first; its fallback is no limit (rules nothing-dropped). A session's state at the look's time is the first that holds of: stalled (step 8); working, while its turn works; behind a job, its turn ended with a background job of its running; waiting on you, its turn ended on its question (step 8); idle.</step>
      <step n="6" name="the operator's words">Each typed or queued message of the operator's, and each interrupt, is classed by the first that holds of: stop, an interrupt, or a message holding a word of attr_recon_stop_words; status ask, one holding a word or phrase of attr_recon_status_words; question, one that ends with ? once its trailing white space is set aside, or whose first word is one of attr_recon_question_words; approval, one whose first word or phrase is one of attr_recon_yes_words; order, any other. A text's words are its runs of letters, digits, apostrophes and hyphens, lowercased; a word of a list holds when the text has it as a word, a phrase when the text has its words in a row. Each message is also marked a correction when it holds a word or phrase of attr_recon_correction_words, and an escalation when one of its words starts with an entry of attr_recon_escalation_words, as params.rs says each list is read. A shell or slash command is the operator's own act: kept and shown whole, neither classed nor marked.</step>
      <step n="7" name="the orders in force">A session's orders in force are the words its agent may act on. The operator's newest order or approval sets them: an order, its words; an approval, its words and the agent's words to the operator between the operator's message before it and the approval, the proposal it approved. A stop, a status ask or a question leaves them as they were. When the session is held to a task file (step 11), the file's words join them. They stay with the watch while it holds any line, though the message that made them left the window. A session with no order known holds none: it judges no call an unordered action and no words unordered work (step 9), and it says so.</step>
      <step n="8" name="it vanished">Reports of an agent the operator cannot see. Quiet, warn: at a call made attr_recon_watch_quiet_secs or more after the newer of the agent's newest words to the operator and the operator's newest message, when the calls made since then number attr_recon_watch_quiet_calls or more; once, until the agent's next words or the operator's next message; its time, that call's. Behind a job, warn: at a look attr_recon_watch_behind_secs or more after a turn end at which a background job of the session's ran, when neither the agent's words nor an operator's message came since the turn end; once a turn end; its time, the turn end's plus attr_recon_watch_behind_secs. Stalled, alert: its turn works, no call of the turn is running (each has its result, a background call aside), and its newest line is at least attr_recon_watch_quiet_secs before the look; once, until its next line; its time, the newest line's plus attr_recon_watch_quiet_secs. Question unanswered, alert: a turn ended after the operator's question or status ask with no words of the agent's between them; once a message; its time, the turn end's. Status asked, alert: each status ask; its time, the message's; its words, the message whole. Waiting on you, info: a turn ended and the agent's newest words in that turn end with ? once trailing white space is set aside; once a turn end; its time, the turn end's; its words, those words whole.</step>
      <step n="9" name="it isn't following you">An act is a call of Edit, Write, MultiEdit or NotebookEdit, or a call that holds an effect: a call holds the effect of each pattern of attr_recon_watch_effects found, as written, in its tool's name or in what it was called on. Acted on a question, alert: after the operator's question, before the agent's next words to the operator, the session made an act; once a question; its time, the act's; its words, the question whole, and the act's tool and what. Unordered action, alert: a call holds an effect none of whose words the orders in force hold, as step 6 reads words and phrases; once a call, naming each such pattern; its time, the call's; its words, what the call was called on, whole, and the orders in force. Unordered work, alert: the agent's words to the operator hold a word or phrase of attr_recon_watch_scope_words the orders in force do not hold; once a text block, naming those words; its time, the block's; its words, the block whole. Stop not honoured, alert: after a stop, before the agent's next words to the operator, the session made attr_recon_watch_stop_calls calls; once a stop; its time, the call's that made the count. Repeated order, alert: an operator's typed or queued message shares with an earlier one, sent within attr_recon_watch_repeat_secs before it, at least attr_recon_watch_repeat_share percent of the shorter one's distinct words, the shorter one having at least attr_recon_watch_order_words of them; once a message; its time, the message's; its words, both messages whole.</step>
      <step n="10" name="trouble ahead">Long estimate, warn: the agent's words to the operator hold a word or phrase of attr_recon_watch_long_words; once a text block; its time, the block's; its words, the block whole. Long run, warn: at a call made attr_recon_watch_run_secs or more after the operator's newest message, when the calls made since it number attr_recon_watch_run_calls or more; once a message, naming the run's calls by tool and the lines its results wrote; its time, that call's. Struggling, warn: attr_recon_watch_failures results in a row, in the order they came, failed; once, until a result that did not fail; its time, the result's that made the count; its words, each failure's error whole. Memory written, warn: a call writes a path in a memory folder (step 13); once a call; its time, the call's; its words, the path and the call's input whole. Compacted, info: a compaction; its time, the line's. Frustration, alert: of the operator's newest attr_recon_watch_mood_prompts typed or queued messages, once there are that many, more than half are marked escalations; at the message that makes it so, and again at each message after which more of them are; naming how many of how many; its time, the message's.</step>
      <step n="11" name="the task file it executes">A session is held to a task file from the operator's newest typed or queued message that names one: a run of the message's characters without white space that holds an entry of attr_recon_watch_spec_paths followed by a file name ending in .xml, brackets and quotes at its start and commas, periods, colons, brackets and quotes at its end set aside. The run, from the entry on, is the file's path in its checkout; the checkout is the run's part before the entry when the run is an absolute path, else the session's folder or the nearest of its ancestors that holds a file at that path. The watcher reads the file as a file, read-only, and parses it as XML: its root element's version attribute; the text of its owns and of its forbid; its words, the text of its start, of each step and cmd, and of its done. Each entry of owns and of forbid, between semicolons, gives its paths: each run of the entry's characters without white space that holds a slash or ends in a file name with an extension, brackets and quotes at its start and commas, periods, colons, brackets and quotes at its end set aside, relative to the checkout; a path ending in a slash matches every path under that folder, ** matches any run of folders, * any characters but a slash, and every other character itself. A file that cannot be read or parsed holds the session to nothing, and the session shows its path and why, in the error's own words. At each look a file whose modification time changed is read again.</step>
      <step n="12" name="judged against the task file">A session held to a task file is judged against it too. A call writes a path in the checkout when a path it writes (step 4) lies under the checkout's folder. Forbidden file, alert: a call writes a path in the checkout that a forbid path matches and no owns path matches; once a call, naming the path and its forbid entry; its time, the call's. Outside what it owns, alert: a call writes a path in the checkout that no owns path and no forbid path matches; once a call, naming the path; its time, the call's. Off the spec's version, warn: the file read again holds a version other than the one it held when the session was first held to it; once a version, naming both; its time, the look's. The operator's words win over the file's: acted on a question and stop not honoured are judged whatever the file's words order, and their report says when the file's words order the act. The session shows the task file it is held to: its path, its version, the version it held when the session was first held to it, and its owns and forbid entries.</step>
      <step n="13" name="its memory">The projects roots are those the Claude Code adapter captures from: the parameter attr_claude_code_projects_root (PROJECTS_ROOT_PARAM) on the adapter adapter_claude_code (ADAPTER_NAME), one path or a list, read at each look with the kernel's own verbs, find_module_by_name with NodeKind::Adapter and get_parameter; when it is not set, the folder .claude/projects under the environment's HOME, the adapter's own fallback (projects_roots). A session's project folder is the root's child folder on its transcript's path, under the projects root that holds the transcript; its memory folder is the folder memory in it; it has none when no root holds its transcript, and then says so. At each look the watcher lists each memory folder's files, as files, read-only: each file's name, its size in bytes, its modification time, and its description, the value of a description: line in the frontmatter that opens the file between two lines of ---, else none. A path is in a memory folder when it lies in the folder memory of a folder that is a projects root's child.</step>
      <step n="14" name="the reports and the snapshot">A report holds: its id, the session's id, its kind and its time joined, the same at every look, and after a restart for every report the lines read back make again, so no report is made twice and the page tells a new one by its id; its session; its kind, one of quiet, behind_a_job, stalled, question_unanswered, status_asked, waiting_on_you (step 8), acted_on_question, unordered_action, unordered_work, stop_not_honoured, repeated_order (step 9), long_estimate, long_run, struggling, memory_written, compacted, frustration (step 10), forbidden_file, outside_owns, spec_version_changed (step 12); its level, info, warn or alert as its step gives it; its time; what it says, one sentence of the facts its step names; and its words, whole, each with whose they are, the operator's, the agent's or a call's: nothing is cut (rules nothing-dropped), and the page clamps what it shows. The snapshot, published at each look, holds: the look's time; attr_recon_watch_secs and whether the watcher is paused; the window's start; the lines read since the watcher started, and the rows it could not read by why and by agent (step 3); every watch, with its session's id, name and key, agent, model, folder, state, newest line's time; what it is doing now, the call running with its tool, what and since when, none when no call runs; its newest words to the operator; the operator's newest message with its class and marks; the orders in force, or that none is known; the run since the operator's newest message, its calls by tool and the lines its results wrote; its background jobs running, each with its tool, what and since when; its newest recap; the task file it is held to (step 12), or why none; its memory folder with its files (step 13), or why none; and every report of every watch, newest first.</step>
    </module>

    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/" name="the crate">
      <step n="15" name="the parameters">These parameters join the module's, after today's, in this order, each in the group watches, with its fallback, the shape a value must take, the input the tab types it into, and what it decides as written here; each is reported by name when it is set to a value of the wrong shape, as today's are, and attr_recon_correction_words and attr_recon_escalation_words stay as they are in the group measures. The input pairs is new: an object of patterns to lists of words, each a string that is not empty, shown as each pattern, an arrow and its words, the pairs joined by semicolons, in the patterns' order as strings.
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_secs" fallback="5" shape="a whole number, 0 or more" input="number">How often the watcher reads the newest lines, in seconds; 0 pauses it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_sessions" fallback="all" shape="a whole number, 1 or more, or all" input="number or all">Sessions the watcher holds at once, the most recently active kept.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_window_secs" fallback="21600" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">How far back each session is kept, and read back at a start, in seconds.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_quiet_secs" fallback="600" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Seconds an agent may call tools with no word to you, or stall mid-turn, before the watcher reports it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_quiet_calls" fallback="10" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Tool calls it must have made in that time for the watcher to report it quiet.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_behind_secs" fallback="300" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Seconds after a turn ended with a background job running, and nothing said since, before the watcher reports it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_run_secs" fallback="600" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Seconds an agent may run on its own since your last message before the watcher names the run.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_run_calls" fallback="20" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Tool calls a run must have made for the watcher to name it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_stop_calls" fallback="2" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Tool calls after your stop, before a word, at which the watcher reports it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_repeat_secs" fallback="600" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Seconds within which saying the same thing again is a repeated order.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_repeat_share" fallback="75" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Percent of the shorter message's words two messages share to be the same order.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_order_words" fallback="3" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">The fewest words the shorter of two messages has for them to be the same order.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_mood_prompts" fallback="5" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Your newest messages read for frustration: the watcher reports it when more than half escalate.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_failures" fallback="3" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Tool calls failing in a row at which the watcher says the agent struggles.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_stop_words" fallback="stop, halt, enough" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words in your message that stop the agent; an interrupt stops it without words.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_status_words" fallback="status, what is going on, what's going on, whats going on, what's up, whats up, eta, where are we" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words or phrases in your message that ask for status.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_question_words" fallback="what, why, how, when, where, who, which, is, are, was, were, does, did, should" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words your message starts with when it is a question; one ending in ? is one too.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_yes_words" fallback="yes, approved, approve, go, ok, okay, proceed, do it, sure" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words or phrases your message starts with when it approves what the agent proposed.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_scope_words" fallback="review, rehearsal, rehearse, rerun, re-run, regenerate, rewrite, from scratch, refactor" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words or phrases in the agent's words that announce work your orders did not say.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_long_words" fallback="hours, an hour, overnight" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words or phrases in the agent's words that make a long estimate.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_spec_paths" fallback="kn-specs/tasks/, specs/recon/tasks/" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Where task files live: a session whose message names one is held to it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_effects" fallback="CommentToJira → jira, comment; createJiraIssue → jira, ticket, issue; deploy → deploy; editJiraIssue → jira; gh pr create → pr, pull request; gh pr merge → merge; git branch -D → delete, branch; git commit → commit, commits, committed; git merge → merge, merged; git push → push, pushed, pr, pull request, merge; git push --force → force; git rebase → rebase; git reset → reset; rm -rf → delete, remove, drop, clean; transitionJiraIssue → jira" shape="an object of patterns to lists of non-empty strings" input="pairs">Actions beyond the session, each a pattern found in a call's tool or in what it was called on, with the words an order must say for it.</param>
      </step>
      <step n="16" name="the routes">server.rs serves, beside today's routes and behind the same Host check, two routes, each answering from the watcher's snapshot and never reading the kernel's database: GET /api/watch, the snapshot as JSON (step 14); and GET /api/watch/memory?session=&lt;a session's id&gt;&amp;file=&lt;a file's name&gt;, that file of that session's memory folder, read at the request as a file, read-only, and answered whole as text/plain; charset=utf-8. It refuses with HTTP 400 a file that is empty, holds a slash or a backslash, or is . or .., and a session that is not a UUID; with HTTP 404 a session no watch is held for, one with no memory folder, and a file the folder's newest listing does not hold; each refusal is a JSON object whose error says why, as today's refusals are. A route that refuses writes nothing and reads nothing else.</step>
      <step n="17" name="the process">superx-recon run starts the watcher once the page listens, as a task of its own beside the crawler's, with the kernel's connection, the instance and the same stop the crawler is given, so one stop ends both; the page's routes are given the watcher's snapshot. At the stop the process waits for the watcher as it waits for the crawler, and logs a watcher that did not end cleanly as it logs the crawler. Nothing else of run changes.</step>
    </module>

    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/ui/" name="the page">
      <step n="18" name="the Watcher tab">ui/src/App.tsx: the page's tabs are Crawler, Results and Watcher, in that order; #watcher opens the Watcher; on it the header's trail reads SuperX › Recon › Watcher, Recon leading to #crawler as on the other tabs, and the browser's title is Watcher · Recon · SuperX. The subtitle under Recon reads: The crawler, and what it gathers: the models, the agents and the repositories they worked in, measured over time; and the watcher, every session as it works. The Watcher's panel and its parts are in ui/src/watcher/.</step>
      <step n="19" name="the sessions">The Watcher asks /api/watch every two seconds, as the Crawler's Now panel asks /api/now, and shows at its top when the watcher last looked, its interval, paused when it is, the window's start, the lines read and the rows not read by why and agent; then one card per session, those with an alert among their reports first, then those with a warn, then the others, each group by its newest line, newest first. A card shows: its name, agent, model and folder; its state as a badge; what it is doing now; its newest words; your newest message with its class and marks; the orders in force, or that none is known; the run since your newest message; its jobs running; its recap; the task file it executes with its version, the version it held when the session was first held to it, its owns and its forbid, or why none; its memory files (step 20); its reports, newest first. Times are in the operator's own zone, as ui/src/time.ts writes them. A long text is shown clamped and opens whole on a click.</step>
      <step n="20" name="the reports and the memory">Beside the cards, every report, newest first: its time, its level as a badge, its kind in words (stop not honoured for stop_not_honoured, and so on), the session's name, what it says, and its words, clamped, opening whole. A card's memory lists each file with its description and when it last changed; a file opens in a modal that holds it whole, as /api/watch/memory answers it.</step>
      <step n="21" name="notify me">A switch, Notify me, kept in the browser's localStorage, each read and write in a try/catch, off when it cannot be read. When it is turned on the page asks the browser's permission (Notification.requestPermission). While it is on and the permission granted, each report of level warn or alert that an answer of /api/watch holds for the first time since the page opened or the switch was turned on raises one browser notification: its title the kind in words and the session's name, its body what the report says. A report the page already held raises none. When the browser refuses or has no notifications, the switch says so.</step>
      <step n="22" name="the parameters and the build">ui/src/panels/Params.tsx lists the group watches as Watches, after Measures, and types the input pairs: one pair per line, the pattern, an equals sign and its words separated by commas, the first equals sign of a line splitting it; none for no pair; its hint says so. ui/src/api.ts gains the snapshot's types and the two fetches. No dependency joins: package.json and package-lock.json are as merged. The page is built with npm ci and npm run build in crates/superx-mod-recon/ui, and ui/dist and ui/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo are committed as the build writes them (gate G5).</step>
    </module>

BLUEPRINT.md 186 lines · the architecture
# SuperX Blueprint — The Agentic OS (Reset of 2026-08-06)

> **Status: v1.0 — decisions D1–D4 locked by the operator 2026-08-06.**
> This document lands with the G0 reset PR; merging that PR is the
> operator's approval of the reset. The complete v1 FVP is preserved at
> the tag `archive/pre-reset-2026-08-06`. This is the living canon —
> it replaces `ARCHITECTURE.md` (retired with v1).

## 1. Mission

SuperX is an **agentic operating system** written in Rust.

- **SurrealDB is the OS's filesystem.** Every fact the OS knows —
  agents, telemetry, state, configuration, the module registry itself —
  lives in the substrate. If it isn't in SurrealDB, the OS doesn't know
  it. No sidecar config files, no in-memory-only state that matters —
  with one deliberately narrow bootstrap-scope exception (D12/D16):
  what must be known BEFORE the substrate is reachable lives in the
  `<home>/{params,logs,db}` instance layout (`params/superx.json`,
  the credentials file, the pidfile, the logs).
- **Telemetry capture is the kernel's core capability.** From the moment
  the OS boots, it captures all telemetry emitted by every agent on the
  system. This is not a module, not an option, not a plugin — it is
  what the kernel *is*. An agentic OS that cannot see its agents is not
  an OS.
- **Everything else is a module.** Data fusion, graphify, and every
  future capability arrive as modules loaded on top of the kernel. The
  kernel has zero knowledge of any module's domain.

## 2. What the kernel does (and nothing else)

1. **Substrate access** — typed verbs over SurrealDB. Append-only:
   SELECT + CREATE only, no verb ever issues UPDATE / DELETE / DDL.
   "Current state" is computed, never mutated (SCD-2).
2. **Boot** — `superx boot` connects to the substrate, registers
   modules, brings them up with failure isolation, and starts the
   telemetry engine. Boot always completes; a failed module never
   takes the OS down.
3. **Agent telemetry capture** — the engine that discovers agents on
   the machine, tails whatever they emit, writes typed telemetry rows
   **and first-class conversation rows** (`message` table — every
   session with every agent, very detailed, raw event preserved), and
   checkpoints with cursors so restarts are lossless. Runs from boot
   until shutdown; captures everything by default (operator decision
   2026-08-07 — no per-agent grant gate; OS-level file prompts are the
   permission surface).
4. **CLI** — the operator interface: `--initialize` (one-command
   provisioning + background boot, idempotent per instance; the
   terminal returns), `start` / `stop` / `restart` / `status` (background-OS
   lifecycle, pidfile-backed), `agents`, `actions [--agent] [--live]` (the
   telemetry stream), `sessions` (list conversations), `read
   <session-fragment> [--live]` (render a conversation, historical
   then follow), `logs [--follow] [--daemon]` (the OS's own log).
   `boot` remains as the foreground debug mode. All bootstrap config
   resolves flag > env > `params/superx.json` > fallback. The CLI is
   part of the kernel deliverable, not an app bolted on later.
5. **Module system** — modules register with the kernel, declare
   dependencies, and get lifecycle management (starting / active /
   failed / skipped). The registry lives in the substrate like
   everything else.

**Self-log.** The kernel keeps its own rolling log file in a local
`logs/` directory (override: `SUPERX_LOG_DIR`), independent of the
substrate — boot diagnostics and signin failures must be observable
when the database is down. Substrate telemetry remains the audit log
of record; the self-log is the layer below it. (Operator directive,
2026-08-07.)

**Agent adapters.** The capture *engine* is kernel-core. Knowing how a
*specific* agent emits telemetry (Claude Code writes JSONL transcripts;
another agent may expose OTLP, logs, or an API) is adapter knowledge.
Adapters are the one plugin interface the kernel itself defines — small,
per-agent, and hot-swappable without touching the engine.

## 3. What carries forward from v1 (proven mechanics, not code)

The v1 codebase is archived wholesale; no file survives on `main`. But
these v1 mechanics were verified live and are carried forward as
*design decisions*, re-implemented clean:

- Append-only substrate with SCD-2 (`valid_from`, latest-wins reads).
- Types-as-data metamodel: new entity/attr types arrive by INSERT,
  never by schema migration.
- Cursor-checkpointed capture: poll → emit telemetry → write cursor;
  restart-lossless; errors become telemetry, never panics.
- Service-account discipline: the kernel signs in as a minimal-privilege
  user, never root; the operator owns the schema and applies it once.
- Module lifecycle with failure isolation and topological boot order.
- Tolerant parsing: unknown telemetry shapes are captured raw, not
  dropped.

## 4. What is retired

- The 6-crate L0–L3 layering (kernel / kernel-modules / drivers / apps).
  v2 started as **one workspace, two crates**: `superx-kernel` (lib) and
  `superx` (bin, the CLI). Crates are extracted only when a module
  graduates and proves the seam — the graduates are
  `superx-mod-hello`, the reference module (epic #141), `superx-ops` (P3): the shared runners/renderers both the CLI and
  modules consume — kernel-only dependencies, so modules never depend
  on the bin — and `superx-mod-ui` (P4+): the UI module itself, an
  axum server inside the daemon on `attr_ui_port`: the template
  contributors copy, exercising every facility of the module contract
  (own schema/db, own dir, own log, own CLI).
- The F-phase roadmap, the 2026-06 quality backlog, and all 20 open
  pre-reset GitHub issues (they reference retired concepts).
- `ARCHITECTURE.md` (v42.15, 88 KB) — the historical vision document.
  This blueprint replaces it as the living canon. Bloat was a named
  failure mode; this document stays under 300 lines or it gets split.

## 5. The new FVP

> The operator runs `superx boot`. Every coding agent on the machine
> is discovered and stored in the substrate. Detailed telemetry AND
> full conversations stream in from Claude Code, Gemini CLI, and
> Claude Desktop. `superx actions --live` shows what agents are doing;
> `superx read <session> --live` lets the operator read any
> conversation — historical first, then live as it continues. Adding a
> new agent adapter touches zero engine code.

Measured by: **three** agent adapters working day one (Claude Code,
Gemini CLI, Claude Desktop), conversations readable end-to-end,
lossless restart mid-stream, and the whole workspace green at the
gates.

For every build — a session whose kickoff line names a task file — it records the spec files the build read (once per content) and their clauses, and checks what the build did against them; level 1 is the read order the root requires. The fault ledger (`superx specs ledger`) sets every spec version a session committed — by which model, and why — beside every build of it and what its git did to the build's branch. It reads the captured sessions only (SELECT): no repository, no git, no process; own database `superx/specs`, every row derived. Next: ownership and gate checks, then a read-only repository crawler | Replayed against the real ENG-8071/8072 builds; schema read back from the engine and diffed against the file |

Measurement is **one module, `superx-mod-recon`** (operator, 2026-10-25: one module, one port, one schema, its parameters, a credentials file and one crawler; 2026-10-26: named recon, built from the XML build spec under `specs/recon/`, the structure first, then the crawler, then its tab): **its own executable, `superx-recon`**, its own database `superx/recon`, provisioned by `superx-recon --initialize`, and its own URL (`attr_recon_port`), which answers only requests addressed to it. **One crawler** fills it through its sources: the session logs (every captured line, SELECT only), the git repositories the sessions worked in (read-only, with a mirror clone of each fetched into the module's own folder, never pruned), and the hosts they live on (GitHub, Bitbucket: their read APIs, GET only, with the operator's read-only tokens, every answer kept whole), one worker per repository, as many at once as a parameter allows. It correlates what the agents did with what the code bases show, diligence before speed. It is never linked into `superx`, so nothing it does can reach capture. It replaces `superx-mod-performance` (`superx-performance`), its first build, and `superx-mod-repos` (`superx-repos`), the first repository crawler: their code moves into it and their crates leave the workspace in the spec's first task | `repos` schema operator-approved 2026-10-21; the executable, its registry entry and its URL (#467). `performance` schema operator-approved 2026-10-24; its executable, registry entry and URL (#506); the reader (#508). The one schema and the one crawler operator-approved 2026-10-25 (#510). The recon structure, operator-approved 2026-10-26 and cut on 2026-10-29 to 44 tables, the ones a measure uses or the crawler writes, every source kept whole beside them, in `crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql` |

A label is an entity, so a new label or a new kind of connection is data, never a migration; the module never interprets. **Provisioning removes the DATABASE and rebuilds it**, and a test reads the schema back out of the engine and diffs it against the file — the second attempt defined its tables `IF NOT EXISTS`, left an older shape underneath, and nothing checked | schema verified against the engine; the whole UI flow driven in a browser |

| D7 | Conversation storage (2026-08-07) | **Dedicated `message` table** — first-class, readable historical + live; raw source event always preserved |
| D8 | Capture consent (2026-08-07) | **Capture everything by default** — no per-agent grant gate; OS file prompts are the permission surface |
| D9 | Agent storage (2026-08-07) | **Agents stay entities** (`node_agent`); entity row id = agent_id; new agent properties are data writes, never schema migrations |
| D10 | CLI viewing surface (2026-08-07) | `actions [--live]` + `sessions` + `read <session> [--live]` |
| D11 | One-command init (2026-08-18, issue #120) | `superx --initialize` prompts for ONE password (any accepted at this phase) serving both database root and the service account; root is used once in-process to apply the schema and never stored |
| D12 | Credential persistence (2026-08-18) | Service password saved to a `0600` `superx-credentials` file beside the datastore so query commands need no exports; env var still wins |
| D13 | Git discipline (2026-08-18) | Every change ties to a GitHub issue; branches + PRs reference it |
| D14 | Session lookup (2026-08-18, #122) | Sessions identified `<agent>/<uuid7>`; `read` matches any unique fragment; source id rides as `src=` |
| D15 | Background OS (2026-08-18, #124) | `--initialize` returns the terminal: OS runs detached (pidfile, daemon log); `superx stop`; duplicate-capture guards; shutdown honored between source polls |
| D16 | Instance config (2026-08-18, #125) | `params/superx.json` controls all bootstrap config inside the `<home>/{params,logs,db}` layout; precedence flag > env > file > fallback; `superx logs` surfaces the self-log. Released as **v1.0.0** |
| D17 | Module facility contract (2026-08-19, epic #141) | A module is a self-contained mini-app: own db `superx/<name>` + service account (via `modules provision`), own dir, own log target, own CLI namespace, own parameters; kernel is the hub — modules depend on the kernel only, never on each other |
| D18 | Module identity (2026-08-19, epic #141) | Every module is a registry entity with a uuid7 id; same-kind modules coexist (nothing assumes a singleton); compiled-in v1 contract (linkme), WASM parked |
| D19 | Native graph edges (2026-08-19, epic #166) | The entities module links nodes with SurrealDB `RELATE` edges (`TYPE RELATION IN entity OUT entity ENFORCED`): traversal follows record pointers (cost = a node's degree, never table size); RELATE is creation-only — blessed as CREATE-equivalent under the append-only contract, module db only |
| D20 | Identity/state split (2026-08-19, epic #166) | Graph nodes = one immutable uuid7 anchor row (stable edge target, forever) + SCD-2 `entity_state` version chains; edge history = `edge_uid` chains where unlink appends `active=false` |
| D21 | Executor is the next epic (2026-08-19, epic #166) | This epic ships the graph substrate + CLI; the runner module that walks the graph and executes task nodes — and the dashboard graph page — follow separately |
| D22 | Text-as-entity (2026-08-19, epic #166) | All long-form text (descriptions, comments, instructions) is a `text` NODE linked by role edges (`describes`/`comments`/`instructs`), direction owner → target; entities keep a one-line `name` label; describe/instruct evolve one node (its history = the text's evolution), comments multiply (threads = comment a comment) |
| D23 | Runner schema (2026-08-19, epic #189) | Two tables, SCD-2, inserts only, uuid7 everywhere: `schedule` intent chains (time + entity ref + recurrence — NOTHING else; the graph is the plan) + `run` fact chains (one per firing×task; statuses appended) |
| D24 | Cross-module contract v1 (2026-08-19, epic #189) | Modules talk through each other's CLIs via the kernel's in-process dispatch (`entities graph --json`, `entities create/link`) — the same surface humans, the Console, and agents use; no module-to-module crate deps |
| D25 | Dependency semantics (2026-08-19, epic #189) | `depends_on` edges among task nodes; runnable = all deps done THIS firing; the runnable frontier dispatches in parallel up to `attr_runner_max_parallel`; failed deps block dependents (never dispatched) |
| D26 | Executor v1 (2026-08-19, epic #189) | One contract: spawn `attr_runner_agent_cmd` (NO default — unset refuses loudly) with the assembled prompt (task name · instructs · product describes · 1-hop linked context); stdout writes back as `task —produced→ text`; recurrence `every:<n><s\|m\|h\|d>` |
| D27 | In-flight steering (2026-08-19, epic #189) | The subgraph re-reads at every frontier re-evaluation — mid-run graph/instruction edits apply to everything not yet dispatched; every run row pins the instruct version it dispatched; cancel halts future dispatch, in-flight finishes and records |

## 8. Binding rules (unchanged from v1, restated)

Trunk-based development: single trunk `main`, always releasable, short-
lived branches, one PR at a time, all-or-nothing branches. Gates:
`cargo test --workspace` + `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets
--all-features -- -D warnings` + `python3 tools/skill_audit.py`
(CI-enforced as required checks). Schema changes are operator-approved,
per-change, schema-first (`SUPERX_SCHEMA.md` before code). The kernel
authenticates as a service account, never root. No hardcoded policy —
constants flow through substrate parameters.

.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md 189 lines · the mandates every change obeys
---
name: zero-trust-execution
description: SuperX project operating mode — invoke (or treat as always-on) for any work in the SuperX codebase. Enforces no shortcuts, mandatory documentation research, fidelity to BLUEPRINT.md, a stop-and-ask protocol, and verification-as-truth (cargo test + cargo clippy -- -D warnings + skill_audit.py).
---

<instructions>
You are operating under **Zero-Trust Execution Mode** for SuperX — the
agentic operating system (reset of 2026-08-06; canon: `BLUEPRINT.md`).
These mandates are binding. The section numbers are load-bearing: CI
(`.github/workflows/skill-audit.yml`) and `tools/skill_audit.py`
reference them by number.

### §1 The Anti-Velocity Mandate
- NEVER optimize for speed. No workarounds, hacks, or quick fixes to
  get past a compiler, database, or runtime error.
- If you hit an error you do not fully understand: **STOP**. Research
  it or ask. Never paper over it.

### §2 The Research Imperative
- Every technical decision (library configuration, data-type handling,
  error semantics) is grounded in official documentation — SurrealDB,
  Tokio, clap, serde — fetched and read, not recalled.
- Never guess a function signature. Never hallucinate a workaround.
  Implement exactly what the library authors specify, in idiomatic Rust.

### §3 Architectural Fidelity
- `BLUEPRINT.md` and the operator's explicit instructions are absolute
  law. You have ZERO authority to alter the architecture, the kernel's
  five responsibilities, the module/adapter seams, or core patterns
  (append-only substrate, SCD-2, cursor checkpointing) without explicit
  prior operator permission.
- The kernel boundary is part of the architecture: the kernel owns
  substrate access, boot, the telemetry capture engine, the CLI, and
  the module system — and knows nothing about any module's domain.
  Agent-format knowledge lives only in adapters. Module or adapter
  logic leaking into the engine is an architecture change and requires
  operator sign-off.
- If an implementation detail seems to conflict with the blueprint:
  **STOP AND ASK.** Do not invent a path forward.

### §4 The Stop-and-Ask Protocol
Halt and ask the operator when any of these holds:
1. An error prevents the workspace from compiling or tests from passing
   and the fix is not evident.
2. Official documentation contradicts your understanding.
3. You are tempted to write a workaround because the correct way seems
   too difficult or slow.
4. You realize you are acting on an assumption rather than a verified
   fact.

### §5 Verification Is the Only Truth
- Code does not exist until `cargo test --workspace` passes.
- Code is not clean until
  `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`
  passes.
- The tree is not compliant until `python3 tools/skill_audit.py` passes.
- You are forbidden from declaring a task complete, or updating
  `BLUEPRINT.md` status, until all three gates are green.

### §6 Trunk-Based Development (mandatory workflow)
- Single trunk `main`, always releasable. Every commit on `main` passes
  the §5 gates. Releases are tagged from `main`.
- All work happens on a topic branch (`feat/`, `fix/`, `chore/`,
  `docs/`); every change ships as a PR against `main`. Never commit to
  `main` directly; never force-push `main`.
- Every change ties to a GitHub issue (operator directive 2026-08-18);
  branch names carry the issue number and PRs reference/close it.
- Branches are short-lived (hours, never overnight). One open branch +
  one open PR at a time — sequential, never parallel.
- Each branch is atomic: it lands fully working (gates green,
  feature operational) or it does not land. Unfinished work hides
  behind a substrate parameter flag, never as broken code on `main`.
- One logical step per PR — small diffs. If two changes can be
  reasoned about independently, they are two PRs. Load-bearing
  refactors use branch-by-abstraction: seam first, migrate second,
  remove third — each PR green.
- Pre-flight before any branch: is the change self-contained; is the
  path to green gates visible; does the diff fit one mental model; is
  a flag needed; can you state the PR description in 2–3 sentences?
- Banned: mega-branches, speculative branches, long-lived branches,
  force-merging red gates, mid-branch side-quests.
- Merged branches are deleted, locally and on origin.

### §7 Schema Immutability — STOP. ASK. THEN MAYBE.
- The substrate schema (`SUPERX_SCHEMA.md` and every `schema/*.surql`
  file, once G1 lands them) is load-bearing architecture. Every table,
  field, index, and permission is a contract.
- You have ZERO authority to change schema-defining files without
  explicit, prior, **per-change** operator permission. "Refactor,"
  "cleanup," "small change," and "the prior PR was wrong" are not
  authorization. Authorization for one change does not extend to
  adjacent changes.
- CI enforces this: any PR touching schema-defining files or
  `.claude/skills/` fails unless the PR body carries an
  `Operator-approved: …` marker.
- If you believe a shipped schema is wrong: STOP, surface it, propose
  the fix, and wait.

### §8 The Kernel Mental Model
Layers, and what may know what:
- **Substrate (SurrealDB)** — the OS's filesystem. Dumb, honest,
  append-only facts: entities, state, telemetry, cursors, parameters,
  the module registry. If it isn't in the substrate, the OS doesn't
  know it.
- **Kernel** — substrate verbs, boot, the capture engine, CLI, module
  lifecycle. Generic over agents and modules.
- **Adapters** — per-agent format knowledge only (where an agent's
  telemetry lives and how to parse it). An adapter never touches the
  engine's internals; the engine never names a specific agent.
- **Modules** — every capability that is not the kernel (data fusion,
  graphify, …). A module consumes kernel verbs; the kernel never
  consumes a module.
Putting logic in the wrong layer is a §3 violation.

### §9 No Hardcoded Policy
- Every tunable — poll intervals, limits, paths, thresholds, defaults —
  flows through substrate parameters. No magic numbers or magic paths
  in code. Tests inject fixtures via parameters, not env vars.
- A constant may be exempted only with an explicit audited
  `// skill-allow: §9-…` marker that `tools/skill_audit.py` accepts.
- Judgments (rankings, priorities, retry policy) are not schema fields
  and not Rust constants — they are decisions recorded in the
  substrate, made by whatever decision-maker the blueprint assigns.
  Facts go in columns; judgments do not.

### §10 Account Separation — Operator Owns the Schema
- The operator owns the database root account and applies all DDL.
  The model never holds or uses root credentials, ever.
- The kernel runs on a minimal service account whose behavior contract
  is **SELECT + CREATE only**. No kernel verb ever emits UPDATE,
  DELETE, UPSERT, DEFINE, REMOVE, or ALTER — append-only is enforced by
  kernel-verb discipline (SurrealDB role grants alone cannot narrow a
  system user to insert-only; the verbs are the enforcement layer).
- "Current state" is computed at read time (latest `valid_from` wins),
  never by mutating prior rows.

### §11 Schema-First, Code-After
- Every new table, field, index, or type is designed in
  `SUPERX_SCHEMA.md` first, with the operator, before any code.
  The operator applies the DDL under root; the model codes against the
  locked schema afterward.
- Schema designs include: typed field lists, `record<table>` FKs with
  their `ASSERT` constraints, the SCD-2 ordering contract
  (`valid_from`, latest-wins; no mutable currency flags — append-only
  forbids them), the UUIDv7 row-id contract (kernel-set, never
  auto-generated), and a migration plan if the table exists.
- Session authorization is scoped to the specific change designed in
  that session — nothing adjacent.

### §12 Constraints Are the Debugging Surface
- Typed FKs, `ASSERT` clauses, and SCHEMAFULL refusals are the OS's
  primary debugging lattice: bugs surface as engine refusals at the
  offending call, not as silent corruption downstream.
- An engine refusal is always a signal to fix the code that issued the
  operation. It is never a reason to relax a constraint, cast around a
  type, or escalate credentials.

### §13 Credentials — Service Account Only, Never Root
- The kernel authenticates exclusively as the service account defined
  in `SUPERX_SCHEMA.md` (name, env-var override, and grant are fixed
  there at G1 — the schema doc is the single source of truth for
  credentials; this skill intentionally does not duplicate them).
- If any code path appears to need root: STOP and hand it to the
  operator. Using root to get past an engine refusal is banned in all
  forms, including "just for this test" and "just for this migration."
- One narrow carve-out (operator-directed, 2026-08-18, issue #120):
  `superx --initialize` is the operator's provisioning tool. It
  prompts the operator for the root password interactively, uses it
  once in-process (`superx_kernel::provision`) to apply the locked
  schema, and never stores it. No runtime path may call into the
  provision module.

### §14 No Data-Type Conversions — Types Flow End-to-End
- A `record<table>` is a `Thing` everywhere. A `uuid` is a `Uuid`
  everywhere. A `datetime` is a `DateTime<Utc>` everywhere. Strings
  appear only at genuine text-payload boundaries (prompt bodies, log
  lines, error messages).
- Banned: `<string>` casts in SurrealQL to satisfy a mis-typed Rust
  struct; `format!`-rebuilding record ids at call sites;
  string-comparing what the engine compares typed; "just `String` it
  for the test."
- A type-mismatch refusal from the engine means the Rust side is
  wrong. Fix the struct, not the query.

### Execution-Loop Enforcement
Before every action, silently ask: *Am I guessing? Am I rushing? Did I
read the documentation?* If any answer is yes, you are violating this
protocol.
</instructions>

SUPERX_SCHEMA.md 267 lines · the kernel's tables
# SUPERX_SCHEMA.md — Substrate Schema, Source of Truth (v2.3)

> **Status: locked on apply.** v2 designed operator + model on
> 2026-08-06; **v2.1** (conversations first-class: `message` table +
> per-agent query fields) designed operator + model on 2026-08-07 —
> both per skill §11 (schema-first, code-after). The DDL lives in
> [`schema/kernel.surql`](schema/kernel.surql); the operator applies it
> under root via [`scripts/deploy-schema.sh`](scripts/deploy-schema.sh).
> From that moment the schema is locked: every change requires a fresh
> per-change `Operator-approved:` PR marker (skill §7, CI-enforced).
> **v2.2** (module registration ledger) designed 2026-08-19 per the
> operator directive in issue #150.
> **v2.3** (`message.raw_text`, each event exactly as written) approved
> by the operator on 2026-10-25, issue #528.

## 1. Contracts (binding on every kernel verb)

- **SurrealDB is the OS's filesystem.** One namespace `superx`, one
  database `kernel` for the kernel layer. Modules/adapters that need
  their own tables ship their own schemas + service accounts in later
  operator-approved PRs.
- **Append-only.** The service account's behavior contract is SELECT +
  CREATE. No kernel verb ever emits UPDATE, DELETE, UPSERT, DEFINE,
  REMOVE, or ALTER. Enforcement is kernel-verb discipline (SurrealDB's
  EDITOR role cannot be narrowed to insert-only for system users; the
  `FOR update/delete NONE` PERMISSIONS clauses below are documentation
  of intent).
- **SCD-2 by ordering, not by flags.** "Current" is always computed at
  read time: latest `valid_from` wins within a chain
  (`ORDER BY valid_from DESC LIMIT 1`). There are **no** `is_current` /
  `valid_to` columns — append-only forbids mutable currency flags, and
  advisory ones go permanently stale (v1 lesson).
- **UUIDv7 row ids, kernel-set.** Every row id is a UUIDv7 generated by
  the kernel at insert time — never SurrealDB auto-ids. Time-ordered
  ids make insertion order recoverable from the id itself.
- **Types flow end-to-end** (skill §14): `record<…>` FKs stay `Thing`
  in Rust, `datetime` stays `DateTime<Utc>`, `uuid` stays `Uuid`.
- **Facts, not judgments** (skill §9): a column records what happened
  or what is; rankings, priorities, and policy are never schema.

## 2. Service account

| | |
|---|---|
| User | `superx_kernel` (ON DATABASE) |
| Role | EDITOR |
| Password | `$SUPERX_KERNEL_PASSWORD`, substituted at deploy time; the kernel reads the same env var at signin. No literal ever lives in the repo. |
| Duration | `FOR TOKEN 1h, FOR SESSION NONE` — sessions never expire (operator decision 2026-08-06; v1's 1 h session killed long-running OS processes). Confirmed against SurrealDB docs: session duration `NONE` is valid and unexpiring. |

Root is operator-only, used exactly once per substrate to apply this
schema. The kernel signs in as `superx_kernel` forever after (skill §10/§13).

## 3. Tables (9)

Every table is SCHEMAFULL, global (single-operator OS, no tenancy), and
carries `valid_from: datetime` set by the kernel at insert.

### 3.1 `type_definition` — the metamodel
Types are data: new entity/edge/attribute types arrive by INSERT, never
DDL. **Immutable once created** (backed by the UNIQUE index): type
evolution means a new uid, or an operator-approved migration.

| field | type | constraint |
|---|---|---|
| `uid` | `string` | semantic key (`node_agent`, `attr_desc`, …); UNIQUE index |
| `category` | `string` | `node` \| `edge` \| `attribute` |
| `is_acyclic` | `bool` | meaningful for edge types |
| `sch_json` | `option<string>` | optional JSON-schema for payload validation |
| `memory_tier` | `string` | ASSERT ∈ `['core','working','archival','recall']` |

Kernel metamodel seed types are runtime INSERTs by the boot
orchestrator (G3), not DDL — they are listed in kernel code, not here.

### 3.2 `cursor_type` — cursor-kind metamodel
Same immutability + UNIQUE-uid contract as `type_definition`.

| field | type | constraint |
|---|---|---|
| `uid` | `string` | UNIQUE index |
| `category` | `string` | ASSERT ∈ `['workload','telemetry']` |
| `description` | `string` | |
| `sch_json` | `option<string>` | |

### 3.3 `entity` — identity (the persistent "who")
Agents, sources, modules, adapters, products, … — everything the OS
knows exists. Differentiated by `type`.

| field | type | constraint |
|---|---|---|
| `type` | `record<type_definition>` | ASSERT `$value.category = 'node'` |

### 3.4 `relation` — typed graph edges

| field | type | constraint |
|---|---|---|
| `in` | `record<entity>` | |
| `out` | `record<entity>` | |
| `type` | `record<type_definition>` | ASSERT `$value.category = 'edge'` |
| `is_acyclic` | `bool` | |

### 3.5 `state_ledger` — SCD-2 content (the versioned "what")
Every piece of state attached to an entity is a row here; new rows
supersede older ones per `(target, type)` chain.

| field | type | constraint |
|---|---|---|
| `target` | `record<entity>` | |
| `type` | `record<type_definition>` | ASSERT `$value.category = 'attribute'` |
| `payload` | `object FLEXIBLE` | shape enforced per attribute type by kernel-verb discipline |

Composite index `(target, type, valid_from)` backs the current-state
read path.

### 3.6 `cursor` — durable checkpoints
Append-only capture/workload checkpoints; the capture engine resumes
losslessly from the latest row per `(subject, cursor_type)`.

| field | type | constraint |
|---|---|---|
| `subject` | `record<entity>` | the source/run being checkpointed |
| `cursor_type` | `record<cursor_type>` | |
| `last_processed` | `option<string>` | opaque position token |
| `metadata` | `option<object> FLEXIBLE` | e.g. per-file offsets |

Composite index `(subject, cursor_type, valid_from)` backs the
latest-cursor read path.

### 3.7 `telemetry_stream` — the firehose
One row per event: every kernel mutation, every capture emission, every
lifecycle transition. Global, unscoped.

| field | type | constraint |
|---|---|---|
| `lifecycle_event` | `string` | event name (`transcript_event`, `module_active`, …) |
| `payload` | `any` | event body, tolerant by design |
| `subject` | `option<record<entity>>` | who emitted this — module, agent, or source; no type ASSERT |
| `agent` | `option<record<entity>>` | *(v2.1)* the agent this event concerns; ASSERT `node_agent` when present — direct per-agent queries |

Index on `valid_from` backs the stats/live poll path; `(agent,
valid_from)` backs per-agent action queries. Event rows are never
superseded — `valid_from` here is the event timestamp.

### 3.8 `message` — conversations, first-class *(v2.1)*
One row per conversation event captured from an agent session —
readable historically and live. The agent's row id **is** the
agent_id; per-agent reads are one indexed query
(`SELECT * FROM message WHERE agent = $agent_id ORDER BY valid_from`).

| field | type | constraint |
|---|---|---|
| `session` | `record<entity>` | ASSERT type `node_session` — the conversation |
| `agent` | `record<entity>` | ASSERT type `node_agent` — who the conversation is with |
| `role` | `string` | source-native label (`user`, `assistant`, `tool`, …); deliberately un-ASSERTed — tolerance beats constraint at the capture boundary |
| `content` | `string` | extracted readable text; empty when the event has none |
| `raw` | `option<object> FLEXIBLE` | the full original source event — capture is very detailed by design |
