  <command>
    <form>superx-recon run, as t01 left it: the crawler's passes, one every attr_recon_read_secs. And superx-recon as git's askpass program (step 23).</form>
    <runs>Its own process, as today. After this task's deploy its first pass crawls every repository on the list and every host it leads to in depth, which takes as long as git and the hosts' rate limits make it.</runs>
  </command>

  <build>
    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/repos/" name="the repositories' part of the crawler">
      <step n="1" name="which repositories, and their workers">The list is read as t01 left it, with step 12. Then the pass crawls repositories in depth: at a full pass (every attr_recon_crawl_secs, as today) every repository on the list; at any other pass, each repository new to the list in this pass, and each repository one of whose checkouts holds the dir of a git_event the lines source wrote in this pass (its valid_from at or after the pass began) of the action commit, push, merge, rebase, revert, cherry_pick, reset, branch_deleted, pr_created or pr_merged. A repository the operator excludes (attr_recon_exclude) is never crawled. A repository with no present checkout is crawled through its common git directory while that exists; one whose common git directory is gone is not crawled, and what the crawler holds of it stays. Each repository crawled has a worker of its own, as many at once as attr_recon_repos_at_once allows (step 24), started in the order of the repositories' common directories. A worker runs steps 3 to 11 in their order, step 4 starting the host crawl of each host repository it takes; then it finishes those host crawls (steps 15 to 21); then step 13; and it holds every row they make; when they are done it writes them, the crawl row first and then each row after the rows it links, and nothing of another repository. The pass waits for every worker before it records itself. A stop ends every worker where it is: the call it waits on is killed, and a worker stopped writes nothing.</step>
      <step n="2" name="git, read-only">Every git call of a crawl runs as t01's calls run (repos/git.rs): one git process per call, never a shell; the signature check and the fsmonitor overridden; no diff driver and no external diff where a subcommand takes the flags; no optional lock; killed at attr_recon_git_timeout_ms. In the operator's repositories nothing is written, fetched or checked out, no format that verifies a signature is used (git's %G placeholders), and no program a repository names runs. Every call of a crawl is a git_call row: crawl; step, the word the step below gives it (setup, fetch, refs, unreachable, log, patches, reflog, placement, landed, tip, blame; tags, merges and checkouts are not used); dir; args, exactly as run, the overrides included; started_at; ms; exit, NONE when it was cut off or could not start; timed_out; bytes_out and lines_out, what it printed; stderr, what git wrote to its error output, whole, NONE when it wrote nothing. The list's calls (t01's) stay counted in the source's record and are no rows, since no crawl holds them.</step>
      <step n="3" name="the remotes">A crawl first reads its repository's configuration (step setup): every remote's url and pushurl, user.email, and whether git says the repository is shallow. A remote is a remote_state row (repository, crawl, name, url, push_url, NONE when it has no pushurl, host, path) when one of url, push_url, host and path differs from the newest row of the same repository and name; a remote of a newest row whose url is not NONE and that the configuration no longer holds is one row more with url, push_url, host and path NONE. host and path come from the url, in its forms https://[user@]host[:port]/path, ssh://[user@]host[:port]/path and the scp-like [user@]host:path: the host lowercased, the path lowercased without a trailing .git or slash; a local path, a file:// url and a url of no such form have neither. crawl.identity is the user.email the configuration gives there, NONE when it gives none. A shallow repository's crawl says shallow in its note, and its commits are those git holds.</step>
      <step n="4" name="the hosts' repositories and the mirrors">Then, for each remote, in the order of their names, whose host is github.com or bitbucket.org and which has a path: its host_repository (host, path), made the first time a crawl reaches it, found_at then, host_id the host's own id for it (GitHub's id, Bitbucket's uuid) from the repository object that host crawl reads, NONE when it reads none; no second row is ever made for it. A host repository is crawled at most once a pass, by the first worker to reach it; a worker that reaches one already taken in the pass goes on without it and uses what is kept of it. The worker that takes it starts its host crawl (step 21): when the credentials file (credentials.rs), read afresh at each pass, gives the host a token, with the repository object, its first call (step 17, step 18), and, when the host answers it, with the mirror: a bare repository of the crawler's own at &lt;repos folder&gt;/&lt;host&gt;/&lt;path&gt;.git, made with git init --bare and no template when its folder is absent, its mirror row (host_repository, url https://&lt;host&gt;/&lt;path&gt;.git, folder, created_at) written when the database holds none for it, then fetched from its url (step fetch). Every git call on a mirror has on its command line and in its environment: every ref the host advertises taken as it is (the refspec +refs/*:refs/*, the fetch's only one); nothing pruned (fetch.prune and fetch.pruneTags false, no --prune); no maintenance and nothing collected (gc.auto 0, maintenance.auto false, --no-auto-maintenance); every ref's moves logged (core.logAllRefUpdates always) and never expired (gc.reflogExpire and gc.reflogExpireUnreachable never); no hook (core.hooksPath /dev/null); no redirect followed (http.followRedirects false); no submodule (--no-recurse-submodules); the operator's configuration unread (GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL /dev/null, GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM 1) and no credential helper (credential.helper empty); no prompt (GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT 0); the username credential.username, x-access-token for github.com (GitHub uses no username to authenticate, and needs one that is not empty) and x-bitbucket-api-token-auth for bitbucket.org; the password from superx-recon itself, which GIT_ASKPASS names (step 23), with SUPERX_RECON_ASKPASS the host and SUPERX_HOME the instance's home in its environment; no token in any argument, variable, file or url. A fetch alone is not cut off at attr_recon_git_timeout_ms, since a first fetch takes what the repository's size makes it: git ends one whose transfer moves less than a byte a second for a minute (http.lowSpeedLimit 1, http.lowSpeedTime sixty). No mirror is made or fetched when the credentials file gives the host no token or the host does not answer its repository.</step>
      <step n="5" name="the refs and the main line">Every ref the repository holds (branches, remote-tracking refs, tags, notes, the stash, any other) and every ref of the mirrors of its remotes is read with the commit it points at, a tag's peeled to its commit (step refs); crawl.refs_read counts them. Against the refs the crawler read at its previous crawl of the same repository since superx-recon started, a ref is new; moved, when it points at another commit; rewritten, when it moved and its old commit is not an ancestor of its new one (a rebase, an amend, a force-push); or gone: refs_new, refs_moved, refs_rewritten (each also counted moved) and refs_gone; at the repository's first crawl since superx-recon started they are 0, and the crawl's note says the refs were compared with none. The main line is the first of these that resolves: override, the ref attr_recon_mainline_refs names for the repository's name, as git resolves it there; host_default, the default branch the newest repository object kept of the host repository its remote origin names says (GitHub's default_branch, Bitbucket's mainbranch.name), as refs/heads/&lt;branch&gt; in that mirror when the mirror holds it, else as refs/remotes/origin/&lt;branch&gt;; origin_head, the ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD points at; local_branch, refs/heads/main, else refs/heads/master; head, HEAD. crawl.mainline is that ref's full name and mainline_from its word, HEAD and head when none resolves; tip is the commit it points at, NONE when none resolves; a crawl with no tip places no commit on the main line, blames nothing, and says so in its note.</step>
      <step n="6" name="the unreachable commits">The commits git still holds in the repository that neither a ref nor a reflog reaches, as git fsck --unreachable names them (step unreachable), the first attr_recon_unreachable_commits of them in the order fsck names them, join the commits step 7 reads; crawl.unreachable_read counts those fsck named. A mirror is not asked: nothing in it is ever collected.</step>
      <step n="7" name="the commits">The repository's commits are every commit its refs and reflogs reach and every commit the refs and reflogs of its mirrors reach (step log), with those of step 6, each once, committed at or after the crawl's since: the time attr_recon_since_days before the crawl began, since NONE and all of history when it is 0. commits_held is how many they are. Each one the repository holds no commit row for is read once, whole, from the repository's git directory when that holds it, else from the mirror that does (step log): hash; tree; parent_hashes, first parent first, and parents, their count; author and committer, the emails, and author_name and committer_name; authored_at and committed_at, git's times; author_offset and committer_offset, the offsets git recorded beside them in minutes east of UTC; subject, the message's first line as written; body, what follows that line, the empty lines that open it removed, whole, trailers included, NONE when nothing follows; signature, the commit object's gpgsig header whole, its lines joined by newlines without the space that opens each line after the first, NONE when it has none, never verified; patch_id, git patch-id --stable of the commit's change against its first parent (step patches), NONE for a merge and for a commit that changes nothing. Its files are commit_file rows: every path its change against its first parent (a root commit's against the empty tree) touches, with no rename detection, so a rename is a delete and an add; change added, modified, deleted or type_changed as git's A, M, D and T say; added and removed as git's numstat counts them; added_ignoring_space and removed_ignoring_space, the same with --ignore-all-space; binary when git counts no lines in it, its counts 0. A commit's added and removed sum its files. crawl.commits_read counts the commits of commits_held the repository holds rows for once the crawl is done; commits_new, commit_files_new, lines_added_new and lines_removed_new what this crawl wrote; files_read the file changes it read.</step>
      <step n="8" name="the reflogs">Every reflog entry git keeps is one ref_move row: the repository's, each ref's under its common git directory, each checkout's HEAD, the main one's and each worktree's own (checkout, that checkout), the stash's; and each mirror's (mirror, that mirror), read by the worker that took the mirror's host repository in the pass and by no other. An entry is read from its reflog file as git writes it, one line each: the old and the new commit, the identity's name and &lt;email&gt;, the time in seconds and its offset, then a tab and the message. ref is the ref's full name, HEAD for a checkout's; at, the time; old and new, the commits, NONE for git's zero id; identity, the email; action, the message whole; crawl, this crawl; checkout and mirror as said, NONE otherwise. A repository whose refs git keeps in a reftable (as git rev-parse --show-ref-format says) is read with git reflog show instead (step reflog), its old NONE. An entry the table holds is not written again: the crawler reads what the table holds of the repository before it writes, since the engine holds no unique index where a field is NONE. crawl.reflog_read counts the entries read, head_moves_new the checkouts' HEAD entries this crawl wrote. An entry git has expired since stays as written.</step>
      <step n="9" name="the placements">Every commit the repository holds, committed in the crawl's since, has one place at each crawl (step placement): mainline when the main line's tip reaches it; else in_flight when a branch under refs/heads/ that a present checkout of the repository has checked out reaches it; else branch when any other ref of the repository or of its mirrors reaches it; else recovered; whether a ref reaches a commit is read in the git directory that holds the ref. first_parent is true when it is on the main line's first-parent history. A placement row (commit, crawl, place, first_parent) is written when the commit has none or its newest differs; crawl.placements_changed counts them.</step>
      <step n="10" name="what landed">For each commit placed branch, in_flight or recovered, and each path its change against its first parent added or modified, the version it wrote is the blob it gives the path. When a commit of the main line's first-parent history gave the same path the same blob in its own change against its first parent, one landed_via row (commit, path, via, crawl) is written, via the oldest such commit (step landed): the file's work landed by squash or by replay though the commit did not. A row the table holds is not written again; crawl.landed_new counts the new ones.</step>
      <step n="11" name="what survives">When the main line's tip differs from the tip the crawler last blamed whole for the repository since superx-recon started, every file of the tip's tree (step tip) is blamed at the tip (step blame), in the git directory that holds the tip, attr_recon_blames_at_once at once, but a file whose newest change on the main line git counted no lines in (a binary commit_file), a file attr_recon_survival_skip names, and the files past the first attr_recon_blame_files in the tree's order, which crawl.files_left counts. A commit's alive is the lines blame credits it, never more than its added; lines credited to a commit the repository holds no row for are counted in blame_lines_read and make no row. A survival row (commit, crawl, alive) is written for each commit whose alive differs from its newest survival row, and with alive 0 for a commit blame no longer credits whose newest row is above 0. crawl.files_blamed, blame_lines_read, survival_changed and survival_delta, the sum over the new rows of alive less the newest earlier alive, record it. When a blame fails or is cut off, the crawl writes no survival row, its survival_changed and survival_delta are 0, and the tip is not blamed whole, so the next crawl blames it again; the crawl's note names each file whose blame failed, in git's words. A commit's survival is as of its newest survival row's crawl, that crawl's tip and time; a commit with no survival row is not known, never 0.</step>
      <step n="12" name="the checkouts' operations">Where the list reads a present checkout's branch, it also reads what git is in the middle of there, from the state files of the checkout's own git directory, never through a command that touches the checkout: operations, in this order, each that is there of merge (MERGE_HEAD), am (rebase-apply/applying), rebase (rebase-apply without applying, or rebase-merge without interactive), rebase_interactive (rebase-merge/interactive), cherry_pick (CHERRY_PICK_HEAD), revert (REVERT_HEAD) and bisect (BISECT_LOG), empty when none; operation_commits, the commits MERGE_HEAD, CHERRY_PICK_HEAD and REVERT_HEAD name, in that order, NONE when none; rebase_branch, the branch the rebase's head-name names without refs/heads/; rebase_onto, its onto; rebase_done and rebase_left, the commands in rebase-merge/done and in rebase-merge/git-rebase-todo, a line that is empty or starts with # being none; bisect_start, what BISECT_START holds; each NONE where its file is not there. A checkout not present has operations NONE. A checkout_state row is written when one of these, branch, first_branch or present differs from the newest.</step>
      <step n="13" name="commits tied to sessions">Last, a crawl ties commits to sessions. printed: each git_event the database holds, of the action commit, merge, revert or cherry_pick, with a hash, whose dir a checkout of the repository holds, ties the one commit of the repository whose hash begins with it (none when no commit or more than one does) to the session of its tool call's reply, with tool_call that call and at the event's at. pull_request: each git_event of the action pr_created or pr_merged whose url is the url of a pull request of a host repository the repository's remotes name, or, when its url is NONE and a checkout of the repository holds its dir, whose pr_number is the number of one, ties each commit of the repository whose hash begins with a hash that pull request has listed (pull_request_commit) or with its newest merge_sha, to the event's session, with pull_request that pull request, tool_call and at as above. Each (commit, session, via) is one commit_session row, written once.</step>
      <step n="14" name="the crawl and the repos source's record">Each repository crawled is one crawl row: repository, started_at, finished_at, mainline, mainline_from, identity, tip, since; outcome complete; partial when a git call failed or was cut off, or the repository's commits_read differs from its commits_held; unreadable, every count 0, mainline HEAD and mainline_from head, when its git directory is there and git cannot read it; every count of steps 5 to 11; checkouts_read, the repository's checkouts; git_calls, git_failed, git_timeouts and git_ms of its git calls; bytes_read, the bytes they printed; rows_written; note, what could not be read in git's words, what steps 3, 5 and 11 say, and each difference. The repos source's pass_source lists its crawls (crawls); its calls, failed and timed_out count the list's calls and the crawls' git calls; its counts keep t01's and gain commits_held and commits_read, summed over its crawls. It is partial when a crawl is partial or unreadable, its note naming each repository and, for a difference, both numbers.</step>
    </module>

    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/hosts/" name="the hosts' part of the crawler">
      <step n="15" name="the calls">Every call to a host is a GET, through reqwest with rustls (decision http-client), at most one at a time per host across every worker, cut off at attr_recon_host_timeout_ms (step 24). GitHub's go to https://api.github.com with Accept application/vnd.github+json, X-GitHub-Api-Version 2022-11-28 (the version whose pull requests hold merge_commit_sha, which 2026-03-10 removed; it is served until 2028-03-10), User-Agent superx-recon and Authorization Bearer and the github.com token; Bitbucket's to https://api.bitbucket.org/2.0 with Accept application/json, User-Agent superx-recon and Authorization Bearer and the bitbucket.org token. These addresses and headers, the page sizes and the minute below are the hosts' own, constants with an audited skill-allow marker, never parameters; a test points the crawler at a server of its own on the loopback, and a mirror at a repository of its own, through the crawler's arguments, never through a parameter or the environment. A token goes to its own host's address alone, never into a path or a query. A list is paged as the host gives it: GitHub's by the URL its link header names rel="next", Bitbucket's by its answer's next, each followed as given until there is none; a list's first page as the crawler asks it sets per_page a hundred on GitHub and pagelen fifty on Bitbucket, the most each list of theirs takes. A redirect is followed when it keeps the scheme, the host and the port, the token with it; any other is not followed, and the call fails. When the newest answer to the same path, query included, gave an etag in a host crawl that ended complete, the etag is sent back as If-None-Match, and an answer HTTP 304 is not_modified: what that path held is unchanged, and nothing of it is read again; after a host crawl that did not end complete, its paths are asked again whole. A page of a list answered HTTP 304 is followed by the page the newest earlier answer to it named next, asked with that page's own etag. The rate limit is read from each answer: GitHub's x-ratelimit-limit, x-ratelimit-remaining and x-ratelimit-reset (UTC epoch seconds), Bitbucket's X-RateLimit-Limit, and retry-after in seconds. A call answered HTTP 429, or HTTP 403 with x-ratelimit-remaining 0, a retry-after, or a message that says a rate limit was exceeded, is refused for the rate: the crawler waits retry-after seconds when the answer gives them, else until x-ratelimit-reset when x-ratelimit-remaining is 0, else one minute, and asks again; a call made while the host's newest answer said x-ratelimit-remaining 0 waits until its reset first; each wait is counted, and a stop ends it. An answer HTTP 400, HTTP 403 or HTTP 404 to a call after the host repository's own (steps 17 and 18) that is not refused for the rate is the host's answer: what the host gives this token for that path, such as settings only an admin may read, or the commits of a pull request whose branch is gone. It holds no object and is no failure; its call is kept as every call is, and its host crawl's note names its path and status (step 21). Every path is asked at every host crawl whatever the token can read, so a token given more scopes reads more at the next host crawl, with nothing rebuilt or restarted. Every call is a host_call row: host_crawl; method GET; path, the path and query asked, never the host and never a token; started_at; ms; status, NONE when no answer came; not_modified; bytes, the answer's body; etag; rate_limit, rate_remaining, rate_reset; retry_after_ms; objects, the objects its answer held; next, whether a page followed; error, NONE for an answer HTTP 304 and for an answer HTTP 200 that is read, else what went wrong, whole: the transport's error; for an answer HTTP 200 that is unread (step 16), `an answer not of the shape asked: ` and the answer's body; else the status and the answer's body.</step>
      <step n="16" name="the objects">An answer's objects are the items of a GitHub array, the items of the one list a GitHub answer wraps beside its total_count (workflow_runs, jobs, environments, check_suites, check_runs), the values of a Bitbucket page, or the answer itself when it is one object. Each is a host_object of the kind its call gives (steps 17 and 18), with the key given there; its hash the SHA-256, in lowercase hexadecimal, of the object as serde_json writes a Value, compact, every object's keys in their sorted order; a row (host_repository, kind, key, object whole, hash, call, at, when the answer came) when the host repository holds none of that kind, key and hash: host_crawl.objects_new counts those of a key it held none of, objects_changed the others. Every account an object holds at any depth is a host_object of the kind user, its key the account's id: on GitHub an object with login, id and type; on Bitbucket an object with uuid and the type user, team or app_user. The first time a host names an account it is a host_user row: host; host_id, GitHub's id or Bitbucket's uuid; login, GitHub's login or Bitbucket's nickname; name, GitHub's name or Bitbucket's display_name; kind, its type as written; found_at; each NONE where the object has none. An answer that is not JSON, or not of the shape its call gives, is unread: counted in the source's unread, its call failed, its error as step 15 gives it.</step>
      <step n="17" name="GitHub">A host repository on github.com, path &lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;, is asked, in this order, each under /repos/&lt;path&gt;: itself, kind repository, key id, an answer HTTP 401, HTTP 403 or HTTP 404 making the host crawl unreadable with nothing more asked; /pulls?state=all&amp;sort=created&amp;direction=asc, whose objects are not kept, since each pull request is kept whole as its own answer, /pulls/&lt;number&gt;, kind pull_request, key id, asked when no pull_request object of that id is kept or the list's updated_at differs from the newest kept one's, and with it /pulls/&lt;number&gt;/commits, kind pull_request_commit, key sha, /pulls/&lt;number&gt;/files, kind pull_request_file, key &lt;number&gt;/&lt;filename&gt;, /pulls/&lt;number&gt;/reviews, kind review, key id, and its timeline; /issues?state=all&amp;sort=created&amp;direction=asc, kind issue, key id, a pull request being an issue there too, and for each issue object new or changed, and each pull request asked, /issues/&lt;number&gt;/timeline, kind timeline_event, key its id, else a committed event's sha, else its own hash; /issues/comments?sort=created&amp;direction=asc, kind issue_comment, key id; /pulls/comments?sort=created&amp;direction=asc, kind review_comment, key id; /issues/events, kind issue_event, key id; /actions/runs, kind workflow_run, key id, and for each run new or changed /actions/runs/&lt;id&gt;/jobs?filter=all, kind workflow_job, key id; /commits/&lt;sha&gt;/statuses, kind commit_status, key id, for the head commit of every open pull request and the main line's tip at every host crawl, for every other commit a pull request has listed and every commit of the main line's first-parent history at the first host crawl that knows it, and again at each host crawl while a status it holds is pending; /commits/&lt;sha&gt;/check-suites, kind check_suite, key id, and then /commits/&lt;sha&gt;/check-runs?filter=all, kind check_run, key id, for the same commits at the same host crawls as the statuses, and again at each host crawl while a suite or run it holds is not completed; /deployments, kind deployment, key id, and for every deployment /deployments/&lt;id&gt;/statuses, kind deployment_status, key id; /environments, kind environment, key id; /releases, kind release, key id; /rulesets, whose objects are not kept, and /rulesets/&lt;id&gt;, kind ruleset, key id, asked when none of that id is kept or the list's updated_at differs; /branches/&lt;the repository's default_branch&gt;/protection, kind branch_protection, key the branch's name, which GitHub answers HTTP 404, the host's answer (step 15), when the branch is not protected and when the token is not an admin of the repository. A token that cannot call GitHub's Checks API (a fine-grained token cannot; GitHub names it among the gaps of fine-grained tokens) is given the host's answer for the check suites and runs, and they are asked again at the next host crawl.</step>
      <step n="18" name="Bitbucket">A host repository on bitbucket.org, path &lt;workspace&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;, is asked, in this order, each under /repositories/&lt;path&gt;: itself, kind repository, key uuid, an answer HTTP 401, HTTP 403 or HTTP 404 making the host crawl unreadable with nothing more asked; /pullrequests?state=OPEN&amp;state=MERGED&amp;state=DECLINED&amp;state=SUPERSEDED&amp;sort=created_on, whose objects are not kept, and /pullrequests/&lt;id&gt;, kind pull_request, key id, asked when no pull_request object of that id is kept or the list's updated_on differs from the newest kept one's, and with it /pullrequests/&lt;id&gt;/commits, kind pull_request_commit, key hash, /pullrequests/&lt;id&gt;/diffstat, which the host redirects within itself, kind pull_request_file, key &lt;id&gt;/&lt;its new path, else its old&gt;, /pullrequests/&lt;id&gt;/comments, kind pull_request_comment, key id, /pullrequests/&lt;id&gt;/activity, kind pull_request_activity, key its own hash, and /pullrequests/&lt;id&gt;/tasks, kind pull_request_task, key id; /commit/&lt;hash&gt;/statuses, kind commit_status, key &lt;hash&gt;/&lt;its key&gt;, for the same commits at the same crawls as on GitHub, a status pending while its state is INPROGRESS; /pipelines?sort=-created_on, kind pipeline, key uuid, and for each pipeline new or changed /pipelines/&lt;uuid&gt;/steps, kind pipeline_step, key uuid, a uuid's braces written %7B and %7D in a path; /deployments, kind deployment, key uuid; /environments, kind environment, key uuid; /branch-restrictions, kind branch_restriction, key id; /default-reviewers, kind default_reviewer, key uuid. Issues are not asked: Bitbucket removed its issue tracker and its API on 2026-08-20.</step>
      <step n="19" name="the pull requests">A pull_request object makes its pull_request row the first time (host_repository, number): number, GitHub's number or Bitbucket's id; host_id, GitHub's id or Bitbucket's id; author, the host_user of GitHub's user or of Bitbucket's author; created_at, GitHub's created_at or Bitbucket's created_on; source_branch and target_branch, GitHub's head.ref and base.ref or Bitbucket's source.branch.name and destination.branch.name; source_path, the repository its source branch lives in (GitHub's head.repo.full_name, Bitbucket's source.repository.full_name, lowercased) when that is not the host repository, else NONE; url, GitHub's html_url or Bitbucket's links.html.href; object. Each pull_request object read also makes a pull_request_state row when one of these differs from the newest: state as written; merged, GitHub's merged, Bitbucket's state MERGED; draft; title; head_sha and base_sha, GitHub's head.sha and base.sha, Bitbucket's source.commit.hash and destination.commit.hash; merge_sha, GitHub's merge_commit_sha when merged is true (before a merge it names a trial merge), Bitbucket's merge_commit.hash; merged_at, GitHub's, NONE on Bitbucket; merged_by, GitHub's merged_by, Bitbucket's closed_by when the state is MERGED; closed_at, GitHub's, NONE on Bitbucket; closed_by, on GitHub the actor of the newest closed event of its timeline as kept, on Bitbucket its closed_by; reason, Bitbucket's; additions, deletions, changed_files, commits, comments and review_comments, GitHub's own counts, and on Bitbucket comment_count as comments, the others NONE; updated_at, GitHub's updated_at or Bitbucket's updated_on; object, the object it was read from. Each reading of a pull request's commits whose every page was answered HTTP 200 or HTTP 304 makes pull_request_commit rows, and one with a page that failed or that the host gave its answer to (step 15) makes none: a commit listed for the first time, present true and position its place in the list from 0; a commit listed before and absent now, present false, position NONE; a commit absent before and listed again, present true; object, its pull_request_commit object. GitHub lists two hundred and fifty commits of a pull request at most: when its own count of commits is more than its list gave, no commit is marked absent in that reading.</step>
      <step n="20" name="the CI runs">A check_suite, check_run, workflow_run, workflow_job or commit_status object of GitHub, and a pipeline, pipeline_step or commit_status object of Bitbucket, makes its ci_run row the first time (host_repository, kind, host_id), kind the object's kind: a check suite, host_id its id, name its app's name, commit_sha its head_sha, ref its head_branch, pull_request the first of its pull_requests whose number is a pull request of the host repository, url its url, created_at its created_at; a check run, host_id its id, name its name, commit_sha its head_sha, parent its suite's ci_run by check_suite.id, pull_request as a suite's, url its html_url; a workflow run, host_id its id, name its name, commit_sha its head_sha, ref its head_branch, pull_request the first of its pull_requests whose number is a pull request of the host repository, url its html_url, created_at its created_at; a workflow job, host_id its id, name its name, commit_sha its head_sha, ref its head_branch, parent its run's ci_run by run_id, url its html_url, created_at its created_at; a GitHub commit status, host_id its id, name its context, commit_sha the commit asked, url its target_url, created_at its created_at; a pipeline, host_id its uuid, name its target.selector.pattern, commit_sha its target.commit.hash, ref its target.ref_name, pull_request the pull request its target.pullrequest.id names where its target names one, created_at its created_on; a pipeline step, host_id its uuid, name its name where it has one, parent its pipeline's ci_run, commit_sha and ref its pipeline's; a Bitbucket commit status, host_id &lt;hash&gt;/&lt;its key&gt;, name its name, commit_sha the commit asked, ref its refname, url its url, created_at its created_on; object; every other column NONE. Each such object read also makes a ci_run_state row when one of these differs from the newest: status and conclusion, a check suite's and check run's, a GitHub run's and job's status and conclusion, a commit status's state as status and conclusion NONE, a pipeline's and a step's state.name and state.result.name; started_at, a check run's started_at, a run's run_started_at, a job's started_at, a step's started_on; completed_at, a check run's completed_at, a job's completed_at, a pipeline's and a step's completed_on; attempt, a run's and a job's run_attempt; number, a run's run_number, a pipeline's build_number; actor, the host_user of a run's triggering_actor or a pipeline's creator; runner, a job's runner_name; each NONE where the object has none; and object, the object it was read from, which alone never makes a row.</step>
      <step n="21" name="the host crawl and the hosts source's record">Each host repository a worker crawls is one host_crawl row, written after the crawl row it ran beside and before the rows that point to it: host_repository; crawl; started_at, finished_at; calls, not_modified, failed, rate_waits, waited_ms, objects_new, objects_changed, rows_written; outcome no_credentials, nothing asked, when the credentials file gives the host no token, its note naming why (none, refused or unreadable as credentials.rs answers, or no token); unreadable as steps 17 and 18 say; partial when a call failed or was cut off, or a list's stated total differs from what was read of it, a host's answer (step 15) being no failure; complete otherwise; note, each path that failed with its status, each path the host gave its answer to with its status, and each difference. A list states its total in GitHub's total_count beside workflow_runs, jobs, environments, check_suites and check_runs, and in Bitbucket's size where a page gives it; when every page of it was answered HTTP 200 in this crawl, the objects read on it are compared with that total. The hosts source is a pass_source row of its own, source hosts: items, the host repositories crawled; unread; crawls empty; calls, failed and timed_out, its host calls; rows; counts objects_new, objects_changed, not_modified, rate_waits, waited_ms, totals_stated and totals_read, the totals compared and the objects read on those lists; outcome partial when a host crawl is partial, its note naming each and its difference; a host crawl unreadable or without credentials leaves the source complete and is named in its note.</step>
    </module>

    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/" name="the crate">
      <step n="22" name="the pass">A pass runs the lines source first, as today; then the repos source with its workers, each crawling beside its repository the host repositories it takes; then it records itself, a pass_source row each for lines, repos and hosts, its outcome partial when one of them is, as today. The pass's log line also names hosts_items and host_calls.</step>
      <step n="23" name="the askpass">superx-recon started with SUPERX_RECON_ASKPASS in its environment and one argument is git's askpass: it reads no command, starts nothing and logs nothing, and answers the prompt git gives as that argument. When the prompt asks for the password of https://&lt;host&gt; or https://&lt;user&gt;@&lt;host&gt;, that host is the one SUPERX_RECON_ASKPASS names and is github.com or bitbucket.org, and the credentials file of the home SUPERX_HOME names gives that host a token (credentials.rs), it prints the token and a newline to its standard output and exits 0; for any other prompt, or with no token, it prints nothing there, says why on its error output without a token, and exits 1.</step>
      <step n="24" name="the parameters">Two parameters join the module's, after today's, in this order: attr_recon_repos_at_once, the repositories crawled at once, a whole number, 1 or more, its fallback no limit, shown all; attr_recon_host_timeout_ms, the milliseconds a host call may take before it is cut off, a whole number, 1 or more, its fallback the fallback of attr_recon_git_timeout_ms. Each is reported by name when it is set to a value of the wrong shape, as today's are.</step>
      <step n="25" name="the status">In status's last pass, the repos source's line is `&lt;items&gt; read, &lt;unread&gt; in a shape it does not know · &lt;commits_read&gt; commits read of &lt;commits_held&gt; held · &lt;calls&gt; calls, &lt;failed&gt; failed, &lt;timed_out&gt; cut off · &lt;rows&gt; rows` and the hosts source's `&lt;items&gt; read, &lt;unread&gt; in a shape it does not know · &lt;totals_read&gt; objects read of &lt;totals_stated&gt; stated · &lt;calls&gt; calls, &lt;failed&gt; failed, &lt;timed_out&gt; cut off, &lt;rate_waits&gt; waits · &lt;rows&gt; rows`, each with its outcome after it as today; the lines source's line stays as t02 left it.</step>
      <step n="26" name="the dependencies">crates/superx-mod-recon/Cargo.toml gains reqwest 0.13, its default features off and its feature rustls on, and sha2 0.10, the version the workspace's lock holds; Cargo.lock follows. No other dependency.</step>

      <how>cargo test, on SurrealDB's in-memory engine, the kernel's schema and the structure applied as the merged tests apply them.       <prove step="1">a pass crawls in depth every repository at a full pass, and at another pass the new ones and those a git event of the pass's lines names by its dir, and no other; an excluded repository never</prove>
      <prove step="1">several repositories crawled with attr_recon_repos_at_once 1 and at its fallback write the same facts, each crawl's rows written after its crawl row</prove>
      <prove step="1">a repository with no present checkout is crawled through its common git directory, and one whose common git directory is gone is not crawled and keeps what the crawler holds of it</prove>
      <prove step="2">every git call in an operator's repository carries the overrides, no diff driver and no optional lock, never a %G placeholder, a fetch or a write, and is a git_call row with its step, dir, args as run, exit, timed_out, bytes_out, lines_out and stderr whole</prove>
      <prove step="3">each remote's url, push url, host and path is a remote_state row when it changes and one with url NONE once it is gone; the https, ssh and scp-like forms give host and path lowercased without .git, a local path neither; identity is the configured email</prove>
      <prove step="3">a shallow repository's crawl says shallow in its note and reads the commits git holds</prove>

      <prove step="5">refs are counted new, moved, rewritten and gone against the previous crawl, and not compared at a repository's first crawl since start</prove>
      <prove step="5">the main line is chosen override, then host_default from the mirror's branch or else origin's, then origin_head, local_branch and head, each recorded with its word, and with no tip nothing is placed on it</prove>
      <prove step="6">a commit no ref and no reflog reaches is read and placed recovered, at most attr_recon_unreachable_commits of them</prove>
      <prove step="7">a commit takes its hash, tree, parents, emails, names, times, offsets in minutes, subject, body with its trailers, signature whole and patch_id, a cherry-picked copy sharing its original's patch_id and a merge and an empty commit having none; a commit only a mirror holds is read from the mirror; attr_recon_since_days leaves older commits unread</prove>
      <prove step="7">a commit's files are added, modified, deleted and type_changed, a rename a delete and an add, a binary's counts 0, and the counts ignoring space beside the others; a merge's against its first parent; its added and removed their sums</prove>
      <prove step="8">every reflog entry of a branch, a remote-tracking ref, each checkout's HEAD, the stash and a mirror's ref is one ref_move row with its old, new, identity, action and at, the zero id NONE, never written twice across passes, and still there after git expires it</prove>
      <prove step="8">a repository whose refs git keeps in a reftable has its reflog entries read with git reflog show, old NONE</prove>
      <prove step="9">commits are placed mainline, in_flight, branch and recovered with first_parent, and a placement row is written only when it changes</prove>
      <prove step="10">the commits of a squash-merged branch land via the squash commit for each file whose version it carries, and not for a file the squash wrote otherwise</prove>
      <prove step="11">survival credits each commit the lines blame gives it at the tip, never above its added; binaries, skipped paths and the files past attr_recon_blame_files are not blamed, the last counted in files_left; nothing is blamed while the tip stays, and a commit no longer credited takes alive 0</prove>
      <prove step="11">a blame that fails or is cut off writes no survival row from its crawl, which is partial and names the file, and the next crawl blames the same tip again</prove>
      <prove step="12">a checkout mid-merge, mid-rebase, mid-interactive-rebase with its commands done and left, mid-cherry-pick, mid-revert, mid-bisect and mid-am says so in its checkout_state, and one in the middle of nothing has empty operations</prove>
      <prove step="12">a checkout not present has operations NONE</prove>

      <prove step="15">every host call is a GET with the host's headers and Bearer token, sent only to its host, the token never in a path, a query, a row or a log line; and is a host_call row with each column</prove>

      <prove step="15">a call made while the host's newest answer said none of its limit is left waits until its reset first</prove>

  <piece>The watcher task. The module's second part, beside the crawler and sharing nothing that runs with it (decision watcher): its own loop, every attr_recon_watch_secs, follows the newest captured lines of every session and holds one watch per session in memory. It hears the operator's words, orders, approvals, questions, status asks, stops, corrections and escalations, and keeps the orders in force; it judges every act of the agent against them and, for a session building from a task file, against that file: what it owns, what it forbids, its version and its words. It finds each session's memory folder. It reports, as it happens, an agent that vanished (quiet, behind a job, stalled, a question unanswered, a status asked, waiting on the operator), one that does not follow (it acted on a question, an unordered action, unordered work, a stop not honoured, a repeated order) and trouble ahead (a long estimate, a long run, struggling, memory written, a compaction, frustration). The Watcher tab of the module's page shows every session, what it is doing this second, its words and the operator's, the orders in force, its memory and every report, and notifies in the browser. It writes nothing: no row, no file, no parameter; the structure is unchanged.</piece>

  <before>Merged on main when this task starts: t01 (#536), t02 (#537), t03 (#538) and t04 (#539): the module superx-mod-recon, its crawler crawling the lines, the repositories and the hosts into the structure, and its page on the module's own URL with the tabs Crawler and Results; and #562 (PR #563): the page's header with its trail of breadcrumbs, SuperX, Recon and the tab, its first crumb the core dashboard's URL that /api/ping gives. server.rs serves the page and its routes under /api/ and answers only a request that names it (its Host check); the Crawler tab lists and sets the parameters by group (when, where, how much, listens, measures) through /api/params, as params.rs's about gives them; superx-recon run starts the crawler beside the page and waits for it at the stop. The kernel captures every Claude Code line whole (crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs): a user or assistant line as a message row, its raw_text beside its parsed copy since #546; every other line as a transcript_event, its payload holding session, kind, file, line and raw_text; a file beside the transcripts as a transcript_event of kind file.   <build>
    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/watcher/" name="the watcher">
      <step n="1" name="its own part">The watcher is a part of the module of its own, src/watcher/, beside the crawler, sharing nothing that runs with it (rules crawler-is-the-reader): no file under src/watcher/ names crate::crawler or crate::results, and no file under src/crawler/ or src/results/ names crate::watcher. It reads the kernel's database with queries of its own, SELECT alone (step 3; rules kernel-read-only), reads the task files and the memory folders as files, read-only (steps 11 and 13), keeps what it judges in memory, and writes nothing: no row in any database, no file, no parameter, no registry entry. What it has from the rest of the module is what every part has: the kernel's connection the process opened, the instance, and the parameters as params.rs resolves them (step 15). It judges Claude Code's lines, the agent the kernel names claude_code (AGENT_NAME of the adapter); another agent's lines are counted (step 3) and judged nowhere.</step>
      <step n="2" name="its loop">superx-recon run starts the watcher beside the crawler (step 17). It looks every attr_recon_watch_secs seconds. Each look resolves the parameters and the projects roots (step 13); reads the lines captured since the last look (step 3); gives each line to its session's watch, in the order of their ids (steps 4 to 13); judges every watch at the look's time for what time alone makes (steps 8 and 10); takes from each watch what the window leaves behind (step 5); reads again each task file whose modification time changed (step 11); lists each memory folder (step 13); and publishes what it holds as its snapshot (step 14), which the routes answer from without waiting on a look. While attr_recon_watch_secs is 0 it reads nothing and holds what it held, and looks at the parameter again every minute, a constant with an audited skill-allow marker; set again, it reads every line captured since its last look. A look that cannot read, because the kernel's database or a parameter does not answer, changes nothing the watcher holds and is logged at warn with the error, once until a look reads again; the next look tries again from where the last look that read stood (rules capture-first). A stop ends the watcher where it is, in its wait or in a look, and nothing it holds outlives the process. It logs at the module's log target, recon: that it started, with its window; a look that could not read; that it stopped. A look judges at the clock's time, and can be given the time it judges at, so a test judges at a time of its own and waits for nothing.</step>
      <step n="3" name="the lines it follows">At its start the watcher reads back the window: every line captured from attr_recon_watch_window_secs before its start on, so it never starts blind; then each look reads every line captured since. A line is a row the kernel captured: in message, a conversation line, with its id, session, agent, raw_text and raw; in telemetry_stream, a row whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, with its id, agent and payload. Each table is read a page of rows at a time, in the order of their ids, the UUIDv7 the kernel set when it captured the line, with SELECT … FROM type::record('&lt;table&gt;', $from&gt;..) LIMIT $n, never through a time or a datetime index; a page of telemetry_stream reads its rows' ids and whether each is a transcript_event, then those rows whole by their ids, since most of that stream is not a line. The page sizes are constants with audited skill-allow markers. Each look starts a minute before the newest id it has read in each table, a constant with an audited skill-allow marker, and skips every id it has read already, so a line the kernel committed after a newer one is read, and every line is read once. The lines of both tables are given to the watches in the order of their ids. A line is its raw_text parsed as JSON; a row that holds no raw_text is read from the parsed copy the kernel kept, a message's raw or a payload's line. A row's agent is the name the kernel gives its agent entity; its session is the session entity a message row names, or, for a telemetry row, the session the kernel names &lt;agent&gt;/&lt;key&gt;, the key being the payload's session; both come from kernel.list_named_entities (node_agent with attr_agent_descriptor, node_session with attr_session_descriptor), read again when a row names an agent or a session not yet known, at most once a look. A transcript_event of kind file is a file beside the transcripts, not a line, and is skipped. A session's transcript is the file the payload of its newest transcript_event names. A look counts the lines it read and, apart, by why, the rows it could not read: no line, when a row holds neither raw_text nor a parsed copy or its raw_text is not JSON; no session, when the names give none; another agent, by the agent's name. These are counted and shown (step 14), never guessed at (rules nothing-dropped). A line's time is the timestamp its agent wrote in it, else the time its id holds.</step>
      <step n="4" name="what a line says">Of a Claude Code line the watcher reads, as the adapter captured it (crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs): from a user line (type user), each tool_result block of its content, the result of the call its tool_use_id names: failed when its is_error is true, and then its error, the block's content as text, whole (a string, or its text parts joined by newlines); and the lines it wrote: in the line's toolUseResult, the lines of its structuredPatch hunks that start with + and not with +++, else, for a toolUseResult of type create, the lines of its content. The line's text, its content when that is a string, else its text blocks joined by newlines, when it is not empty: when the line's origin.kind is task-notification, the end of a background job, the call its &lt;tool-use-id&gt; names, ending as its &lt;status&gt; says, each the text between its tags, trimmed; else, unless the line is isSidechain, isMeta or isCompactSummary, the operator's message: an interrupt when it starts with [Request interrupted by user; a shell command when it starts with &lt;bash-input&gt;; a slash command when it starts with &lt;command-name&gt;; else typed, when its origin.kind is human, or it has no origin and does not start with &lt;; any other text is no one's message. From an attachment line (type attachment) whose attachment.type is queued_command, the operator's message typed while the agent worked, its text the attachment's prompt (a string, or its parts' text joined by newlines): queued when attachment.origin.kind is human, a shell or slash command by its start as above; a shell command when it has no origin and starts with &lt;bash-input&gt;; any other is no one's. A subagent's lines are captured into the session that ran it, marked isSidechain. From an assistant line (type assistant), its model, message.model, unless it starts with &lt; or the line is isSidechain; each text block that is not empty, unless the line is isSidechain, the agent's words to the operator; each tool_use block, a call: its id; its tool, the block's name; what it was called on, the first its input holds of command, file_path, notebook_path, pattern, url, query, description and prompt, else the input whole as JSON; its input whole; background, when the input's run_in_background is true or the tool is Monitor; and the paths it writes: an Edit, Write, MultiEdit or NotebookEdit call's file_path or notebook_path; and a Bash call's targets: the word after each &gt; or &gt;&gt;, one that starts with &amp; aside; each word after a tee to the end of its command, the next |, ;, &amp;&amp;, || or newline, that does not start with -; and for a sed with an option that starts with -i, each word of its command after the first that does not start with -, the script, that does not start with -. A command's words are split at white space, their quotes set aside; a target is read as an absolute path, with the environment's HOME for a ~ that starts it, or joined to the line's folder. From a system line (type system) that is not isSidechain: a turn end, subtype turn_duration; a compaction, compact_boundary; the agent's own recap, away_summary, its content whole. A line's folder is the cwd it names. Every other line says nothing to the watcher. Every text is kept whole, as written.</step>
      <step n="5" name="one watch per session">Each session the lines name has one watch, in memory. It holds, of its session's lines in the window, what step 4 reads from them, whole and in their order, and what steps 6 to 13 make of them: its agent and its name, &lt;agent&gt;/&lt;key&gt;; its model, the newest; its folder, the newest a line names; the time of its newest line; its transcript (step 3); the operator's messages with their classes and marks; the agent's words; each call with its result; each background job running, a background call whose end no task notification has given; its turn, ended from a turn end until the next operator's message, task notification or assistant line, working otherwise; its orders in force; the task file it is held to; its memory folder; its reports. The window is the span the watcher watches: a line captured more than attr_recon_watch_window_secs before the look leaves its watch, with what was made of it, its reports included, but for the orders in force and the task file the session is held to, which stay while the watch holds any line (steps 7 and 11); a watch that holds no line leaves the watcher. Every line stays in the kernel, and the crawler crawls them all. When more watches are held than attr_recon_watch_sessions allows, those whose newest line is oldest leave first; its fallback is no limit (rules nothing-dropped). A session's state at the look's time is the first that holds of: stalled (step 8); working, while its turn works; behind a job, its turn ended with a background job of its running; waiting on you, its turn ended on its question (step 8); idle.</step>
      <step n="6" name="the operator's words">Each typed or queued message of the operator's, and each interrupt, is classed by the first that holds of: stop, an interrupt, or a message holding a word of attr_recon_stop_words; status ask, one holding a word or phrase of attr_recon_status_words; question, one that ends with ? once its trailing white space is set aside, or whose first word is one of attr_recon_question_words; approval, one whose first word or phrase is one of attr_recon_yes_words; order, any other. A text's words are its runs of letters, digits, apostrophes and hyphens, lowercased; a word of a list holds when the text has it as a word, a phrase when the text has its words in a row. Each message is also marked a correction when it holds a word or phrase of attr_recon_correction_words, and an escalation when one of its words starts with an entry of attr_recon_escalation_words, as params.rs says each list is read. A shell or slash command is the operator's own act: kept and shown whole, neither classed nor marked.</step>
      <step n="7" name="the orders in force">A session's orders in force are the words its agent may act on. The operator's newest order or approval sets them: an order, its words; an approval, its words and the agent's words to the operator between the operator's message before it and the approval, the proposal it approved. A stop, a status ask or a question leaves them as they were. When the session is held to a task file (step 11), the file's words join them. They stay with the watch while it holds any line, though the message that made them left the window. A session with no order known holds none: it judges no call an unordered action and no words unordered work (step 9), and it says so.</step>
      <step n="8" name="it vanished">Reports of an agent the operator cannot see. Quiet, warn: at a call made attr_recon_watch_quiet_secs or more after the newer of the agent's newest words to the operator and the operator's newest message, when the calls made since then number attr_recon_watch_quiet_calls or more; once, until the agent's next words or the operator's next message; its time, that call's. Behind a job, warn: at a look attr_recon_watch_behind_secs or more after a turn end at which a background job of the session's ran, when neither the agent's words nor an operator's message came since the turn end; once a turn end; its time, the turn end's plus attr_recon_watch_behind_secs. Stalled, alert: its turn works, no call of the turn is running (each has its result, a background call aside), and its newest line is at least attr_recon_watch_quiet_secs before the look; once, until its next line; its time, the newest line's plus attr_recon_watch_quiet_secs. Question unanswered, alert: a turn ended after the operator's question or status ask with no words of the agent's between them; once a message; its time, the turn end's. Status asked, alert: each status ask; its time, the message's; its words, the message whole. Waiting on you, info: a turn ended and the agent's newest words in that turn end with ? once trailing white space is set aside; once a turn end; its time, the turn end's; its words, those words whole.</step>
      <step n="9" name="it isn't following you">An act is a call of Edit, Write, MultiEdit or NotebookEdit, or a call that holds an effect: a call holds the effect of each pattern of attr_recon_watch_effects found, as written, in its tool's name or in what it was called on. Acted on a question, alert: after the operator's question, before the agent's next words to the operator, the session made an act; once a question; its time, the act's; its words, the question whole, and the act's tool and what. Unordered action, alert: a call holds an effect none of whose words the orders in force hold, as step 6 reads words and phrases; once a call, naming each such pattern; its time, the call's; its words, what the call was called on, whole, and the orders in force. Unordered work, alert: the agent's words to the operator hold a word or phrase of attr_recon_watch_scope_words the orders in force do not hold; once a text block, naming those words; its time, the block's; its words, the block whole. Stop not honoured, alert: after a stop, before the agent's next words to the operator, the session made attr_recon_watch_stop_calls calls; once a stop; its time, the call's that made the count. Repeated order, alert: an operator's typed or queued message shares with an earlier one, sent within attr_recon_watch_repeat_secs before it, at least attr_recon_watch_repeat_share percent of the shorter one's distinct words, the shorter one having at least attr_recon_watch_order_words of them; once a message; its time, the message's; its words, both messages whole.</step>
      <step n="10" name="trouble ahead">Long estimate, warn: the agent's words to the operator hold a word or phrase of attr_recon_watch_long_words; once a text block; its time, the block's; its words, the block whole. Long run, warn: at a call made attr_recon_watch_run_secs or more after the operator's newest message, when the calls made since it number attr_recon_watch_run_calls or more; once a message, naming the run's calls by tool and the lines its results wrote; its time, that call's. Struggling, warn: attr_recon_watch_failures results in a row, in the order they came, failed; once, until a result that did not fail; its time, the result's that made the count; its words, each failure's error whole. Memory written, warn: a call writes a path in a memory folder (step 13); once a call; its time, the call's; its words, the path and the call's input whole. Compacted, info: a compaction; its time, the line's. Frustration, alert: of the operator's newest attr_recon_watch_mood_prompts typed or queued messages, once there are that many, more than half are marked escalations; at the message that makes it so, and again at each message after which more of them are; naming how many of how many; its time, the message's.</step>
      <step n="11" name="the task file it executes">A session is held to a task file from the operator's newest typed or queued message that names one: a run of the message's characters without white space that holds an entry of attr_recon_watch_spec_paths followed by a file name ending in .xml, brackets and quotes at its start and commas, periods, colons, brackets and quotes at its end set aside. The run, from the entry on, is the file's path in its checkout; the checkout is the run's part before the entry when the run is an absolute path, else the session's folder or the nearest of its ancestors that holds a file at that path. The watcher reads the file as a file, read-only, and parses it as XML: its root element's version attribute; the text of its owns and of its forbid; its words, the text of its start, of each step and cmd, and of its done. Each entry of owns and of forbid, between semicolons, gives its paths: each run of the entry's characters without white space that holds a slash or ends in a file name with an extension, brackets and quotes at its start and commas, periods, colons, brackets and quotes at its end set aside, relative to the checkout; a path ending in a slash matches every path under that folder, ** matches any run of folders, * any characters but a slash, and every other character itself. A file that cannot be read or parsed holds the session to nothing, and the session shows its path and why, in the error's own words. At each look a file whose modification time changed is read again.</step>
      <step n="12" name="judged against the task file">A session held to a task file is judged against it too. A call writes a path in the checkout when a path it writes (step 4) lies under the checkout's folder. Forbidden file, alert: a call writes a path in the checkout that a forbid path matches and no owns path matches; once a call, naming the path and its forbid entry; its time, the call's. Outside what it owns, alert: a call writes a path in the checkout that no owns path and no forbid path matches; once a call, naming the path; its time, the call's. Off the spec's version, warn: the file read again holds a version other than the one it held when the session was first held to it; once a version, naming both; its time, the look's. The operator's words win over the file's: acted on a question and stop not honoured are judged whatever the file's words order, and their report says when the file's words order the act. The session shows the task file it is held to: its path, its version, the version it held when the session was first held to it, and its owns and forbid entries.</step>
      <step n="13" name="its memory">The projects roots are those the Claude Code adapter captures from: the parameter attr_claude_code_projects_root (PROJECTS_ROOT_PARAM) on the adapter adapter_claude_code (ADAPTER_NAME), one path or a list, read at each look with the kernel's own verbs, find_module_by_name with NodeKind::Adapter and get_parameter; when it is not set, the folder .claude/projects under the environment's HOME, the adapter's own fallback (projects_roots). A session's project folder is the root's child folder on its transcript's path, under the projects root that holds the transcript; its memory folder is the folder memory in it; it has none when no root holds its transcript, and then says so. At each look the watcher lists each memory folder's files, as files, read-only: each file's name, its size in bytes, its modification time, and its description, the value of a description: line in the frontmatter that opens the file between two lines of ---, else none. A path is in a memory folder when it lies in the folder memory of a folder that is a projects root's child.</step>
      <step n="14" name="the reports and the snapshot">A report holds: its id, the session's id, its kind and its time joined, the same at every look, and after a restart for every report the lines read back make again, so no report is made twice and the page tells a new one by its id; its session; its kind, one of quiet, behind_a_job, stalled, question_unanswered, status_asked, waiting_on_you (step 8), acted_on_question, unordered_action, unordered_work, stop_not_honoured, repeated_order (step 9), long_estimate, long_run, struggling, memory_written, compacted, frustration (step 10), forbidden_file, outside_owns, spec_version_changed (step 12); its level, info, warn or alert as its step gives it; its time; what it says, one sentence of the facts its step names; and its words, whole, each with whose they are, the operator's, the agent's or a call's: nothing is cut (rules nothing-dropped), and the page clamps what it shows. The snapshot, published at each look, holds: the look's time; attr_recon_watch_secs and whether the watcher is paused; the window's start; the lines read since the watcher started, and the rows it could not read by why and by agent (step 3); every watch, with its session's id, name and key, agent, model, folder, state, newest line's time; what it is doing now, the call running with its tool, what and since when, none when no call runs; its newest words to the operator; the operator's newest message with its class and marks; the orders in force, or that none is known; the run since the operator's newest message, its calls by tool and the lines its results wrote; its background jobs running, each with its tool, what and since when; its newest recap; the task file it is held to (step 12), or why none; its memory folder with its files (step 13), or why none; and every report of every watch, newest first.</step>
    </module>

    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/" name="the crate">
      <step n="15" name="the parameters">These parameters join the module's, after today's, in this order, each in the group watches, with its fallback, the shape a value must take, the input the tab types it into, and what it decides as written here; each is reported by name when it is set to a value of the wrong shape, as today's are, and attr_recon_correction_words and attr_recon_escalation_words stay as they are in the group measures. The input pairs is new: an object of patterns to lists of words, each a string that is not empty, shown as each pattern, an arrow and its words, the pairs joined by semicolons, in the patterns' order as strings.
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_secs" fallback="5" shape="a whole number, 0 or more" input="number">How often the watcher reads the newest lines, in seconds; 0 pauses it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_sessions" fallback="all" shape="a whole number, 1 or more, or all" input="number or all">Sessions the watcher holds at once, the most recently active kept.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_window_secs" fallback="21600" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">How far back each session is kept, and read back at a start, in seconds.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_quiet_secs" fallback="600" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Seconds an agent may call tools with no word to you, or stall mid-turn, before the watcher reports it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_quiet_calls" fallback="10" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Tool calls it must have made in that time for the watcher to report it quiet.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_behind_secs" fallback="300" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Seconds after a turn ended with a background job running, and nothing said since, before the watcher reports it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_run_secs" fallback="600" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Seconds an agent may run on its own since your last message before the watcher names the run.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_run_calls" fallback="20" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Tool calls a run must have made for the watcher to name it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_stop_calls" fallback="2" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Tool calls after your stop, before a word, at which the watcher reports it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_repeat_secs" fallback="600" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Seconds within which saying the same thing again is a repeated order.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_repeat_share" fallback="75" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Percent of the shorter message's words two messages share to be the same order.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_order_words" fallback="3" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">The fewest words the shorter of two messages has for them to be the same order.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_mood_prompts" fallback="5" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Your newest messages read for frustration: the watcher reports it when more than half escalate.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_failures" fallback="3" shape="a whole number, 1 or more" input="number">Tool calls failing in a row at which the watcher says the agent struggles.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_stop_words" fallback="stop, halt, enough" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words in your message that stop the agent; an interrupt stops it without words.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_status_words" fallback="status, what is going on, what's going on, whats going on, what's up, whats up, eta, where are we" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words or phrases in your message that ask for status.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_question_words" fallback="what, why, how, when, where, who, which, is, are, was, were, does, did, should" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words your message starts with when it is a question; one ending in ? is one too.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_yes_words" fallback="yes, approved, approve, go, ok, okay, proceed, do it, sure" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words or phrases your message starts with when it approves what the agent proposed.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_scope_words" fallback="review, rehearsal, rehearse, rerun, re-run, regenerate, rewrite, from scratch, refactor" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words or phrases in the agent's words that announce work your orders did not say.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_long_words" fallback="hours, an hour, overnight" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Words or phrases in the agent's words that make a long estimate.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_spec_paths" fallback="kn-specs/tasks/, specs/recon/tasks/" shape="a list of non-empty strings" input="list">Where task files live: a session whose message names one is held to it.</param>
        <param name="attr_recon_watch_effects" fallback="CommentToJira → jira, comment; createJiraIssue → jira, ticket, issue; deploy → deploy; editJiraIssue → jira; gh pr create → pr, pull request; gh pr merge → merge; git branch -D → delete, branch; git commit → commit, commits, committed; git merge → merge, merged; git push → push, pushed, pr, pull request, merge; git push --force → force; git rebase → rebase; git reset → reset; rm -rf → delete, remove, drop, clean; transitionJiraIssue → jira" shape="an object of patterns to lists of non-empty strings" input="pairs">Actions beyond the session, each a pattern found in a call's tool or in what it was called on, with the words an order must say for it.</param>
      </step>
      <step n="16" name="the routes">server.rs serves, beside today's routes and behind the same Host check, two routes, each answering from the watcher's snapshot and never reading the kernel's database: GET /api/watch, the snapshot as JSON (step 14); and GET /api/watch/memory?session=&lt;a session's id&gt;&amp;file=&lt;a file's name&gt;, that file of that session's memory folder, read at the request as a file, read-only, and answered whole as text/plain; charset=utf-8. It refuses with HTTP 400 a file that is empty, holds a slash or a backslash, or is . or .., and a session that is not a UUID; with HTTP 404 a session no watch is held for, one with no memory folder, and a file the folder's newest listing does not hold; each refusal is a JSON object whose error says why, as today's refusals are. A route that refuses writes nothing and reads nothing else.</step>
      <step n="17" name="the process">superx-recon run starts the watcher once the page listens, as a task of its own beside the crawler's, with the kernel's connection, the instance and the same stop the crawler is given, so one stop ends both; the page's routes are given the watcher's snapshot. At the stop the process waits for the watcher as it waits for the crawler, and logs a watcher that did not end cleanly as it logs the crawler. Nothing else of run changes.</step>
    </module>

    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/ui/" name="the page">
      <step n="18" name="the Watcher tab">ui/src/App.tsx: the page's tabs are Crawler, Results and Watcher, in that order; #watcher opens the Watcher; on it the header's trail reads SuperX › Recon › Watcher, Recon leading to #crawler as on the other tabs, and the browser's title is Watcher · Recon · SuperX. The subtitle under Recon reads: The crawler, and what it gathers: the models, the agents and the repositories they worked in, measured over time; and the watcher, every session as it works. The Watcher's panel and its parts are in ui/src/watcher/.</step>
      <step n="19" name="the sessions">The Watcher asks /api/watch every two seconds, as the Crawler's Now panel asks /api/now, and shows at its top when the watcher last looked, its interval, paused when it is, the window's start, the lines read and the rows not read by why and agent; then one card per session, those with an alert among their reports first, then those with a warn, then the others, each group by its newest line, newest first. A card shows: its name, agent, model and folder; its state as a badge; what it is doing now; its newest words; your newest message with its class and marks; the orders in force, or that none is known; the run since your newest message; its jobs running; its recap; the task file it executes with its version, the version it held when the session was first held to it, its owns and its forbid, or why none; its memory files (step 20); its reports, newest first. Times are in the operator's own zone, as ui/src/time.ts writes them. A long text is shown clamped and opens whole on a click.</step>
      <step n="20" name="the reports and the memory">Beside the cards, every report, newest first: its time, its level as a badge, its kind in words (stop not honoured for stop_not_honoured, and so on), the session's name, what it says, and its words, clamped, opening whole. A card's memory lists each file with its description and when it last changed; a file opens in a modal that holds it whole, as /api/watch/memory answers it.</step>
      <step n="21" name="notify me">A switch, Notify me, kept in the browser's localStorage, each read and write in a try/catch, off when it cannot be read. When it is turned on the page asks the browser's permission (Notification.requestPermission). While it is on and the permission granted, each report of level warn or alert that an answer of /api/watch holds for the first time since the page opened or the switch was turned on raises one browser notification: its title the kind in words and the session's name, its body what the report says. A report the page already held raises none. When the browser refuses or has no notifications, the switch says so.</step>
      <step n="22" name="the parameters and the build">ui/src/panels/Params.tsx lists the group watches as Watches, after Measures, and types the input pairs: one pair per line, the pattern, an equals sign and its words separated by commas, the first equals sign of a line splitting it; none for no pair; its hint says so. ui/src/api.ts gains the snapshot's types and the two fetches. No dependency joins: package.json and package-lock.json are as merged.
